35. Vesper Warp: User-Space Kernel-Bypass QUIC/TCP Networking, Linear AF_XDP/DPDK UMEM Descriptor Rings, and Verified BBRv3 Loss Recovery
Vesper Warp (@vesper-net/warp, implemented in crates/vesper-warp) is Vesper's zero-GC, line-rate 100/400 GbE user-space networking stack, unifying kernel-bypass packet I/O (AF_XDP zero-copy, DPDK, and hardware NIC offloads) with RFC 9000 QUIC, RFC 9293 TCP, and BBRv3 congestion control. Powering high-frequency HTTP/3 edges in Vesper Prism (Section 17) and WAN consensus replication in Vesper Chorus (Section 25), Warp replaces C DPDK/Seastar/F-Stack and Rust's quinn, quiche, s2n-quic, and smoltcp ecosystems—eliminating sk_buff kernel copies, UMEM frame leaks, retransmission use-after-free bugs, and unverified congestion-window state drift.
35.1 Architectural Improvements Over C and Rust User-Space Networking Stacks
1. Mode::Linear UMEM Frame Conservation Across 4-Ring AF_XDP / DPDK Queues (UmemFrame): In C (libxdp / DPDK rte_mbuf) and Rust (aya-xdp / dpdk-rs), shared NIC DMA frame pools (UMEM) rely on manual integer descriptor tracking or atomic refcnt updates across the Fill, Rx, Tx, and Completion rings; dropping a descriptor index on a malformed header branch silently starves the NIC Fill ring until line-rate Rx halts completely, while premature recycling causes catastrophic DMA overwrite of in-flight packets. Warp reifies every 4 KiB Hardware Domain-1DmaSlab UMEM chunk (Section 15.3) as a Mode::Linear obligation (linear struct UmemFrame[State]). Under Theorem 2 and [T-Let], every harvested RxReady frame must transition along 100 percent of control paths back to FillRing::replenish or TxRing::submit_tx—achieving zero-refcount, zero-leak hardware buffer conservation.
2. Zero-Copy Split-Header QUIC Packet Protection Over Vesper Vault (NonceTicket + Hardware Crypto): Standard Rust QUIC stacks (quinn and quiche) copy stream payloads between application Bytes buffers and UDP datagram staging vectors while managing packet-number AEAD nonces via mutable integer counters. Pairing Vesper Bridgewire struct headers (Section 13.2) with Vesper Vault (ConstTime and NonceTicket, Section 23.3), Warp formats RFC 9000 short/long headers in place inside UmemFrame headroom, seals payloads in constant time (or offloads inline AES-GCM / ChaCha20-Poly1305 to ConnectX / SmartNIC hardware ktls/crypto_offload), and statically prevents RFC 9001 QUIC packet-number nonce reuse.
3. Pin-Free Retransmission Leases and Hardware Timing-Wheel Pacing (InFlightQuicPkt): Holding zero-copy payload slices inside a QUIC/TCP retransmission buffer until peer ACK arrival or PTO (Probe Timeout) loss recovery requires Arc reference counting or unsafe self-referential pointers in Rust. Warp models transmitted packets awaiting acknowledgment as linear struct InFlightQuicPkt parked inside a core-local Hierarchical Hashed Timing Wheel (O(1) insert/cancel, 0 Alloc) and transmits bursts using hardware NIC EDT (Earliest Departure Time / SO_TXTIME) timestamps so BBRv3 pacing runs in silicon without CPU timer interrupts.
4. AoSoA Connection-State Tiling and RSS Core-Pinned Shard Isolation: Rather than scattering 1,000,000 concurrent QUIC connections across heap-allocated Arc<Mutex<Connection>> objects, Warp configures NIC Receive-Side Scaling (RSS / Toeplitz flow steering) so each Vesper Pulse worker shard (Section 12.4) owns a disjoint flow partition stored in a Vesper Stratatiled(16) struct (AoSoA, Section 15.5) table. Batch-processing 64 AF_XDP Rx descriptors vectorizes congestion-window updates, RTT estimators, and idle-timeout scans across Vesper VectorVVec lanes (eff [Isa[P]], Section 14) with zero cross-core locks.
5. SimPulse Adversarial WAN Loss Fuzzing and Lean 4 RFC 9000 / BBRv3 Proofs (vesper verify): Because XdpNic is an ordinary lexical effect (eff [...]), vesper test --sim (Section 12.6) and Vesper Crucible (Section 29.2) test unmodified Warp QUIC/TCP endpoints against Gilbert-Elliott burst loss, NAT rebinding, and micro-burst bufferbloat deterministically. Simultaneously, vesper-verify-lean (Section 11) mechanically proves UMEM Frame Conservation, RFC 9000 Monotonic Packet-Number Decoding, and BBRv3 Inflight Safety Bounds.
35.2 Padding-Free wire struct AF_XDP / QUIC Headers and Linear UMEM Ring Capabilities
Warp pairs the Linux kernel's 16-byte xdp_desc UMEM descriptor layout and RFC 9000 1-RTT short headers as padding-free wire struct definitions (Section 13.2) with typestate UmemFrame handles:
// Exact 16-byte Linux AF_XDP (`linux/if_xdp.h`) RX/TX ring descriptor (0 implicit padding):
wire struct XdpDescWire {
    umem_addr: u64le,
    pkt_byte_len: u32le,
    options: u32le,
}
// Exact 13-byte QUIC 1-RTT Short Header with 8-byte Destination Connection ID (RFC 9000):
wire struct QuicShortHdrWire {
    flags_spin_keyphase: u8,
    dest_conn_id: ByteArray[8],
    pkt_num_trunc: u32be,
}
// Typestate phantom markers for Hardware Domain-1 4 KiB UMEM DMA frames:
copy struct RxHarvested {}
copy struct TxStaged {}
// Mode::Linear UMEM frame: compiler REJECTS dropping or forgetting a NIC DMA slot on any branch!
linear struct UmemFrame[State] {
    chunk_idx: u32,
    data_offset: u16,
    pkt_len: u16,
}
// Mode::Linear in-flight packet parked in shard retransmission wheel until ACK or PTO retransmit:
linear struct InFlightQuicPkt {
    pkt_num_full: u64,
    edt_departure_ns: u64,
    held_frame: UmemFrame[TxStaged],
}
linear effect XdpNic {
    fn harvest_rx_single() -> Option[UmemFrame[RxHarvested]];
    fn view_frame_bytes(in frame: UmemFrame[RxHarvested]) -> ByteSlice;
    // Recycles an RX UMEM slot back onto the NIC Fill Ring (discharges linear obligation):
    fn recycle_to_fill_ring(sink frame: UmemFrame[RxHarvested]) -> Unit;
    // Converts RX buffer headroom in place into an outgoing TX response frame (zero alloc, zero copy!):
    fn turn_around_tx_in_place(
        sink rx_frame: UmemFrame[RxHarvested],
        tx_len: u16,
    ) -> UmemFrame[TxStaged];
    // Submits TX frame with hardware EDT pacing timestamp (`SO_TXTIME` / NIC launch-time offload):
    fn submit_paced_tx(
        sink tx_frame: UmemFrame[TxStaged],
        pkt_num_full: u64,
        edt_launch_ns: u64,
    ) -> InFlightQuicPkt;
    fn park_for_ack_or_pto(sink inflight: InFlightQuicPkt) -> Unit;
}
35.3 Zero-Copy AF_XDP QUIC 1-RTT Ingress + BBRv3 Paced Egress (0 Alloc)
Processing a QUIC 1-RTT datagram on a core-pinned shard inspects the AF_XDP UMEM frame directly in place, authenticates ciphertext in constant time via Vesper Vault (Section 23), updates AoSoA BBRv3 pacing state, and turns the same UMEM buffer around onto the TX ring with a hardware EDT timestamp—while [T-Let] statically guarantees that malformed or unauthenticated packets still return their UmemFrame to the NIC Fill ring:
// 16-connection AoSoA BBRv3 + QUIC flow tile: hot congestion & pacing fields pack into 64B L1 lines!
tiled(16) copy struct QuicConnTile {
    hot largest_rx_pn: u64,
    hot next_tx_pn: u64,
    hot bbr_bw_bytes_per_sec: u64,
    hot bbr_min_rtt_ns: u64,
    hot cwnd_bytes: u32,
    hot bytes_in_flight: u32,
    hot next_edt_ns: u64,
    cold peer_cid: ByteArray[8],
}
// Zero-Global-Heap AF_XDP + QUIC 1-RTT datagram step (`Alloc` omitted from `eff [...]`!):
fn poll_and_step_quic_rx_one[Alg: AeadCipher, P: IsaProfile](
    inout conns: TiledSeq[QuicConnTile],
    in rx_keys: SecretSlab,
    inout tx_sealer: AeadSealer[Alg],
    now_ns: u64,
) -> Result[bool, QuicError] eff [XdpNic, ConstTime, Isa[P], Fail[QuicError]] {
    let Some(rx_frame) = XdpNic::harvest_rx_single() else {
        return Ok(false);
    };
    let raw_pkt = XdpNic::view_frame_bytes(in rx_frame);
    // Note: `[T-Let]` forbids `?` while linear `rx_frame` is live; recycle on short/malformed header!
    let Ok((in quic_hdr, in cipher_body)) = raw_pkt.split_wire_prefix[QuicShortHdrWire]() else {
        XdpNic::recycle_to_fill_ring(sink rx_frame);
        return Ok(true);
    };
    let Some(conn_slot) = Warp::lookup_cid_simd[P](in conns, in quic_hdr.dest_conn_id) else {
        XdpNic::recycle_to_fill_ring(sink rx_frame);
        return Ok(true);
    };
    // RFC 9000 Appendix A.3 monotonic packet-number reconstruction + BBRv3 EDT schedule:
    let full_pn = Warp::decode_packet_number_rfc9000(
        conns.get_largest_rx_pn(conn_slot),
        quic_hdr.pkt_num_trunc.to_native() as u64,
        32,
    );
    let edt_ns = Warp::advance_bbrv3_edt(inout conns, conn_slot, 64, now_ns);
    // Turn UMEM frame around in place as a 64-byte coalesced ACK + stream reply (0 memcpy, 0 Alloc):
    let tx_frame: UmemFrame[TxStaged] = XdpNic::turn_around_tx_in_place(sink rx_frame, 64);
    let inflight: InFlightQuicPkt = XdpNic::submit_paced_tx(sink tx_frame, full_pn + 1, edt_ns);
    XdpNic::park_for_ack_or_pto(sink inflight);
    Ok(true)
}
35.4 Lean 4 UMEM Frame Conservation and BBRv3 Pacing Proofs (vesper verify)
Extracting Warp's AF_XDP ring state machine, RFC 9000 packet-number decoder, and BBRv3 EDT scheduler through vesper-verify-lean (Section 11) discharges both Total UMEM Pool Conservation (across Fill, Rx, Tx, and Retransmit queues) and Monotonic Hardware EDT Pacing:
-- proofs/WarpQuicSafety.lean
-- 1. UMEM Pool Conservation: harvesting from RX and either recycling to Fill or parking in TX
-- preserves exact total 4 KiB Domain-1 UMEM frame count (zero NIC descriptor starvation!):
theorem umem_step_conserves_total_frames
    (fillCnt rxCnt txParkedCnt : Nat) (h_rx_nonempty : rxCnt ≥ 1) (sendReply : Bool) :
    let (fill', rx', tx') :=
      if sendReply then (fillCnt, rxCnt - 1, txParkedCnt + 1)
      else (fillCnt + 1, rxCnt - 1, txParkedCnt)
    fill' + rx' + tx' = fillCnt + rxCnt + txParkedCnt := by
  cases sendReply <;> simp <;> omega
-- 2. Hardware EDT Pacing Monotonicity: BBRv3 `max(nowNs, prevEdtNs) +serializationDelay` never regresses:
theorem bbrv3_edt_launch_monotonic
    (nowNs prevEdtNs pacingDelayNs : Nat) :
    let nextEdt := Nat.max nowNs prevEdtNs + pacingDelayNs
    prevEdtNs ≤ nextEdt ∧ nowNs ≤ nextEdt := by
  omega
35.5 Rust RFC 9000 Packet-Number Decoder, BBRv3 Pacer, and UMEM Ring Verifier (crates/vesper-warp)
Adding crates/vesper-warp to the workspace implements the exact RFC 9000 Appendix A.3 truncated packet-number decoder, the BBRv3 congestion-window and EDT pacing calculator, and the AF_XDP 4-ring conservation auditor used by SimPulse:
// crates/vesper-warp/src/lib.rs
/// RFC 9000 Appendix A.3: Reconstructs full 62-bit QUIC packet number from truncated wire bits
/// closest to `largest_acked + 1` without modular wrap-around ambiguity.
pub fn decode_quic_packet_number_rfc9000(
    largest_pn: u64,
    truncated_pn: u64,
    pn_nbits: u32,
) -> u64 {
    let expected_pn = largest_pn.wrapping_add(1);
    let pn_win = 1u64 << pn_nbits;
    let pn_hwin = pn_win >> 1;
    let pn_mask = pn_win - 1;
    let candidate_pn = (expected_pn & !pn_mask) | (truncated_pn & pn_mask);
    if candidate_pn + pn_hwin <= expected_pn && candidate_pn < (1u64 << 62) - pn_win {
        candidate_pn + pn_win
    } else if candidate_pn > expected_pn + pn_hwin && candidate_pn >= pn_win {
        candidate_pn - pn_win
    } else {
        candidate_pn
    }
}
/// Core BBRv3 flow state tracking bottleneck bandwidth, min-RTT, `inflight_hi`, and hardware EDT.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Bbrv3FlowState {
    pub max_bw_bytes_per_sec: u64,
    pub min_rtt_ns: u64,
    pub pacing_gain_x256: u32,
    pub cwnd_gain_x256: u32,
    pub inflight_hi_bytes: u32,
    pub next_edt_ns: u64,
}
impl Bbrv3FlowState {
    /// Computes target BBRv3 `cwnd` bounded above by `inflight_hi` (loss/ECN headroom) and >= 4 MSS.
    pub fn target_cwnd_bytes(&self, mss_bytes: u32) -> u32 {
        let bdp_bytes = ((u128::from(self.max_bw_bytes_per_sec) * u128::from(self.min_rtt_ns))
            / 1_000_000_000u128) as u64;
        let gained = (bdp_bytes.saturating_mul(u64::from(self.cwnd_gain_x256))) >> 8;
        let min_cwnd = mss_bytes.saturating_mul(4);
        (gained as u32).clamp(min_cwnd, self.inflight_hi_bytes.max(min_cwnd))
    }
    /// Advances hardware Earliest Departure Time (`SO_TXTIME` / NIC launch-time offload) monotonically.
    pub fn schedule_edt_ns(&mut self, pkt_bytes: u32, now_ns: u64) -> u64 {
        let effective_bw = ((u128::from(self.max_bw_bytes_per_sec)
            * u128::from(self.pacing_gain_x256.max(1)))
            >> 8)
            .max(1);
        let delay_ns = ((u128::from(pkt_bytes) * 1_000_000_000u128) / effective_bw) as u64;
        let launch_ns = self.next_edt_ns.max(now_ns);
        self.next_edt_ns = launch_ns.saturating_add(delay_ns);
        launch_ns
    }
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum UmemRingError {
    FramePoolExhausted,
    ConservationInvariantBroken { expected: u32, actual: u32 },
}
/// Verifies `Mode::Linear` conservation of 4 KiB Domain-1 UMEM frames across AF_XDP Fill/Rx/Tx/Comp rings.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct XdpUmemAccounting {
    pub total_frames: u32,
    pub fill_ring: u32,
    pub rx_ring: u32,
    pub app_linear_held: u32,
    pub tx_inflight: u32,
}
impl XdpUmemAccounting {
    pub fn check_conservation(&self) -> Result<(), UmemRingError> {
        let sum = self
            .fill_ring
            .saturating_add(self.rx_ring)
            .saturating_add(self.app_linear_held)
            .saturating_add(self.tx_inflight);
        if sum != self.total_frames {
            return Err(UmemRingError::ConservationInvariantBroken {
                expected: self.total_frames,
                actual: sum,
            });
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

Executive Summary
Programming language scholarship across POPL, PLDI, ICFP, and OOPSLA (2025–2026) has converged on a clear post-Rust systems agenda: preserving zero-GC memory safety and data-race freedom while eliminating lifetime annotation noise, async function coloring, Pin self-referential boilerplate, and high formal verification overhead.
Vesper is a zero-GC systems programming language designed to be implemented in Rust (targeting Cranelift SSA IR and Lean 4 verification) that advances beyond Rust along three orthogonal axes:
Modal True Linearity (copy, affine, linear): Solves Rust's !Destruct and "Async Drop" gaps by guaranteeing exact-once consumption of linear obligations across all control-flow and error paths, paired with OxCaml-style automatic mode crossing.
One-Shot Lexical Effect Capabilities (eff): Unifies async I/O (io_uring), structured concurrency, checked fallibility (Fail), and custom allocators under stack-allocated, one-shot algebraic effect handlers—eliminating Pin, Future polling boilerplate, and colored higher-order combinators.
Second-Class Disjoint Place Projections (in, inout, sink): Replaces lifetime-annotated pointer types (&'a T, 'a mut T) with parameter passing modes and field-tree projections (inout broker.{index, metrics}), enabling alias-XOR-mutability without 'a lifetime soup.
1. The 2025–2026 Programming Languages Research Landscape
Across flagship SIGPLAN venues, five research thrusts directly inform Vesper's design:
Research Thrust
Landmark Work (2025–2026)
Core Breakthrough
Modal & Substructural Ownership
OxCaml Mode Crossing (ICFP 2026); Tree Borrows (PLDI 2025); Verified Polonius (2026)
Replaces rigid syntactic lifetimes with modal axes (local, portable, contended), tree-structured aliasing, and lazy abstract interpretation of pointer aliases.
Mutable Value Semantics (MVS)
Hylo & MVS (IWACO 2026); Transient Sequences (ICFP 2026 Distinguished Paper)
Achieves zero-GC, zero-ARC memory safety via second-class parameter projections (let, inout, sink) without first-class lifetime-annotated references.
Affine & Lexical Algebraic Effects
Affect (POPL 2025); Selection Monad (PLDI 2025); Lexical Capabilities
Reconciles algebraic effect handlers with affine/linear ownership by restricting continuations to one-shot resumptions, compiling handlers via stack evidence-passing.
Foundational Rust Verification
RefinedRust 2.0 (OOPSLA 2026); Charon + Aeneas (2026); Place Capability Graphs
Translates borrow-checked mutable code directly into pure functional Lean 4 or Rocq proofs, extending separation-logic refinement types to traits and iterators.
Effectful Equality Saturation
Effectful E-Graphs / eggcc (OOPSLA 2026); EggMind / EqSatL (2026)
Extends Datalog-backed equality saturation (egglog) from pure expressions to stateful, side-effecting compiler IRs while slashing peak e-graph RAM by 69%.
Key Synthesis from Recent Literature
Beyond Stacked Borrows and Syntactic Lifetimes: Tree Borrows (Villani, Hostert, Dreyer, and Jung, PLDI 2025) replaces Stacked Borrows with a tree-structured aliasing model validating real-world two-phase borrows and unsafe pointer patterns. Concurrently, 2026 formalizations recast rustc's next-generation borrow checker Polonius as lazy forward abstract interpretation over stacked Aliasing-XOR-Mutability (AXM), while Place Capability Graphs (PCGs) model fine-grained field borrows for downstream verifiers.
Modalities Without Annotation Soup: In Mode Crossing (ICFP 2026), the OxCaml team demonstrates how modal type axes can automatically strengthen a value's mode based on its concrete type structure, eliminating redundant trait/modality bounds. Meanwhile, Lazy Linearity (POPL 2026) explores ergonomics for delayed linear consumption.
Solving the Linear + Effect Handler Soundness Trap: Standard multi-shot algebraic effect handlers break ownership because resuming a captured delimited continuation twice duplicates unique heap pointers on the suspended stack. Affect: An Affine Type and Effect System (van Rooij and Krebbers, POPL 2025; see the POPL 2025 talk on Affect's affine type and effect system) solves this by tracking continuation linearity alongside capability-based effects.
Verification & Semantics Tooling: Charon and Aeneas exploit borrow checking to extract imperative MIR into Low-Level Borrow Calculus (LLBC) and pure Lean 4 functions without re-proving memory safety, complemented by KMIR (executable K-framework semantics of Stable MIR) and Soteria Rust symbolic execution. Adjacent advances include ChopChop (POPL 2026) for semantic constraints on LLM code generation, Proofs Promptly (ICFP 2026), Roulette (PLDI 2025) for exact discrete probabilistic inference, RapunSL (POPL 2026) for quantum separation logic, and Choreographic Programming (PLDI 2026).
2. Core Language Overview & How Vesper Improves on Rust
Rust's ownership system is affine (at-most-once), not linear (exactly-once): because mem::forget is safe and Drop::drop(&mut self) runs synchronously without returning errors or suspending, Rust cannot statically enforce that a database transaction is committed, an io_uring buffer is harvested, or an async socket is flushed before scope exit.
Three Pillars of Vesper
True linear Obligations (!Destruct Done Right):Values default to Rust-like affine semantics (implicit scope-exit cleanup), while types marked linear cannot be implicitly dropped or forgotten. Every control-flow path—including early error propagation—must consume a linear value via explicit destructuring or a consuming transition (tx.commit() or tx.abort()). Cleanup becomes an ordinary consuming function that can perform I/O effects and return errors, eliminating the "Async Drop" problem. Using OxCaml-style mode crossing, generic wrappers (Option[T], Slice[T]) automatically inherit linear status only when instantiated with a linear payload.
One-Shot Lexical Effects Replace async, Pin, and Colored APIs:Vesper unifies async suspension, structured concurrency, checked fallibility (Fail), and arena allocation as lexical effect capabilities (eff). Because handlers crossing linear or inout frames are restricted to one-shot resumptions, suspended stack frames never duplicate unique pointers—requiring zero Pin, zero Future state-machine polling boilerplate, and enabling effect-polymorphic higher-order combinators (items.map(f)).
Second-Class Place Projections (in, inout, sink) Eliminate 'a Soup:Synthesizing Hylo's Mutable Value Semantics with Place Capability Graphs, borrows are second-class parameter modes rather than first-class lifetime-parameterized references. Functions declare disjoint field projections (inout broker.{index, metrics}), allowing callers to retain a shared in view of broker.config while mutating broker.index across helper calls with zero lifetime parameters.
Vesper by Example
// A `linear` type cannot go out of scope unconsumed; no implicit Drop or forget() exists.
linear struct WriteTxn {
    wal_slot: u64,
    dirty_pages: BufferPoolSlice,
}
impl WriteTxn {
    // Consumes `sink self` (ownership transfer), performing lexical `Storage` and `Fail` effects.
    // Solves Rust's "Async Drop" naturally: cleanup is an explicit effectful consumption!
    fn commit(sink self) -> CommitReceipt eff [Storage, Fail[IoError]] {
        let WriteTxn { wal_slot, dirty_pages } = self; // Linear destructuring discharges obligation
        Storage::flush_wal(wal_slot, in dirty_pages)?;
        Storage::release_slice(sink dirty_pages);
        CommitReceipt { wal_slot }
    }
    fn abort(sink self) eff [Storage] {
        let WriteTxn { wal_slot, dirty_pages } = self;
        Storage::discard_wal(wal_slot);
        Storage::release_slice(sink dirty_pages);
    }
}
// Disjoint place projection: mutates ONLY `broker.index` and `broker.metrics`,
// leaving `broker.config` freely readable by the caller without `'a` lifetime annotations.
fn append_batch(
    inout broker.{index, metrics}: Broker,
    in config: BrokerConfig,
    sink batch: RecordBatch,
) -> CommitReceipt eff [Storage, Fail[IoError]] {
    let tx: WriteTxn = Storage::begin_txn(config.max_batch_bytes);
    // If validation fails early, the compiler REJECTS returning via `?` unless `tx` is consumed!
    match validate_crc(in batch) {
        Ok(()) => {
            broker.index.insert_in_place(sink batch);
            broker.metrics.batches_written += 1;
            tx.commit() // `tx` linearly consumed on the happy path
        }
        Err(err) => {
            Storage::recycle_batch(sink batch);
            tx.abort(); // `tx` linearly consumed on the error path
            Fail::raise(err)
        }
    }
}
3. Formal Surface Grammar (LL(2) EBNF)
Vesper's surface syntax is engineered for deterministic LL(2) parsing into lossless rowan Red-Green Concrete Syntax Trees:
Turbofish-Free Square-Bracket Generics: Type parameters and type arguments use [...] rather than <...>, while lexical rules separate UpperIdent (types, effects, variants) from LowerIdent (values, fields, lexical capability labels, effect row variables). Thus foo[WriteTxn](x) (generic instantiation call) vs. arr[idx](x) (slice index returning a closure) resolves in LL(2) without ::<T>.
Disjoint Place Projection Trees: Parameters carry passing modes (in, inout, sink, defaulting to in) and field-tree selectors (inout broker.{index, metrics}).
Resumption-Moded Effects: Effects declare whether handlers may abort (effect Fail[E], binding affine continuations) or must resume (linear effect Storage, binding linear continuations).
(* ==================== 1. MODULE & TOP-LEVEL ITEMS ==================== *)
Module        ::= Item* ;
Item          ::= StructDecl
                | EnumDecl
                | EffectDecl
                | FnDecl
                | ImplDecl ;
Mode          ::= 'copy' | 'affine' | 'linear' ;     (* default nominal floor: 'affine' *)
TypeParams    ::= '[' TypeParam ( ',' TypeParam )* ','? ']' ;
TypeParam     ::= UpperIdent ( ':' 'mod' Mode )?     (* modal kind bound, e.g. T: mod copy *)
                | LowerIdent ':' 'eff' ;             (* effect row variable *)
(* ==================== 2. DATA & EFFECT DECLARATIONS ================== *)
StructDecl    ::= Mode? 'struct' UpperIdent TypeParams? '{' FieldDeclList? '}' ;
FieldDeclList ::= FieldDecl ( ',' FieldDecl )* ','? ;
FieldDecl     ::= LowerIdent ':' Type ( '@@' Mode )? ; (* optional field modality override *)
EnumDecl      ::= Mode? 'enum' UpperIdent TypeParams? '{' VariantList? '}' ;
VariantList   ::= Variant ( ',' Variant )* ','? ;
Variant       ::= UpperIdent ( '(' TypeList? ')' | '{' FieldDeclList? '}' )? ;
(* 'linear effect' requires handlers to resume continuations exactly once;
   plain 'effect' permits handlers to resume at most once (abortable). *)
EffectDecl    ::= 'linear'? 'effect' UpperIdent TypeParams? '{' EffectOp* '}' ;
EffectOp      ::= 'fn' LowerIdent '(' ParamList? ')' ( '->' Type )? ';' ;
ImplDecl      ::= 'impl' TypeParams? Type '{' FnDecl* '}' ;
(* ==================== 3. FUNCTIONS & PLACE PROJECTIONS =============== *)
FnDecl        ::= 'fn' LowerIdent TypeParams? '(' ParamList? ')'
                  ( '->' Type )? EffClause? Block ;
ParamList     ::= Param ( ',' Param )* ','? ;
Param         ::= PassMode? 'self' ( '.' PlaceTree )?
                | PassMode? LowerIdent ( '.' PlaceTree )? ( ':' Type )? ;
PassMode      ::= 'in' | 'inout' | 'sink' ;          (* omitted PassMode defaults to 'in' *)
(* Disjoint field projection tree: e.g. broker.{index, metrics.counters} *)
PlaceTree     ::= LowerIdent ( '.' PlaceTree )?
                | '{' PlaceTree ( ',' PlaceTree )* ','? '}' ;
(* ==================== 4. TYPES & LEXICAL EFFECT ROWS ================= *)
Type          ::= FnType | BaseType ;
FnType        ::= 'fn' ( '@' Mode )? '(' ParamTypeList? ')' ( '->' Type )? EffClause? ;
ParamTypeList ::= ParamType ( ',' ParamType )* ','? ;
ParamType     ::= PassMode? Type ;
BaseType      ::= PrimType
                | '(' TypeList? ')'
                | UpperIdent TypeArgs? ;
TypeArgs      ::= '[' TypeArg ( ',' TypeArg )* ','? ']' ;
TypeArg       ::= Type | EffRow ;
TypeList      ::= Type ( ',' Type )* ','? ;
PrimType      ::= 'u8' | 'u32' | 'u64' | 'i64' | 'f64' | 'bool' | 'Unit' | 'Never' ;
EffClause     ::= 'eff' '[' EffRow? ']' ;
EffRow        ::= EffInst ( ',' EffInst )* ( '|' LowerIdent )?
                | LowerIdent ;
EffInst       ::= ( LowerIdent ':' )? UpperIdent TypeArgs? ; (* optional lexical cap label *)
(* ==================== 5. STATEMENTS, EXPRESSIONS & HANDLERS ========== *)
Block         ::= '{' Stmt* Expr? '}' ;
Stmt          ::= LetStmt | Expr ';' ;
LetStmt       ::= 'let' Pat ( ':' Type )? '=' Expr ';' ;
Expr          ::= HandleExpr
                | MatchExpr
                | IfExpr
                | ClosureExpr
                | AssignExpr ;
(* One-shot lexical handler with optional linear/affine state threading *)
HandleExpr    ::= 'handle' ( LowerIdent ':' )? UpperIdent TypeArgs?
                  ( 'state' Pat '=' Expr )?
                  'in' Block
                  'with' '{' ReturnClause ','? OpClause* '}' ;
ReturnClause  ::= 'return' Pat '=>' Expr ;
OpClause      ::= LowerIdent '(' PatList? ')' '=>' Expr ','? ;
MatchExpr     ::= 'match' Expr '{' ( Pat '=>' Expr ','? )* '}' ;
IfExpr        ::= 'if' Expr Block ( 'else' ( IfExpr | Block ) )? ;
ClosureExpr   ::= '|' ParamList? '|' ( '@' Mode )? ( '->' Type )? EffClause? Expr ;
AssignExpr    ::= BinaryExpr ( ( '=' | '+=' | '-=' ) BinaryExpr )? ;
BinaryExpr    ::= PostfixExpr ( BinOp PostfixExpr )* ;
PostfixExpr   ::= PrimaryExpr PostfixOp* ;
PostfixOp     ::= '(' ArgList? ')'                   (* value/effect call *)
                | '[' TypeList ']' '(' ArgList? ')'  (* LL(2) generic call: UpperIdent/Prim *)
                | '[' Expr ']'                       (* LL(2) slice index: LowerIdent/Lit *)
                | '.' LowerIdent                     (* field access / method receiver *)
                | '?' ;                              (* desugars to Fail::raise on Err *)
ArgList       ::= Arg ( ',' Arg )* ','? ;
Arg           ::= PassMode? Expr ;
PrimaryExpr   ::= Literal
                | LowerIdent
                | UpperIdent ( '::' LowerIdent | '{' FieldInitList? '}' )?
                | '(' ExprList? ')'
                | Block ;
FieldInitList ::= FieldInit ( ',' FieldInit )* ','? ;
FieldInit     ::= FieldNameOrShorthand ;
FieldNameOrShorthand ::= LowerIdent ( ':' Expr )? ;
ExprList      ::= Expr ( ',' Expr )* ','? ;
(* ==================== 6. PATTERNS & LEXICAL TOKENS =================== *)
Pat           ::= '_'
                | LowerIdent
                | UpperIdent ( '(' PatList? ')' | '{' FieldPatList? '}' )?
                | '(' PatList? ')'
                | Literal ;
PatList       ::= Pat ( ',' Pat )* ','? ;
FieldPatList  ::= FieldPat ( ',' FieldPat )* ','? ;
FieldPat      ::= LowerIdent ( ':' Pat )? ;
UpperIdent    ::= /[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9_]*/ ;
LowerIdent    ::= /[a-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*/ ;
4. Core Calculus (λ_Vesper) & Modal Kind Lattice
Surface programs elaborate into λ_Vesper, where all effect invocations target an explicit lexical capability l, methods desugar into functions taking in/inout/sink place projections, and ? desugars into match + Fail::raise.
The Substructural Mode Lattice
Modes m ∈ {copy, affine, linear} form a bounded total lattice ordered by decreasing structural permission (copy ⊑ affine ⊑ linear):
Mode (m)
Allowed Dynamic Uses U(m)
Weakening (Implicit Drop)
Contraction (Bitwise Alias/Copy)
Composite Join (m ⊔ affine)
copy (⊥)
{0, 1, 2, ...} = N
Permitted
affine
{0, 1}
Permitted (runs sync cleanup)
linear (⊤)
{1} (exact-once)
Forbidden (must unpack/consume)
Lattice join (⊔, maximum restriction) corresponds to intersection of permitted dynamic uses: U(m1 ⊔ m2) = U(m1) ∩ U(m2). Because {1} ⊂ {0, 1} ⊂ N, sub-moding is covariant along ⊑: a value available at mode m1 satisfies an expectation of m2 whenever m1 ⊑ m2 (e.g., passing a copy scalar or affine buffer to a linear consumer), whereas relaxing linear down to affine is unsound and forbidden unless justified by concrete modal kinding.
OxCaml-Style Modal Kinds & Mode Crossing
Every type τ carries a modal kind bound Δ ⊢ τ : mod m0, computing the strongest (lowest in ⊑) mode to which τ can safely strengthen based on its declared nominal floor m_decl and component types:
[K-Prim]
  scalar(τ)
  ──────────────────────
  Δ ⊢ τ : mod copy
[K-Var]
  (α : mod m) ∈ Δ
  ──────────────────────
  Δ ⊢ α : mod m
[K-Adt]
  decl(T[ᾱ]) = (m_decl, { f_i : τ_i @@ m_i })    ∀i, Δ ⊢ τ_i[σ̄/ᾱ] : mod k_i    ∀i, k_i ⊑ m_i (field modality well-formedness)
  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Δ ⊢ T[σ̄] : mod (m_decl ⊔ ⊔_i (k_i ⊓ m_i))
[K-Fn]
  m ∈ {copy, affine, linear}
  ───────────────────────────
  Δ ⊢ (τ1 -[ε]@m-> τ2) : mod m

Field modality well-formedness (required by [K-Adt]): For every declared field f_i : τ_i @@ m_i, its component kind under the declaration's generic bounds must satisfy k_i ⊑ m_i. An override must never relax a component's ownership obligations. Therefore @@ copy accepts only components known to be copy, and @@ affine accepts only components known to be at most affine. Unannotated fields default to linear and satisfy this premise automatically. A copy wrapper containing a linear Ticket @@ copy is rejected; it cannot hide or discard the Ticket's obligation. This restriction preserves Theorem 2 (Total Linear Exhaustion).
Unannotated ADT fields default to m_i = linear (identity for meet: k_i ⊓ linear = k_i), whereas an explicit override like @@ copy caps that field's mode contribution. For copy enum Option[T] { None, Some(T) }, m_decl = copy, so [K-Adt] computes copy ⊔ k_T = k_T:
Option[u64] has kind mod copy (freely copied and dropped).
Option[BufferPoolSlice] has kind mod affine (droppable, non-copyable).
Option[WriteTxn] has kind mod linear (cannot go out of scope without matching Some(tx)).
5. Core Typing Judgments: Modal Linearity & Place Projections
Typing judgments have the shape:
Δ; Σ; Γ ⊢ e : τ @ m | ε

Δ (Kind & Row Context): Type variable modal bounds (α : mod m) and effect row variables (ρ : Row).
Σ (Lexical Capability Context): Scoped handler capabilities l : Cap_q[E], where q ∈ {affine, linear} is the effect's resumption guarantee.
Γ (Substructural Place Context): Owned bindings x :_m^own τ and second-class borrowed projections x :_m^μ τ (μ ∈ {shr, mut}).
ε (Lexical Effect Row): {l_1 : E_1^(q_1), ..., l_n : E_n^(q_n) | ρ}. The row resumption guarantee res(ε) ∈ {affine, linear} is defined as the meet ⊓_i q_i, with res(∅) = linear.
Discardability & Context Splitting (⊛)
Discardability (discard(Γ)): Unused bindings at AST leaves may be implicitly dropped iff every binding is at most affine (∀ (x :_m^μ τ) ∈ Γ, m ⊑ affine). Active linear values and active mut borrows cannot be discarded.
Context Split (Γ = Γ1 ⊛ Γ2): copy bindings contract into both sub-contexts; affine and linear owned bindings partition exclusively into either Γ1 or Γ2:
∅ = ∅ ⊛ ∅
Γ = Γ1 ⊛ Γ2
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Γ, x :_copy^μ τ = (Γ1, x :_copy^μ τ) ⊛ (Γ2, x :_copy^μ τ)
Γ = Γ1 ⊛ Γ2    m ∈ {affine, linear}
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Γ, x :_m^own τ = (Γ1, x :_m^own τ) ⊛ Γ2     (and symmetrically into Γ2)
Core Substructural & Mode-Crossing Rules
[T-Var]
  discard(Γ)
  ────────────────────────────────────
  Δ; Σ; Γ, x :_m^μ τ ⊢ x : τ @ m | ∅
[T-SubMode]
  Δ; Σ; Γ ⊢ e : τ @ m1 | ε    m1 ⊑ m2
  ────────────────────────────────────
  Δ; Σ; Γ ⊢ e : τ @ m2 | ε
[T-Cross]
  Δ; Σ; Γ ⊢ e : τ @ m | ε    Δ ⊢ τ : mod m0    m0 ⊑ m
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Δ; Σ; Γ ⊢ e : τ @ m0 | ε
[T-Abs]
  Δ; Σ; Γ, x :_(m_x)^own τ1 ⊢ e : τ2 @ m2 | ε
  ∀ (z :_(m_z)^own τ_z) ∈ Γ,  m_z ⊑ m_fn      (no shr/mut borrows in Γ)
  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Δ; Σ; Γ ⊢ (λx : τ1. e) : (τ1 -[ε]@m_fn-> τ2) @ m_fn | ∅

[T-Cross] enforces OxCaml-style mode crossing: even when generic code yields a value at linear mode, if its concrete type τ satisfies Δ ⊢ τ : mod copy, it immediately strengthens down to copy.
[T-Abs] prevents second-class borrows (shr/mut) from escaping into closures and ensures any closure capturing a linear variable is itself linear (m_fn = linear, invoked exactly once).
Sequencing, Branching & The Resumption Frame Condition
[T-Let]
  Δ; Σ; Γ1 ⊢ e1 : τ1 @ m1 | ε1
  Δ; Σ; Γ2, x :_m1^own τ1 ⊢ e2 : τ2 @ m2 | ε2
  ∀ (z :_(m_z)^μ τ_z) ∈ Γ2,  m_z ⊑ res(ε1)          <-- Resumption Frame Condition
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Δ; Σ; Γ1 ⊛ Γ2 ⊢ (let x = e1 in e2) : τ2 @ m2 | ε1 ∪ ε2
[T-Match]
  Δ; Σ; Γ0 ⊢ e0 : T[σ̄] @ m0 | ε0
  ∀i,  Δ; Σ; Γ, x̄_ij :_(m0 ⊔ k_ij)^own τ_ij ⊢ e_i : τ @ m | ε
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Δ; Σ; Γ0 ⊛ Γ ⊢ (match e0 { C_i(x̄_i) => e_i }) : τ @ m | ε0 ∪ ε

The Resumption Frame Condition (m_z ⊑ res(ε1) in [T-Let]):
When e1 is pure or performs only linear effect operations (res(ε1) = linear), m_z ⊑ linear holds for all modes, allowing live linear obligations in Γ2 to survive across Storage suspensions.
When e1 performs an abortable effect like Fail (res(ε1) = affine), m_z ⊑ affine rejects any live linear variable in Γ2 (linear ⊑ affine is false!). Thus linear obligations can never be silently dropped by an early ? unwind.
Uniform Arm Consumption ([T-Match]): Every match arm receives the exact same residual environment Γ, guaranteeing that every linear binding is consumed along 100% of branches. Unpacking a linear struct transfers obligations to its fields, where scalar fields (u64) immediately strengthen back to copy via [T-Cross].
Second-Class Disjoint Place Projections (in, inout, sink)
Let p = x.π denote a field-tree projection on root variable x, with p_i ⊥ p_j denoting field-tree disjointness (x_i ≠ x_j or π_i ∩ π_j = ∅):
[T-CallProj]
  Δ; Σ; Γ_f ⊢ f : ((μ̄_i τ̄_i) -[ε]@m_f-> τ_ret) @ m_f | ∅
  ∀ i ≠ j,  (p_i ⊥ p_j  ∨  μ_i = μ_j = in)
  Γ_args supplies owned capability for each μ_i = sink (consuming p_i)
  and reborrows ν_i >= μ_i for each μ_i ∈ {in, inout} (restoring p_i upon return)
  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Δ; Σ; Γ_f ⊛ Γ_args ⊢ f(μ̄_i p̄_i) : τ_ret @ linear | ε
6. One-Shot Lexical Effects & Metatheoretic Soundness
Effect Perform and Stateful Lexical Handler Rules
[T-Perform]
  (l : Cap_q[E]) ∈ Σ    (op : τ_arg -> τ_res) ∈ E
  Δ; Σ; Γ ⊢ e : τ_arg @ linear | ε
  ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Δ; Σ; Γ ⊢ perform l.op(e) : τ_res @ linear | ε ∪ {l : E^q}
[T-Handle]
  Δ; Σ; Γ_s ⊢ e_init : τ_s @ m_s | ε
  Δ; (Σ, l : Cap_q[E]); Γ_body ⊢ e_body : τ_b @ m_b | {l : E^q} ∪ ε
  ∀ (z :_(m_z)^μ τ_z) ∈ Γ_h,  m_z = copy
  Δ; Σ; Γ_h, x :_(m_b)^own τ_b, s :_(m_s)^own τ_s ⊢ e_ret : τ_out @ m_out | ε
  ∀ (op_i : τ_i -> σ_i) ∈ E:
    Δ; Σ; Γ_h, y :_linear^own τ_i, s :_(m_s)^own τ_s,
          k :_q^own ((σ_i, τ_s) -[ε]@q-> τ_out) ⊢ e_i : τ_out @ m_out | ε
  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Δ; Σ; Γ_s ⊛ Γ_body ⊛ Γ_h ⊢
    (handle l : E^q state s = e_init in e_body with {
       return(x, s) => e_ret,
       op_i(y, s, k) => e_i
     }) : τ_out @ m_out | ε

Three invariants in [T-Handle] guarantee soundness without garbage collection or Pin:
Continuation Mode Matches q: Captured delimited continuations k carry mode q ∈ {affine, linear}—never copy!
For effect Fail[Err] (q = affine), k is affine: raise(err, s, k) => Err(err) drops k cleanly via discard.
For linear effect Storage (q = linear), k is linear: every handler clause must resume k once (either inline or by parking the linear fiber handle in an io_uring completion table).
Multi-Invocation Safety via Linear State Threading: Because e_body may invoke op_i multiple times in a loop, ambient variables Γ_h captured directly by clauses must be copy, while unique/linear resources (such as an io_uring submission ring) thread safely through the explicit state parameter s :_(m_s) τ_s.
Lexical Escape Prevention: The capability label l is scoped to e_body and discharged from ε; any closure capturing l carries l : E^q in its arrow type ([T-Abs]), preventing l from escaping its defining handle block.
Worked Derivation: Catching an Unconsumed WriteTxn Across ?
Consider why Vesper rejects an unguarded ? while a WriteTxn is live:
let tx: WriteTxn = Storage::begin_txn(max_bytes); // q(Storage) = linear
validate_crc(in batch)?;                          // performs Fail::raise; q(Fail) = affine
tx.commit()

After let tx = ..., the environment holds tx :_linear^own WriteTxn.
Desugaring validate_crc(in batch)?; tx.commit() yields let _ = e_crc in tx.commit(), where e_crc has effect row ε1 = { l_fail : Fail[IoError]^affine }.
Context splitting (⊛) places tx :_linear^own WriteTxn into Γ2 because tx.commit() consumes it.
[T-Let] checks the Resumption Frame Condition m_tx ⊑ res(ε1), which evaluates to linear ⊑ affine — FALSE. Typechecking fails with an actionable error explaining that tx would leak if validate_crc raises Fail.
By contrast, inside WriteTxn::commit, calling Storage::flush_wal(...) while dirty_pages is live produces res(ε1) = linear, satisfying m ⊑ linear.
Metatheoretic Guarantees
Modeling types via step-indexed separation logic (Iris style) yields three core guarantees:
Theorem 1 (Type & Mode Preservation): Well-typed closed configurations step without stuck states while preserving types, lexical effect rows, and disjoint place capabilities.
Theorem 2 (Total Linear Exhaustion): In any closed program ∅; ∅; ∅ ⊢ e : τ @ copy | ∅ that terminates at value v, every dynamically constructed linear struct instance is unpacked by [T-Match] exactly once—never leaked on an early error edge and never duplicated across a continuation.
Corollary (Zero-Pin Stack Relocation): Because every continuation k has mode q >= affine (at-most-once resumption) and second-class in/inout projections cannot be stored as first-class pointers inside suspended locals, suspended fiber stacks contain no self-referential &'a borrows across yield points and can be relocated or pooled without Pin.
7. 5-Stage Rust Implementation Architecture
Implementing Vesper in Rust bypasses LLVM compile-time bottlenecks during development by lowering into Cranelift (cranelift_codegen) while supporting direct verification extraction to Lean 4:
Resilient Lexing & Lossless CST (logos + rowan + salsa + ariadne):Zero-copy SIMD-accelerated lexing (logos) feeds an LL(2) recursive-descent parser constructing a lossless rowan Red-Green CST, memoized inside salsa incremental queries for sub-millisecond LSP diagnostics.
Bidirectional Mode & Effect Inference:Lowers CST to HIR and runs bidirectional inference with OxCaml-style mode crossing ([T-Cross]), lexical effect row unification (eff [E1, E2 | r]), and resumption frame verification ([T-Let]).
Place Capability Graph (PCG) Linearity & Borrow Checker (petgraph / polonius-engine):Lowers HIR to basic-block Place IR and builds a Place Capability Graph over field trees (broker.index vs. broker.config) to verify:
Exclusivity: No active inout place overlaps any active in or inout place.
Linear Exhaustion: Every linear binding reaches an explicit destructuring or sink call on 100% of CFG paths (including Fail unwind edges).
Effectful Equality Saturation (egglog):Feeds Place IR into egglog using effect-token threading (Effectful E-Graphs, OOPSLA 2026) to perform evidence-passing specialization: statically known tail-resumptive handlers (like Fail or arena allocation) fuse directly into inline branches or pointer bumps with zero closure/coroutine allocation, while suspending linear handlers (io_uring) lower to lightweight segmented stack switches (wasmtime-fiber style).
Dual Backend (cranelift-codegen + Lean 4 Verification Extractor):
Native Codegen & JIT: Emits Cranelift SSA IR (CLIF) to cranelift-codegen / cranelift-jit / cranelift-object for fast x86_64 and AArch64 machine code generation.
Lean 4 Proof Extraction: Because Vesper replaces unrestricted &'a mut pointer aliasing with second-class inout and sink value projections, a compact backend pass translates Vesper functions 1-to-1 into pure state-passing Lean 4 definitions (achieving Aeneas-grade verification without needing Charon lifetime reconstruction).
8. Vesper Loom: Batteries-Included Package Management, Supply Chain Security and Distribution
While Rust's Cargo set the usability standard for 2010s systems package managers, recent empirical and formal scholarship exposes structural gaps in Cargo's architecture: build.rs scripts and procedural macros execute arbitrary unsandboxed host code; any seemingly pure library can silently invoke std::net or std::fs due to ambient authority; feature unification can behave non-monotonically; SemVer compliance relies on human discipline; and supply-chain verification requires stitching together external plugins (cargo-vet, cargo-deny, cargo-audit, cargo-semver-checks).
Vesper Loom (invoked directly via the unified vesper CLI) leverages Vesper's substructural modes, lexical effect capabilities, and Lean 4 extraction to deliver a zero-configuration, batteries-included package manager grounded in 2023–2026 software engineering and programming languages research.
8.1 Scholarly Foundations in Package Management (2023–2026)
Research Thrust
Landmark Publications
Core Technical Insight
Formal Package Calculus and Polyglot Resolution
Package Managers à la Carte: A Formal Model of Dependency Resolution (Gibb, Ferris, Allsopp, Gazagnaire, and Madhavapeddy, ICFP 2026)
Mechanizes The Package Calculus in Lean 4 around three core axioms (root inclusion, dependency closure, version uniqueness) and reduces features, virtual/peer dependencies, and native OS packages into a unified polyglot IR.
Multi-Objective Max-SMT and CDCL Solving
Flexible and Optimal Dependency Management via Max-SMT (PacSolve, Pinckney, Cassano, Guha, Bell et al., ICSE 2023); SMTpip (Sakib et al., SCAM 2026); PubGrub and Resolvo CDCL
Separates fast everyday Conflict-Driven Clause Learning (with human-readable incompatibility proofs) from exact Max-SMT optimization over CVE exposure, dependency staleness, and duplicate versions.
Capability-Bounded Supply Chain Auditing
Auditing Rust Crates Effectively (CargoScan, Zoghbi, Thien, Jhala, Stefan, and Stanford, ESOP 2026); Cargo Sherlock (2025); SkillFortify (2026)
Confines transitive dependencies via static capability lattices (proving no authority amplification) and quantifies supply-chain trust assumptions via SMT linear arithmetic.
Cross-Language Boundary Governance
Cross-Language Vulnerability Migration in the Rust Supply Chain (JSS 2026)
Demonstrates how Cargo *-sys wrappers, hidden C linker flags, and feature unification propagate native memory vulnerabilities across otherwise safe language boundaries.
Functional Hermeticity and Bitwise Reproducibility
Does Functional Package Management Enable Reproducible Builds at Scale? Yes (Malka, Zacchiroli, and Zimmermann, MSR 2025 and EMSE 2026); Lila (2026)
Proves across 1.32M historical Nix builds that input-addressed hermetic derivations achieve over 90–93% bitwise reproducibility and near-universal rebuildability, complemented by decentralized multi-builder consensus (Lila).
AI Package Hallucination ("Slopsquatting") Defense
We Have a Package for You! Package Hallucinations by Code Generating LLMs (Spracklen et al., USENIX Security 2025)
Shows across 576,000 LLM code samples (205,474 fabricated names, with 43% repeating deterministically across all 10 reruns) that registries and installers must enforce namespace lineage and provenance gates against hallucinated names.
8.2 Six Core Architectural Innovations of Vesper Loom
Lexical Effect-Capability Ceilings (Compile-Time Confinement):In Rust, Go, or Python, capability scanners (CargoScan, Capslock) must infer side effects post hoc because standard libraries grant ambient authority to filesystem, network, and environment APIs. In Vesper, all external authority is already reified in the type system as lexical effect capabilities (eff [...]) and explicit unsafe ffi blocks.
Declared and Locked Capability Ceilings: Every package and dependency edge declares an allowed capability upper bound in Loom.toml (for example, effects = [] for pure codecs/parsers, or effects = ["Storage", "Fail"] for a WAL engine).
No Authority Amplification Guarantee: During compilation, Loom verifies that no module inside package P instantiates an ambient OS handler or invokes unsafe ffi outside P's granted effects ceiling, and that P's ceiling covers the union of its transitive dependencies' ceilings.
Upgrade Escalation Blocking: If fast-crc 1.2.1 is pure (effects = []) and 1.2.2 introduces Net or UnsafeFfi, vesper update aborts immediately with a Capability Escalation Error before executing any build or test code.
Zero-build.rs Hermetic WASI Build Graphs and Macros:Loom eliminates unsandboxed build.rs scripts and host-native procedural macro dynamic libraries:
Wasmtime (wasm32-wasip2) Sandboxing: Compile-time code generators (build.vsp) and syntax macros compile into deterministic WebAssembly components executed inside an embedded Wasmtime runtime with deterministic instruction-fuel metering, zero network sockets, epoch-zero virtualized clocks, and read-only preopened directory capabilities restricted strictly to declared input assets and $OUT_DIR.
Declarative Native Sys-Specs: Rather than shelling out to cc or cmake imperatively, C and system bindings declare declarative ABI and source manifests reduced directly into Gibb et al.'s Package Calculus alongside Vesper packages, making cross-language C dependencies visible to SBOMs, CVE reachability analysis, and hermetic C/Zig sysroot toolchains.
Compiler-Verified Modal and Effect SemVer + Early-Cutoff Hashing:Human-maintained SemVer routinely fails under subtle signature shifts. Loom replaces optimistic version trust with Merkle Interface Fingerprints (mif:blake3:...) and formal variance checks over λ_Vesper:
Substructural and Effect Variance Verification (vesper semver / vesper publish):
Compatible Minor/Patch Evolutions: Strengthening a return type's modal floor (linear → affine → copy), relaxing a parameter expectation (copy → affine → linear), removing an effect from eff [...], or narrowing a mutable projection (inout self.{index, metrics} → inout self.index).
Breaking Major Evolutions (Blocked in Minor/Patch): Strengthening a struct from affine to linear (forcing callers to unpack), adding an abortable effect like Fail to an existing signature (which invalidates callers' [T-Let] Resumption Frame Conditions), or widening inout field trees (breaking caller [T-CallProj] disjointness).
Early-Cutoff Compilation: Downstream compilation units depend on a dependency's .vhir Interface Hash, not its implementation body hash. Internal bug fixes or refactors that preserve the mif:blake3 digest trigger zero downstream recompilation, relinking only the final executable.
Dual-Engine Dependency Resolution: Fast CDCL + Optimal Max-SMT:Loom lowers manifests, monotonic capability-gated features, and native sys-dependencies into The Package Calculus IR (ICFP 2026) and runs a two-tier Rust solver:
Tier 1 — Everyday Fast Path (resolvo CDCL, under 50 ms): Uses Conflict-Driven Clause Learning over version intervals to resolve 1,000+ node graphs in milliseconds. When unsatisfiable, Loom extracts a minimal PubGrub-style incompatibility derivation tree translating clause conflicts into plain-English causal chains (including which effect-ceiling or modal SemVer rule rejected a candidate version).
Tier 2 — Policy and Upgrade Optimizer (vesper resolve --optimize, PacSolve / Cargo Sherlock Max-SMT): Encodes the Package Calculus graph alongside OSV vulnerability feeds, capability lattice weights, and maintainer trust costs into Max-SMT (bitwuzla / z3) to synthesize Pareto-optimal lockfiles under explicit objectives such as vesper update --minimize cve,capability_surface,staleness,dup_versions or vesper audit --max-trust-cost 15.
Anti-Slopsquatting Namespace Governance and Multi-Builder Transparency:To defend against both compromised maintainers (xz-style backdoors) and AI-hallucinated package confusion attacks (USENIX Security 2025):
Mandatory OIDC-Scoped Namespaces: Bare top-level names (http, crypto, codec) belong exclusively to Vesper's curated standard platform (std/*). All third-party packages require cryptographic namespace ownership (@org/pkg) bound via keyless Sigstore OIDC to a verified source repository and transparency log (Rekor v2) protected by The Update Framework (TUF).
Agent-Aware Slopsquatting Gate: During vesper add (and enforced strictly whenever VESPER_AGENT_MODE=1 or CI is detected), Loom evaluates conflation and edit-distance similarity against established packages, namespace age, audit velocity, and Cargo Sherlock trust cost. Newly registered lookalikes or conflated names are quarantined before download.
Lila Decentralized Reproducibility Consensus: Precompiled binary/CLIF cache artifacts are marked trusted only when accompanied by at least 2 independent hermetic builder attestations (Lila protocol) matching the exact BLAKE3 output digest built from the tagged Git tree.
Content-Addressed Store, Reflink Workspaces and Lean 4 Proof Certificates:
Content-Addressed Global Store (~/.vesper/store/): Stores immutable source trees, typed .vhir interface files, pre-saturated egglog rewrite rules, Cranelift .o archives, and compiled Lean 4 .olean proof certificates indexed by BLAKE3(derivation_inputs). Workspaces materialize instantaneously via copy-on-write filesystem reflinks (clonefile on macOS APFS, FICLONE on Linux XFS/Btrfs).
Compositional Verification Distribution: When a library author proves functional correctness properties (such as B-tree invariants or CRC framing) via Vesper's Lean 4 backend, vesper publish bundles the machine-checked .olean certificate inside the package artifact. Downstream vesper verify runs import upstream theorems directly as verified certificates in milliseconds rather than re-proving dependencies from scratch.
8.3 Declarative Manifest (Loom.toml) and Lockfile (Loom.lock) Design
Loom.toml is strictly declarative (parsed in under 1 ms with zero Turing-complete evaluation) and makes capability budgets, monotonic features, optimization policies, and verification targets first-class:
[package]
name = "@robot-head/krabka-wal"
version = "0.4.0"
edition = "2026"
license = "Apache-2.0"
# Maximum lexical effects and unsafe capabilities ANY module in this package may use:
capability_ceiling = ["Storage", "Net", "Clock", "Fail"]
forbid_unsafe_ffi = true
[features]
# Features in Loom are strictly monotonic and additive over capabilities:
io_uring = { enables_effects = ["Storage"], sys_deps = ["liburing ^2.6.0"] }
compression = { deps = ["@vesper-compression/lz4-pure"] }
[dependencies]
# Pure library: compiler guarantees ZERO filesystem, network, clock, or FFI access!
"@vesper-core/bytes" = { version = "^1.3.0", effects = [] }
"@vesper-compression/lz4-pure" = { version = "^2.1.0", effects = [], optional = true }
# Scoped storage dependency: permitted Storage and Fail effects, never Net or Process:
"@vesper-io/uring-reactor" = { version = "^0.9.2", effects = ["Storage", "Fail"] }
[build-sandbox]
# Optional WASI build component (replaces Cargo's unsandboxed build.rs):
script = "build.vsp"
wasi_read_paths = ["proto/"]
fuel_limit = 50_000_000
allow_network = false
[resolver.policy]
# Max-SMT objectives and supply-chain gates (PacSolve + Cargo Sherlock + Lila):
optimize = ["zero_cve", "min_capability_surface", "min_dup_majors", "min_staleness"]
min_repro_attestations = 2
block_unvetted_namespaces = true
[verification]
# Extract and bundle Lean 4 functional specifications + .olean certificates on publish:
export_lean4_specs = ["src/wal_txn.vsp"]
The generated Loom.lock pins every resolved node in the Package Calculus graph with four cryptographic anchors:
source_blake3: Merkle tree hash of unpacked source files.
interface_mif: mif:blake3:... digest of exported types, modes (copy/affine/linear), place projections, and effect rows (enabling early-cutoff incremental builds).
effective_capabilities: Verified transitive effect set (["Storage", "Fail"]).
provenance: Sigstore Rekor log index, multi-builder Lila bitwise reproducibility signatures, and optional .olean proof digest.
8.4 Unified Batteries-Included CLI and Rust Implementation Crates
Every workflow ships built-in under a single static vesper binary—eliminating external plugin sprawl:
Command
Built-In Capability (Replaces Fragmented External Tooling)

Scaffolds package or workspace with strict capability_ceiling = [] secure-by-default Loom.toml manifest (Section 8.3).
vesper add @org/pkg
Runs anti-slopsquatting similarity and Sigstore OIDC lineage gates, prompts on non-empty effects, and updates Loom.toml (Section 8.2).

Executes fuel-metered Wasmtime build.vsp and #[wasm_macro] components, hydrates CAS artifacts with mif:blake3 early cutoff, and renders ariadne diagnostics (Sections 8, 16, 21).
vesper test (--sim, --sim-ui, --mutate)
Runs parallel unit/property tests, deterministic SimPulse network/crash simulation (Section 12.6), headless SimAura UI/WCAG suites (Section 18.6), and mutation testing (Section 29.1).
vesper fuzz (--differential)
Runs zero-nightly Cranelift SSA edge-coverage + Bitwuzla/Z3 concolic fuzzing with ChoiceTape shrinking and MPK-isolated @differential_shadow C/Rust equivalence checks (Section 29).

Extracts prophecy-free Lean 4 models, runs Crucible concolic counterexample pre-checks (--refute), links CAS .olean certificates, and audits zero sorryAx (Sections 11, 29.1).

Analyzes C headers and Rust crates to synthesize .vsys manifests, wire struct layouts, Mode::Linear obligations, and lifetime-free inout field-tree projections (Section 29.3).

Attaches zero-instrumentation live Pulse fiber profiler, D3D12/VK/MTL pass timestamp stream, and parked Mode::Linear obligation inspector (Sections 12.6, 17.5, 19.2).

Launches the Vesper Lens sub-2ms salsa incremental Language Server Protocol daemon with PoisonedRecovery obligation-guided autocomplete (Section 21).
vesper resolve --optimize
Invokes the Max-SMT PacSolve / Cargo Sherlock optimizer to minimize CVEs, capability surface, duplicate majors, staleness, and trust cost (Section 8.2).
vesper semver
Verifies modal (copy/affine/linear), FieldTree projection, and lexical-effect contextual substitutability (I_new <=_pub I_old) with counterexample witness synthesis (Section 9).
vesper audit / vesper publish
Runs call-graph-reachable OSV scans, SLSA v1.1 SBOM export, SemVer + Lean 4 .olean hygiene gates, clean-room reproducibility checks, and keyless Sigstore publishing (Sections 8, 11.4).
Rust Crate Architecture (crates/vesper-loom-*)
Loom integrates cleanly as a sibling subsystem alongside the 5-stage compiler workspace:
vesper-loom-calculus: Implements Gibb et al.'s Package Calculus IR, monotonic feature desugaring, and native sys-package reductions.
vesper-loom-solver: Embeds resolvo for sub-50ms CDCL resolution and incompatibility trees, paired with an optional bitwuzla / z3 Max-SMT backend for --optimize and trust-cost minimization.
vesper-loom-sandbox: Embeds wasmtime (wasm32-wasip2) with deterministic fuel accounting, epoch-zero clocks, and capability-restricted virtual filesystems (cap-std) for build.vsp and macros.
vesper-loom-cas: High-concurrency blake3 Merkle store using redb metadata indexing, OS copy-on-write reflinks (reflink-copy), and HTTP/3 sparse-index + zstd binary substituter clients.
vesper-loom-trust: Implements sigstore-rs keyless OIDC verification, TUF repository metadata (tough), Lila multi-builder reproducibility quorum checks, and slopsquatting lexical/conflation detectors.
9. Formal SemVer Variance and Interface Subtyping Calculus (vesper semver)
Rather than trusting human-curated version numbers, Vesper Loom enforces Contextual Substitutability mathematically at publish and resolution time via vesper semver. Given a baseline .vhir package interface I_old and candidate upgrade I_new, I_new is Minor/Patch-compatible (I_new ≼_pub I_old) if and only if every well-typed downstream module C that satisfies modal linearity, disjoint place-projection borrowing, Resumption Frame Conditions, and Loom.toml capability ceilings against I_old remains well-typed—and preserves Theorem 2 (Total Linear Exhaustion) and No Authority Amplification—when linked against I_new without modifying C.
9.1 Substructural Mode, Effect-Row, and Place-Tree Subtyping
All signature compatibility rules reduce to four decidable partial orders over λ_Vesper:
Modal Value & Kind Ordering (m_new ⊑ m_old):Recall copy ⊑ affine ⊑ linear. Because U(copy) = N, U(affine) = {0, 1}, and U(linear) = {1}, strengthening a produced value or ADT kind downward along ⊑ (for example, linear → affine or affine → copy) strictly expands what callers may do with the value while keeping all existing single-consumption code valid via [T-SubMode]. Conversely, moving upward (copy → affine or affine → linear) invalidates caller contractions, implicit drops (discard(Γ)), and [T-Let] Resumption Frame checks.
Lexical Effect-Row Subtyping (ε_new <: ε_old):An upgraded effect row ε_new safely replaces ε_old when it neither introduces ungranted capabilities nor degrades continuation resumption guarantees:
[SV-EffRow]
  caps(ε_new) ⊆ caps(ε_old)      row_vars(ε_new) ⊆ row_vars(ε_old)
  res(ε_old) ⊑ res(ε_new)        (where affine ⊑ linear and res(∅) = linear)
  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  ε_new <: ε_old
Why res(ε_old) ⊑ res(ε_new) is Required: In [T-Let], every live binding z held across a call e1 must satisfy m_z ⊑ res(ε1). Removing an abortable Fail effect raises res(ε) from affine to linear; by transitivity (m_z ⊑ affine ⊑ linear), all existing callers continue to typecheck. Adding an abortable effect drops res(ε) from linear to affine, breaking any caller holding a live linear resource across the call.
Disjoint Field-Tree Narrowing (π_new ⊆ π_old):Let leaves(π) denote the set of leaf field paths selected by a projection tree π (with a bare root variable x denoting the full field tree ⊤_τ). Narrowing a borrowed projection (leaves(π_new) ⊆ leaves(π_old), such as inout self.{index, metrics} → inout self.{index}) preserves every caller's [T-CallProj] disjointness obligation p_i ⊥ p_j, because leaves(π_new) ∩ leaves(π_other) ⊆ leaves(π_old) ∩ leaves(π_other) = ∅.
Passing-Mode Invariance on Linear Consumption (μ_new ≡_pass μ_old):
Changing sink → in or inout is Major-breaking whenever the parameter type can instantiate to linear: callers relied on sink to discharge their linear obligation, so leaving the argument unconsumed violates Total Linear Exhaustion at the call site.
Changing in → inout or sink is Major-breaking: callers holding only shared in access or reusing an affine binding after the call can no longer satisfy [T-CallProj].
Changing inout → in is Minor-safe only via explicit reborrow coercion when π_new ⊆ π_old; otherwise passing modes remain invariant while π narrows.
9.2 Declaration and Signature Compatibility Judgments
[SV-Fn]
  Δ_new = [ᾱ : mod m̄_bound_new]     Δ_old = [ᾱ : mod m̄_bound_old]
  ∀k,  m_bound_old_k ⊑ m_bound_new_k                (contravariant generic modal bounds)
  m_fn_new ⊑ m_fn_old                               (covariant closure/fn mode)
  ∀i,  π_i_new ⊆ π_i_old                            (narrowed field-tree borrow)
  ∀i,  (μ_i ∈ {in, sink} ⟹ Δ_old ⊢ τ_i_old <: τ_i_new)
     ∧ (μ_i = inout      ⟹ Δ_old ⊢ τ_i_new ≡ τ_i_old)  (mutable place invariance)
  Δ_old ⊢ τ_ret_new <: τ_ret_old    m_ret_new ⊑ m_ret_old
  ε_new <: ε_old
  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  (fn @ m_fn_new [Δ_new] (μ̄ x̄.π̄_new : τ̄_new) -> τ_ret_new @ m_ret_new eff [ε_new])
    ≼_pub
  (fn @ m_fn_old [Δ_old] (μ̄ x̄.π̄_old : τ̄_old) -> τ_ret_old @ m_ret_old eff [ε_old])
[SV-Adt-Closed]
  vis(T) = closed_fields     Δ_old ⊢ k_T_new ⊑ k_T_old
  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  (m_new struct T[ᾱ] { private ... }) ≼_pub (m_old struct T[ᾱ] { private ... })
[SV-Enum-Open]
  open enum E[ᾱ]     variants(E_old) ⊆ variants(E_new)
  ∀ added V(τ̄_v) ∈ variants(E_new) \ variants(E_old),  Δ_old ⊢ k_(τ̄_v) ⊑ k_(E_old)
  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  E_new ≼_pub E_old
[SV-Effect-Decl]
  ops(E_new) = ops(E_old)     (sealed(E) ⟹ q_old ⊑ q_new)     (open(E) ⟹ q_new = q_old)
  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  (q_new effect E) ≼_pub (q_old effect E)
Notice two critical soundness guardrails captured above:
Open Enum Modal Preservation ([SV-Enum-Open]): Adding a new variant CorruptWal(WriteTxn) carrying a linear payload to an existing affine open enum IoError is rejected as Major-breaking, because wildcard match arms _ => ... in downstream crates require k_IoError ⊑ affine to discard unknown variants safely.
Open vs. Sealed Effect Resumption Variance ([SV-Effect-Decl]): If downstream crates are permitted to write handle E blocks (open(E)), changing effect E (q = affine) to linear effect E (q = linear) is Major-breaking because existing third-party handler clauses that drop the continuation k fail [T-Handle]. Only sealed effects (handled exclusively inside the defining package) may strengthen q from affine to linear in a minor release.
9.3 Complete SemVer Classification Matrix and Counterexample Synthesis
Interface Evolution (I_old → I_new)
SemVer Class
Formal Rule / Soundness Reason
Refactor function body or private helper without changing .vhir
Patch (mif identical)
Triggers Early Cutoff; downstream crates skip recompilation entirely.
Relax generic parameter bound (T: mod copy → T: mod linear)
Minor (I_new ≼_pub I_old)
Contravariant m_bound_old ⊑ m_bound_new in [SV-Fn]; strictly more types instantiate T.
Strengthen return mode or sealed struct mode (linear → affine → copy)
Minor (I_new ≼_pub I_old)
Covariant [T-SubMode] keeps single-consumption callers valid while permitting drops/copies.
Strengthen struct/enum mode (affine → linear or copy → affine)
Major (Breaking)
Breaks caller discard(Γ), contraction, and [T-Let] Resumption Frame Conditions.
Remove Fail[E] or other capability from function eff [...]
Minor (I_new ≼_pub I_old)
caps shrinks and res(ε) rises (affine ⊑ linear), preserving [T-Let] frames.
Add Fail[E] or Storage to function eff [...]
Major (Breaking)
Violates caller capability ceilings and invalidates live linear variables across [T-Let].
Narrow inout projection (inout self.{index, metrics} → inout self.index)
Minor (I_new ≼_pub I_old)
π_new ⊆ π_old preserves all caller [T-CallProj] disjointness proofs.
Widen inout projection (inout self.index → inout self.{index, config})
Major (Breaking)
Collides with concurrent in self.config borrows at caller sites.
Change sink x: T to in x: T on potentially linear T
Major (Breaking)
Leaves linear argument x unconsumed at call site, breaking Theorem 2.
Whenever vesper semver or vesper publish detects I_new ⋠_pub I_old without a major version increment, the solver synthesizes a minimal broken caller witness—a 5–10 line synthetic Vesper snippet demonstrating the exact [T-Let], [T-CallProj], or [T-Handle] failure induced in downstream code.

Vesper's compiler, verifier, SemVer engine, and Loom package manager live in a single modular Rust workspace compiled into one unified vesper binary.

10.2 Substructural Modes, Field Trees, and Lexical Effects (vesper-hir)
The core type/effect representation encodes the substructural lattice copy ⊑ affine ⊑ linear, resumption modes, and trie-structured disjoint field projections directly into compact value types:
// crates/vesper-hir/src/ty.rs
use la_arena::Idx;
use rustc_hash::FxHashMap;
use smol_str::SmolStr;
/// Substructural mode lattice ordered by `Copy < Affine < Linear` (`copy ⊑ affine ⊑ linear`).
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
#[repr(u8)]
pub enum Mode {
    Copy = 0,   // ⊥: weakening + contraction allowed
    Affine = 1, // implicit drop allowed; bitwise copy forbidden
    Linear = 2, // ⊤: exact-once consumption; neither drop nor copy allowed
}
impl Mode {
    /// Lattice join (`m1 ⊔ m2`): takes the stricter ownership obligation.
    #[inline]
    pub fn join(self, other: Self) -> Self {
        core::cmp::max(self, other)
    }
    /// Lattice meet (`m1 ⊓ m2`): takes the more permissive mode.
    #[inline]
    pub fn meet(self, other: Self) -> Self {
        core::cmp::min(self, other)
    }
    /// Predicate `discard(m)`: true iff `m ⊑ Mode::Affine`.
    #[inline]
    pub fn is_discardable(self) -> bool {
        self <= Self::Affine
    }
}
/// Continuation resumption contract `q ∈ {affine, linear}` on an effect declaration.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
#[repr(u8)]
pub enum ResumptionMode {
    Affine = 1, // Handler may abort (e.g., `effect Fail[E]`)
    Linear = 2, // Handler must resume k exactly once (e.g., `linear effect Storage`)
}
impl ResumptionMode {
    #[inline]
    pub fn as_mode(self) -> Mode {
        match self {
            Self::Affine => Mode::Affine,
            Self::Linear => Mode::Linear,
        }
    }
}
/// Second-class parameter passing modes (`[T-CallProj]`).
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum PassMode {
    In,    // Shared read-only projection (`shr`)
    InOut, // Exclusive mutable projection (`mut`)
    Sink,  // Ownership transfer / linear consumption (`own`)
}
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub struct FieldIdx(pub u16);
/// Prefix tree representing disjoint field projections like `broker.{index, metrics.counters}`.
/// An empty `children` map represents a leaf selection covering the entire sub-place.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct FieldTree {
    pub children: FxHashMap<FieldIdx, FieldTree>,
}
impl FieldTree {
    pub fn whole_place() -> Self {
        Self { children: FxHashMap::default() }
    }
    /// Checks `π_1 ⊥ π_2`: true iff the two selector trees share zero overlapping leaf paths.
    pub fn is_disjoint(&self, other: &Self) -> bool {
        if self.children.is_empty() || other.children.is_empty() {
            return false; // One selects the entire sub-struct, overlapping anything beneath it
        }
        for (field, lhs_sub) in &self.children {
            if let Some(rhs_sub) = other.children.get(field) {
                if !lhs_sub.is_disjoint(rhs_sub) {
                    return false;
                }
            }
        }
        true
    }
    /// Checks `π_new ⊆ π_old` for `vesper semver` `[SV-Fn]` projection narrowing.
    pub fn is_subset_of(&self, older: &Self) -> bool {
        if older.children.is_empty() {
            return true; // `older` borrowed the entire sub-place
        }
        if self.children.is_empty() {
            return false; // `self` widens to the entire sub-place while `older` was partial
        }
        self.children.iter().all(|(f, sub)| {
            older.children.get(f).is_some_and(|old_sub| sub.is_subset_of(old_sub))
        })
    }
}
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub struct EffectId(pub u32);
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct EffectInst {
    pub cap_label: Option<SmolStr>,
    pub effect_id: EffectId,
    pub resumption: ResumptionMode,
    pub type_args: Vec<Idx<Ty>>,
}
/// Lexical effect row `eff [l1: E1^q1, ..., ln: En^qn | tail_var]`.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct EffectRow {
    pub instances: Vec<EffectInst>,
    pub tail_var: Option<u32>,
}
impl EffectRow {
    /// Computes `res(ε) = ⊓_i q_i`, with `res(∅) = Mode::Linear`.
    /// Note: unresolved `tail_var` conservatively bounds resumption via context Δ.
    pub fn resumption_guarantee(&self, tail_bound: Mode) -> Mode {
        self.instances
            .iter()
            .fold(tail_bound, |acc, inst| acc.meet(inst.resumption.as_mode()))
    }
}
10.3 Place IR and Place Capability Graph Checker (vesper-mir-pcg)
After bidirectional inference and OxCaml-style mode crossing ([T-Cross]), HIR lowers into PlaceIr. The PCG engine performs forward dataflow over basic blocks, checking both disjoint field borrowing and the Resumption Frame Condition across every effectful edge:
// crates/vesper-mir-pcg/src/pcg.rs
use rustc_hash::FxHashMap;
use vesper_hir::ty::{EffectRow, FieldIdx, FieldTree, Mode, PassMode};
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub struct LocalId(pub u32);
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub struct BlockId(pub u32);
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct PlaceProjection {
    pub local: LocalId,
    pub tree: FieldTree,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub enum Statement {
    Assign { dst: LocalId, ty_mode: Mode },
    /// Linear struct/enum unpacking (`[T-Match]`), discharging `src` and initializing `fields`.
    UnpackLinear { src: LocalId, bound_locals: Vec<(LocalId, Mode)> },
    CallProj {
        args: Vec<(PassMode, PlaceProjection)>,
        ret_dst: LocalId,
        ret_mode: Mode,
        effects: EffectRow,
    },
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub enum Terminator {
    Goto(BlockId),
    SwitchInt { discr: LocalId, targets: Vec<BlockId> },
    PerformEffect {
        effects: EffectRow,
        resume_target: BlockId,
        /// Populated when `res(effects) == Mode::Affine` (abortable handler unwind edge).
        abort_unwind: Option<BlockId>,
    },
    Return,
}
/// Per-local capability state in the Place Capability Graph lattice.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum LocalCapability {
    LiveOwned { mode: Mode, moved_subtrees: FieldTree },
    Consumed,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum PcgError {
    OverlappingProjection { local: LocalId },
    UnconsumedLinearAtScopeExit { local: LocalId },
    InconsistentLinearConsumptionAtJoin { block: BlockId, local: LocalId },
    ResumptionFrameViolation { live_linear: LocalId },
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct PcgState {
    pub locals: FxHashMap<LocalId, LocalCapability>,
}
impl PcgState {
    /// Enforces `[T-CallProj]` pairwise disjointness + `sink` linear consumption.
    pub fn check_call_projections(
        &mut self,
        args: &[(PassMode, PlaceProjection)],
        effects: &EffectRow,
    ) -> Result<(), PcgError> {
        // 1. Pairwise Alias-XOR-Mutability across all place arguments
        for (i, (m_i, p_i)) in args.iter().enumerate() {
            for (m_j, p_j) in &args[i + 1..] {
                let both_shared = matches!((m_i, m_j), (PassMode::In, PassMode::In));
                if !both_shared && p_i.local == p_j.local && !p_i.tree.is_disjoint(&p_j.tree) {
                    return Err(PcgError::OverlappingProjection { local: p_i.local });
                }
            }
        }
        // 2. Consume `sink` arguments first (so consumed linear values aren't flagged in frame check)
        for (pass_mode, proj) in args {
            if *pass_mode == PassMode::Sink {
                self.locals.insert(proj.local, LocalCapability::Consumed);
            }
        }
        // 3. Enforce `[T-Let]` Resumption Frame Condition: ∀ live z, m_z ⊑ res(ε)
        let res_guarantee = effects.resumption_guarantee(Mode::Linear);
        for (&local, cap) in &self.locals {
            if let LocalCapability::LiveOwned { mode, .. } = cap {
                if *mode > res_guarantee {
                    return Err(PcgError::ResumptionFrameViolation { live_linear: local });
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
    /// Enforces uniform linear consumption across CFG join points (`[T-Match]` / `if-else`).
    pub fn join_at_block(&self, other: &Self, block: BlockId) -> Result<Self, PcgError> {
        let mut merged = self.locals.clone();
        for (&local, rhs_cap) in &other.locals {
            match (merged.get(&local), rhs_cap) {
                (Some(LocalCapability::LiveOwned { mode: Mode::Linear, .. }), LocalCapability::Consumed)
                | (Some(LocalCapability::Consumed), LocalCapability::LiveOwned { mode: Mode::Linear, .. }) => {
                    return Err(PcgError::InconsistentLinearConsumptionAtJoin { block, local });
                }
                (Some(LocalCapability::LiveOwned { mode, .. }), LocalCapability::Consumed)
                    if mode.is_discardable() =>
                {
                    // Implicit drop inserted on the live incoming edge for `affine` / `copy` local
                    merged.insert(local, LocalCapability::Consumed);
                }
                _ => {}
            }
        }
        Ok(Self { locals: merged })
    }
    /// Enforces `discard(Γ)` at function `Return`.
    pub fn check_return_exhaustion(&self) -> Result<(), PcgError> {
        for (&local, cap) in &self.locals {
            if let LocalCapability::LiveOwned { mode: Mode::Linear, .. } = cap {
                return Err(PcgError::UnconsumedLinearAtScopeExit { local });
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}
10.4 Vesper Loom Core Interfaces (vesper-loom-*)
The Loom crates bind directly to vesper-hir and vesper-semver so capability confinement, WASI build sandboxing, and content-addressed proof caching operate as first-class compiler phases:
// crates/vesper-loom-calculus/src/lib.rs
use rustc_hash::FxHashSet;
use smol_str::SmolStr;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct CapabilityCeiling {
    pub allowed_effects: FxHashSet<SmolStr>,
    pub allow_unsafe_ffi: bool,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub enum ConfinementViolation {
    AuthorityAmplification {
        pkg: SmolStr,
        dep: SmolStr,
        escalated_effect: SmolStr,
    },
    ForbiddenUnsafeFfi { pkg: SmolStr },
}
impl CapabilityCeiling {
    /// Verifies the No Authority Amplification invariant across every dependency edge `P -> D`.
    pub fn verify_edge(
        &self,
        pkg_name: &SmolStr,
        dep_name: &SmolStr,
        dep_effective: &Self,
    ) -> Result<(), ConfinementViolation> {
        if dep_effective.allow_unsafe_ffi && !self.allow_unsafe_ffi {
            return Err(ConfinementViolation::ForbiddenUnsafeFfi { pkg: dep_name.clone() });
        }
        for eff in &dep_effective.allowed_effects {
            if !self.allowed_effects.contains(eff) {
                return Err(ConfinementViolation::AuthorityAmplification {
                    pkg: pkg_name.clone(),
                    dep: dep_name.clone(),
                    escalated_effect: eff.clone(),
                });
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

// crates/vesper-loom-sandbox/src/lib.rs
use std::path::Path;
use wasmtime::{Config, Engine, Linker, Store};
use wasmtime_wasi::{DirPerms, FilePerms, WasiCtxBuilder};
pub struct HermeticBuildConfig<'a> {
    pub wasm_component_bytes: &'a [u8],
    pub read_only_inputs: &'a [(&'a Path, &'a str)],
    pub out_dir: &'a Path,
    pub fuel_limit: u64,
}
/// Executes `build.vsp` or syntax macros inside a deterministic, zero-network WASI-p2 sandbox.
pub fn run_hermetic_build_step(cfg: &HermeticBuildConfig<'_>) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    let mut engine_cfg = Config::new();
    engine_cfg.consume_fuel(true); // Deterministic instruction metering (no wall-clock nondeterminism)
    engine_cfg.wasm_component_model(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&engine_cfg)?;
    let mut wasi_builder = WasiCtxBuilder::new();
    // Explicitly omit socket/network capabilities and pin environment variables:
    wasi_builder.env("OUT_DIR", "/out");
    for (host_path, guest_mount) in cfg.read_only_inputs {
        wasi_builder.preopened_dir(host_path, guest_mount, DirPerms::READ, FilePerms::READ)?;
    }
    wasi_builder.preopened_dir(cfg.out_dir, "/out", DirPerms::all(), FilePerms::all())?;
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, wasi_builder.build());
    store.set_fuel(cfg.fuel_limit)?;
    let component = wasmtime::component::Component::from_binary(&engine, cfg.wasm_component_bytes)?;
    let mut linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    wasmtime_wasi::add_to_linker_sync(&mut linker)?;
    let instance = linker.instantiate(&mut store, &component)?;
    let run_fn = instance.get_typed_func::<(), (Result<(), String>,)>(&mut store, "run-build")?;
    let (res,) = run_fn.call(&mut store, ())?;
    res.map_err(|msg| anyhow::anyhow!("hermetic build.vsp failed: {msg}"))
}
Content-Addressed Store (redb + blake3) & Dual Backend Pipeline
vesper-loom-cas (redb Schema): Defines four zero-copy redb::TableDefinition maps inside ~/.vesper/store/index.redb:
DERIVATIONS: &[u8; 32] -> DerivationRecord (maps input Merkle hash to unpacked CoW store directory).
MIF_CACHE: &[u8; 32] -> &[u8; 32] (maps .vhir interface_mif digest to precompiled Cranelift .o archive for Early Cutoff).
OLEAN_CERTS: &[u8; 32] -> &[u8; 32] (maps interface_mif + spec hash to verified Lean 4 .olean proof certificates).
LILA_QUORUM: &[u8; 32] -> Vec<BuilderAttestation> (stores Sigstore/Rekor + multi-builder reproducibility signatures).
