11. The Lean 4 Functional Proof Extractor (vesper-verify-lean)
Memory safety and modal linearity prevent crashes, data races, and forgotten protocol obligations, yet critical systems components—WAL storage engines, consensus state machines, cryptographic codecs, and capability routers—still require mathematical proof of functional correctness. Recent Rust-to-Lean verification pipelines such as Charon + Aeneas and Hax (A Rust-to-Lean Verification Pipeline with AI Provers, CAV/AIMACS 2026) demonstrate that lifting systems code into pure functional Lean 4 enables practical verification backed by a small trusted kernel (itself verified in Lean4Lean, TYPES 2025). However, retrofitting functional extraction onto Rust faces three steep structural hurdles:
First-Class Mutable Reference Prophecy Overhead: Because Rust permits first-class mutable references (&'a mut T) stored inside structs and conditionally reborrowed across branches, Charon must reconstruct Low-Level Borrow Calculus (LLBC) lifetimes and Aeneas must synthesize complex backward prophecy functions to propagate mutations when region 'a expires.
Opaque Self-Referential Coroutines: Rust async state machines rely on self-referential Pin pointers and polling traits that functional extractors cannot directly lift without manual re-modeling.
Whole-Graph Re-Verification and Axiom Drift: Without native package-manager integration, Lean 4 proofs suffer from toolchain drift, bit-vector library collisions, and expensive re-checking of upstream dependencies.
vesper-verify-lean eliminates all three bottlenecks by exploiting Vesper's core invariants ([T-CallProj], [T-Let], and [T-Handle]) to perform a direct, zero-prophecy 1-to-1 functional translation from verified PlaceIr into pure Lean 4 definitions and composable .olean proof certificates.
11.1 Architectural Comparison: Rust (Charon + Aeneas) vs. Vesper (vesper-verify-lean)
Verification Dimension
Rust (Charon + Aeneas / Hax)
Vesper (vesper-verify-lean)
Mutable Borrowing Model
First-class mutable references requiring region inference and synthesized backward continuation/prophecy functions.
Second-class inout x.π parameter projections ending strictly at call return; lowers directly to pure tuple return plus lawful disjoint lenses.
Aliasing and Heap Separation
Must reason about lifetime expiration order or reborrow trees (Tree Borrows / LLBC).
vesper-mir-pcg proves p_i ⊥ p_j statically; Lean 4 models structs as pure immutable values (Type) with zero separation-logic heap framing.
Resource Protocol Completion
Drop is synchronous and mem::forget is safe; cannot assume protocol finalization on early ? returns.
Mode::Linear plus the Resumption Frame Condition (m_z ⊑ res(ε)) guarantee exact-once consumption on all paths prior to extraction.
Async and I/O Effects
Opaque Pin coroutine state machines requiring hand-written functional models.
One-shot lexical effects (eff [...]) translate directly into Freer Interaction-Tree monads (EffM) and pure state-folding handlers (VCVio style).
Dependency Proofs and Caching
Manual Lake subprojects; upstream refactors force fragile proof rebuilds.
Vesper Loom bundles kernel-checked .olean certificates in ~/.vesper/store/ (OLEAN_CERTS) with mif:blake3 early cutoff and sorryAx hygiene gates.
11.2 Formal Functional Translation Rules (PlaceIr to Lean 4)
Because vesper-hir and vesper-mir-pcg have already discharged modal linearity, alias-XOR-mutability, and lexical capability scoping, vesper-verify-lean erases ownership modes (copy, affine, linear) at the value level and translates types, disjoint projections, and effect rows purely structurally.
Scalar, BitVector, and ADT Erasure (⟦τ⟧):
Fixed-Width Machine Scalars: u8, u32, u64, i64 map directly to Lean 4 core fixed-width types (UInt8, UInt32, UInt64, Int64) backed by BitVec, enabling Lean 4's built-in bv_decide (SAT-backed bit-blasting) and omega (linear integer arithmetic) tactics without external library collisions.
Checked vs. Wrapping Arithmetic: Standard Vesper arithmetic (+, -, *) emits explicit overflow checks in the Fail[ArithOverflow] effect (or discharges bounds statically), while explicit wrapping intrinsics (wrapping_add) map directly to total UInt64.add.
Modal ADT Erasure: Both affine struct and linear struct declarations lower to standard Lean 4 structure types, and enum declarations lower to inductive types (Never maps to Empty, Unit maps to Unit).
Disjoint Field-Tree Projections as Lawful Lenses (⟦inout x.π⟧):For every non-trivial field-tree projection x.π (such as broker.{index, metrics}: Broker), the extractor synthesizes a pure projection bundle and auto-proves three @[simp] lens laws by rfl:
get_π : ⟦τ⟧ → ⟦τ_π⟧ (extracts the projected leaf fields into a tuple or slice record).
set_π : ⟦τ⟧ → ⟦τ_π⟧ → ⟦τ⟧ (functional record update replacing only leaves in π).
Auto-Generated @[simp] Frame Lemmas:
set_get_π (s : ⟦τ⟧) : set_π s (get_π s) = s := rfl
get_set_π (s : ⟦τ⟧) (v : ⟦τ_π⟧) : get_π (set_π s v) = v := rfl
Disjoint Frame Preservation (π_1 ⊥ π_2): For every sibling field or projection π_2 disjoint from π_1, get_π2 (set_π1 s v) = get_π2 s := rfl. When a caller holds in broker.config across append_batch(inout broker.{index, metrics}, ...), Lean's simp tactic proves (b.setIndexMetrics p).config = b.config in zero manual steps.
Passing-Mode and Effect-Row Translation:Given a Vesper function f with parameters (μ_1 p_1 : τ_1, ..., μ_n p_n : τ_n), let inout_indices be the ordered indices where μ_i = inout:
Pure Inputs: Every parameter (in, inout, and sink alike) passes its projected value v_i : ⟦τ_i⟧ as a pure Lean 4 function parameter.
Pure State-Tuple Return: f returns the product of its nominal return type and the updated values of all inout projections: RetTuple(f) := ⟦τ_ret⟧ × ∏_{j ∈ inout_indices} ⟦τ_j⟧.
Adaptive Effect Monad Wrapping: Rather than forcing monadic boilerplate onto pure code, vesper-verify-lean stratifies eff [ε] into three clean tiers:
Tier 0 — Pure (ε = ∅): Emits a bare, total Lean 4 function returning RetTuple(f).
Tier 1 — Fallible Only (ε = [Fail[E]]): Wraps the return in Lean's standard Except ⟦E⟧ RetTuple(f) with do-notation (? maps natively to Lean 4's monadic early-return).
Tier 2 — General One-Shot Lexical Effects (ε = [E_1^q1, ..., E_k^qk]): Wraps the return in a universe-polymorphic Freer Interaction-Tree Monad EffM ⟦ε⟧ RetTuple(f):
-- Core Vesper verification prelude (VesperStd.EffM)
inductive EffRowSig where
  | nil  : EffRowSig
  | cons : (Op : Type) -> (Ret : Op -> Type) -> ResumptionMode -> EffRowSig -> EffRowSig
-- One-shot Freer / Interaction-Tree monad representing lexical effect computations
inductive EffM : EffRowSig -> Type -> Type 1 where
  | pure : a -> EffM sig a
  | vis  : MemberOp Op Ret q sig -> (op : Op) -> (Ret op -> EffM sig a) -> EffM sig a
Translating Lexical handle Blocks ([T-Handle]): Because Vesper forbids multi-shot continuations and restricts ambient handler captures Γ_h to copy, a stateful handle l : E^q state s = e_init in e_body with { ... } lowers directly into a structural recursive interpreter folding over EffM. Verifying append_batch against a crash-consistency storage model is simply applying a pure interpreter function in Lean 4.
Structured Loops and Termination Measures:Natural loops in PlaceIr lower via functional SSA conversion into tail-recursive auxiliary functions f.loop taking only the loop-carried mutated locals. Optional decreases annotations emit termination_by clauses discharged automatically via Lean 4's omega tactic.
11.3 End-to-End Example: Extracting and Proving WriteTxn and append_batch
Given the Vesper storage transaction code from Section 2, running vesper verify generates deterministic Types.lean and Funs.lean files, allowing engineers (or integrated AI provers like Aristotle and aesop) to state and prove crash-consistency and invariant theorems in Proofs.lean:
-- Auto-generated by vesper-verify-lean from src/wal_txn.vsp
-- Interface MIF: mif:blake3:9f84c2e1...
import VesperStd.Prelude
namespace KrabkaWal
structure WriteTxn where
  walSlot    : UInt64
  dirtyPages : BufferPoolSlice
structure CommitReceipt where
  walSlot : UInt64
structure BrokerIndexMetricsProj where
  index          : SegmentedIndex
  batchesWritten : UInt64
-- Synthesized lawful disjoint lens for inout broker.{index, metrics}
@[simp] def Broker.getIndexMetrics (b : Broker) : BrokerIndexMetricsProj :=
  { index := b.index, batchesWritten := b.metrics.batchesWritten }
@[simp] def Broker.setIndexMetrics (b : Broker) (p : BrokerIndexMetricsProj) : Broker :=
  { b with index := p.index, metrics := { b.metrics with batchesWritten := p.batchesWritten } }
@[simp] theorem Broker.config_preserved_by_setIndexMetrics (b : Broker) (p : BrokerIndexMetricsProj) :
    (b.setIndexMetrics p).config = b.config := rfl
-- Effect operations for linear effect Storage
inductive StorageOp where
  | beginTxn     (maxBytes : UInt64)
  | flushWal     (slot : UInt64) (pages : BufferPoolSlice)
  | discardWal   (slot : UInt64)
  | releaseSlice (pages : BufferPoolSlice)
  | recycleBatch (batch : RecordBatch)
def WriteTxn.commit (self : WriteTxn) : EffM [StorageEff, FailEff IoError] CommitReceipt := do
  let { walSlot, dirtyPages } := self
  Storage.flushWal walSlot dirtyPages
  Storage.releaseSlice dirtyPages
  pure { walSlot }
def WriteTxn.abort (self : WriteTxn) : EffM [StorageEff] Unit := do
  let { walSlot, dirtyPages } := self
  Storage.discardWal walSlot
  Storage.releaseSlice dirtyPages
-- inout broker.{index, metrics} returns (CommitReceipt x BrokerIndexMetricsProj)
def appendBatch
    (brokerProj : BrokerIndexMetricsProj)
    (config     : BrokerConfig)
    (batch      : RecordBatch)
    : EffM [StorageEff, FailEff IoError] (CommitReceipt × BrokerIndexMetricsProj) := do
  let tx <- liftLinear (Storage.beginTxn config.maxBatchBytes)
  match validateCrc batch with
  | .ok () =>
    let index' := SegmentedIndex.insertInPlace brokerProj.index batch
    let written' <- checkedAddU64 brokerProj.batchesWritten 1
    let receipt <- WriteTxn.commit tx
    pure (receipt, { index := index', batchesWritten := written' })
  | .error err =>
    liftLinear (Storage.recycleBatch batch)
    liftLinear (WriteTxn.abort tx)
    Fail.raise err
end KrabkaWal
In proofs/WalSafety.lean, proving Transactional Atomicity—that on any CRC failure, the WAL slot allocated by beginTxn is cleanly discarded, zero buffer slices remain pinned, and broker state is unchanged—requires zero heap-separation boilerplate:
-- Proof in proofs/WalSafety.lean
theorem appendBatch_crc_err_aborts_cleanly
    (st : RefStorageModel) (proj : BrokerIndexMetricsProj)
    (cfg : BrokerConfig) (batch : RecordBatch) (crcErr : IoError)
    (h_crc : validateCrc batch = .error crcErr) :
    let (st', res) := RefStorageModel.run st (appendBatch proj cfg batch)
    res = .error crcErr ∧
    st'.activeWalSlots = st.activeWalSlots ∧
    st'.pinnedSlices   = st.pinnedSlices := by
  simp [appendBatch, h_crc, WriteTxn.abort, RefStorageModel.run]
  omega
This proof closes in two lines (simp followed by omega) because:
There are no prophecy variables or pointer-aliasing side conditions since [T-CallProj] guaranteed disjoint value projections.
tx could not leak on the error path because Vesper's [T-Let] Resumption Frame Condition forced WriteTxn.abort tx prior to Fail.raise err.
RefStorageModel.run is an ordinary pure Lean 4 interpreter folding StorageOp over an abstract WAL state.
11.4 Compositional .olean Certificates and Axiom-Hygiene Governance in Vesper Loom
vesper-verify-lean integrates tightly with Vesper Loom (Section 8) and vesper semver (Section 9) so formal verification scales modularly across dependency graphs:
Four-File Hermetic Split: For each verified module, Loom materializes:
Types.lean and Funs.lean (compiler-generated, read-only, hashed into lean_model_blake3).
Specs.lean (exported public theorem statements and invariants, hashed into lean_spec_mif).
Proofs.lean (private proof scripts compiled into machine-checked .olean bytecode; editing tactics never invalidates downstream caches).
Zero-sorryAx Kernel and Lean4Lean Hygiene Gate: During vesper publish, Loom runs Lean 4's kernel environment inspector (and optionally the external self-verified Lean4Lean typechecker) over every exported theorem in Specs.lean to audit its transitive axiom footprint:
Permitted standard axioms: propext, Quot.sound, and Classical.choice (or strictly constructive empty axioms when forbid_classical_axioms = true in Loom.toml).
Hard-blocked axioms: sorryAx, native reduction trust escapes, or undeclared custom axiom declarations. Any unproved sorry blocks publication with an Unsound Certificate Error.
Instant Compositional Importing (OLEAN_CERTS): When @robot-head/krabka-wal imports @vesper-core/bytes, vesper verify mounts pre-verified .olean certificates from ~/.vesper/store/ via copy-on-write reflinks. Downstream proofs apply upstream lemmas in milliseconds without re-running upstream tactic search.
SemVer Proof Stability: Extending Section 9, if a dependency ships a Minor or Patch upgrade where interface_mif is compatible (I_new ≼_pub I_old) and lean_spec_mif preserves exported theorem statements (Specs_old ⊆ Specs_new), downstream .olean proof obligations remain structurally valid.
11.5 Rust Implementation Architecture (crates/vesper-verify-lean)
vesper-verify-lean operates directly on validated PlaceIr (from vesper-mir-pcg) and emits structured Lean 4 ASTs alongside an embedded certificate-verification driver:
// crates/vesper-verify-lean/src/lib.rs
use rustc_hash::FxHashSet;
use smol_str::SmolStr;
use std::path::Path;
use std::process::Command;
use vesper_hir::ty::{EffectRow, PassMode};
use vesper_mir_pcg::pcg::PlaceProjection;
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub enum LeanType {
    UInt8,
    UInt32,
    UInt64,
    Int64,
    Bool,
    Unit,
    Empty,
    Nominal { name: SmolStr, args: Vec<LeanType> },
    Prod(Vec<LeanType>),
    Except { err: Box<LeanType>, ok: Box<LeanType> },
    EffM { row_sig: Vec<SmolStr>, ret: Box<LeanType> },
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub enum LeanExpr {
    Var(SmolStr),
    LitU64(u64),
    Let { pat: SmolStr, val: Box<LeanExpr>, next: Box<LeanExpr> },
    DoBind { bound_vars: Vec<SmolStr>, call: Box<LeanExpr>, next: Box<LeanExpr> },
    Pure(Box<LeanExpr>),
    Tuple(Vec<LeanExpr>),
    App { func: SmolStr, args: Vec<LeanExpr> },
    LensGet { base: Box<LeanExpr>, lens_fn: SmolStr },
    LensSet { base: Box<LeanExpr>, lens_fn: SmolStr, new_val: Box<LeanExpr> },
}
/// Stratified effect-row encoding avoiding monadic noise on pure functions.
pub fn wrap_return_with_effects(base_ret: LeanType, effects: &EffectRow) -> LeanType {
    if effects.instances.is_empty() && effects.tail_var.is_none() {
        return base_ret; // Tier 0: Pure total Lean 4 function
    }
    if effects.instances.len() == 1 && effects.instances[0].cap_label.as_deref() == Some("Fail") {
        // Tier 1: Specialized Except E Ret
        return LeanType::Except {
            err: Box::new(LeanType::Nominal { name: "IoError".into(), args: vec![] }),
            ok: Box::new(base_ret),
        };
    }
    // Tier 2: General one-shot Freer Interaction-Tree EffM
    let row_sig = effects
        .instances
        .iter()
        .map(|inst| inst.cap_label.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| "AnonEff".into()))
        .collect();
    LeanType::EffM { row_sig, ret: Box::new(base_ret) }
}
/// Translates a [T-CallProj] statement into pure value passing plus lawful lens writeback.
pub fn lower_call_proj(
    callee: &SmolStr,
    args: &[(PassMode, PlaceProjection, SmolStr)],
    ret_dst: SmolStr,
    effects: &EffectRow,
    continuation: LeanExpr,
) -> LeanExpr {
    let mut call_args = Vec::with_capacity(args.len());
    let mut inout_writebacks: Vec<(SmolStr, SmolStr, SmolStr)> = Vec::new();
    let mut bound_vars = vec![ret_dst.clone()];
    for (idx, (mode, proj, lens_stem)) in args.iter().enumerate() {
        let root_var = SmolStr::new_inline("x_local");
        let projected_arg = if proj.tree.children.is_empty() {
            LeanExpr::Var(root_var.clone())
        } else {
            LeanExpr::LensGet {
                base: Box::new(LeanExpr::Var(root_var.clone())),
                lens_fn: format!("get_{lens_stem}").into(),
            }
        };
        call_args.push(projected_arg);
        if *mode == PassMode::InOut {
            let out_tmp: SmolStr = format!("{ret_dst}_inout_{idx}").into();
            bound_vars.push(out_tmp.clone());
            inout_writebacks.push((root_var, format!("set_{lens_stem}").into(), out_tmp));
        }
    }
    let mut post_call = continuation;
    for (root_var, set_lens, out_tmp) in inout_writebacks.into_iter().rev() {
        post_call = LeanExpr::Let {
            pat: root_var.clone(),
            val: Box::new(LeanExpr::LensSet {
                base: Box::new(LeanExpr::Var(root_var)),
                lens_fn: set_lens,
                new_val: Box::new(LeanExpr::Var(out_tmp)),
            }),
            next: Box::new(post_call),
        };
    }
    let call_expr = Box::new(LeanExpr::App { func: callee.clone(), args: call_args });
    if effects.instances.is_empty() && effects.tail_var.is_none() {
        let pat: SmolStr = bound_vars.join(", ").into();
        LeanExpr::Let { pat, val: call_expr, next: Box::new(post_call) }
    } else {
        LeanExpr::DoBind { bound_vars, call: call_expr, next: Box::new(post_call) }
    }
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub enum AxiomHygieneError {
    SorryDetected { theorem: SmolStr },
    UnauthorizedAxiom { theorem: SmolStr, axiom: SmolStr },
    LeanKernelFailed { stderr: String },
}
/// Compiles .olean certificates and audits that zero sorryAx or unapproved axioms exist.
pub fn verify_and_audit_olean_bundle(
    lake_dir: &Path,
    exported_theorems: &[SmolStr],
    allow_classical: bool,
) -> Result<blake3::Hash, AxiomHygieneError> {
    let mut allowed: FxHashSet<&str> = FxHashSet::from_iter(["propext", "Quot.sound"]);
    if allow_classical {
        allowed.insert("Classical.choice");
    }
    let output = Command::new("lake")
        .arg("build")
        .current_dir(lake_dir)
        .output()
        .map_err(|e| AxiomHygieneError::LeanKernelFailed { stderr: e.to_string() })?;
    if !output.status.success() {
        return Err(AxiomHygieneError::LeanKernelFailed {
            stderr: String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).into_owned(),
        });
    }
    let audit_out = Command::new("lake")
        .args(["env", "vesper-lean-axiom-audit"])
        .args(exported_theorems.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()))
        .current_dir(lake_dir)
        .output()
        .map_err(|e| AxiomHygieneError::LeanKernelFailed { stderr: e.to_string() })?;
    let report = String::from_utf8_lossy(&audit_out.stdout);
    for line in report.lines() {
        // Line format is theorem_name:axiom_1,axiom_2
        if let Some((thm, axioms_csv)) = line.split_once(':') {
            for ax in axioms_csv.split(',').filter(|a| !a.is_empty()) {
                if ax == "sorryAx" {
                    return Err(AxiomHygieneError::SorryDetected { theorem: thm.into() });
                }
                if !allowed.contains(ax) {
                    return Err(AxiomHygieneError::UnauthorizedAxiom {
                        theorem: thm.into(),
                        axiom: ax.into(),
                    });
                }
            }
        }
    }
    let mut hasher = blake3::Hasher::new();
    hasher.update(report.as_bytes());
    Ok(hasher.finalize())
}

12. Vesper Pulse: Native Asynchronous Runtime, Stack-Switching ABI, and Deterministic Simulation
 (@vesper-std/pulse, implemented in crates/vesper-pulse) is Vesper's native asynchronous I/O, structured concurrency, and live telemetry substrate—serving as the direct counterpart to Rust's tokio, tokio-uring, turmoil, and tokio-console, while eliminating Future polling state machines, Pin self-referential boilerplate, 'static + Send task bounds, and tokio::select! cancel-safety bugs.
Recent programming languages scholarship—including Zero-Overhead Lexical Effect Handlers (Ma et al., OOPSLA 2025), Virtualizing Continuations (Ma, Jung, and Zhang, PLDI 2026), An Equational Axiomatization of Dynamic Threads via Algebraic Effects (Kammar et al., POPL 2026), and Iris-WasmFX (2026)—demonstrates that lexically scoped capabilities paired with one-shot delimited stack switching match or outperform poll-based coroutines while preserving compositional reasoning. Vesper Pulse unifies these results directly with Vesper's modal linearity, disjoint place projections, Loom confinement, and Lean 4 verification.
12.1 Architectural Comparison: Rust (Tokio Ecosystem) vs. Vesper Pulse
Systems Concurrency Dimension
Rust (tokio + tokio-uring + tokio-console)
Vesper Pulse (@vesper-std/pulse)
Execution and Stack Model
Viral async fn coloring lowers into Pin-wrapped Future state machines polled top-down at every .await.
Colorless functions with lexical eff [...] rows execute on pooled one-shot fiber stacks; ready paths fuse inline at zero coroutine cost.
Cancel Safety (select! and Timeouts)
Dropping a losing Future at any .await silently aborts in-flight state, corrupting half-flushed protocol frames or losing read bytes.
Compile-time cancel safety:[T-Let] Resumption Frame Conditions statically reject abortable Cancel points while any linear obligation is live.
Completion I/O (io_uring and IOCP)
Synchronous Drop and safe mem::forget force owned buffer-passing ((Result, Vec)) or Arc heap wrappers so the kernel never DMAs into freed stack memory.
linear effect I/O guarantees continuation resumption after kernel CQE harvest, permitting zero-copy in and inout stack and field-slice DMA borrows.
Task Spawning and Borrowing
tokio::spawn requires 'static + Send, forcing Arc and Mutex heap indirection even for request-scoped fan-out.
Structured Pulse::scope borrows disjoint struct projections (inout broker.index vs. inout broker.repl_stream) across child fibers via [T-CallProj] with zero 'a lifetimes.
Work-Stealing vs. Thread-Per-Core
Fragmented ecosystem (tokio multi-threaded work-stealing vs. monoio or glommio local shards).
Unified via OxCaml-style portability modes (portable work-stealing spawn vs. local core-pinned spawn_local).
Telemetry and Deterministic Testing
Requires --cfg tokio_unstable and tracing macros for tokio-console, plus separate turmoil networking mocks for simulation.
Zero-code lexical handler interposition powers both vesper top live diagnostics and SimPulse deterministic simulation (vesper test --sim).
12.2 The Pulse Lexical Capability Hierarchy and Compile-Time Cancel Safety
Rather than exposing ambient global functions (tokio::spawn, tokio::time::sleep, TcpStream::connect), Pulse factors concurrency and I/O into granular lexical capabilities distinguished by their resumption contractq ∈ {affine, linear}:
// 1. Cooperative fiber scheduling: linear effect (q = linear) MUST resume exactly once.
linear effect Async {
    fn yield_now() -> Unit;
}
// 2. Explicit cancellation and deadline checkpoints: plain effect (q = affine) MAY abort.
effect Cancel {
    fn check() -> Unit;
}
// 3. Monotonic timers: sleep_ns is uncancellable linear; deadlines compose with Cancel.
linear effect Clock {
    fn now_ns() -> u64;
    fn sleep_ns(duration_ns: u64) -> Unit;
}
// 4. Completion-based network I/O (io_uring / kqueue / IOCP): linear effect (q = linear).
linear effect Net {
    fn accept(in listener: TcpListener) -> Result[TcpStream, IoError];
    fn read_some(inout stream: TcpStream, inout buf: ByteSlice) -> Result[u32, IoError];
    fn write_all(inout stream: TcpStream, in buf: ByteSlice) -> Result[Unit, IoError];
}
// 5. Structured concurrency nursery tied lexically to the enclosing scope.
linear effect Scope {
    fn spawn_scoped[T: mod copy](
        f: fn@linear() -> T eff [Async, Cancel]
    ) -> JoinHandle[T];
}
How [T-Let] Eliminates tokio::select! Cancel-Safety Bugs Without New Typing Rules
In Tokio, cancelling a branch inside tokio::select! or tokio::time::timeout drops the suspended Future at whichever .await point happens to be active. In Vesper Pulse, compile-time cancel safety is a direct theorem of Section 5's [T-Let] Resumption Frame Condition (∀ z ∈ Γ_2, m_z ⊑ res(ε_1)):
Uncancellable Linear I/O Suspensions (q = linear): Because Net, Storage, Clock, and Async are linear effects, res(ε) = linear. A fiber holding a live linear protocol obligation—such as WriteTxn, a half-written length-prefixed FrameWriter, or an in-flight io_uring SQE—freely suspends across Storage::flush_wal or Net::write_all because linear ⊑ linear holds.
Explicit Cancellation Checkpoints (q = affine): Cancellation can unwind a fiber only at operations carrying Cancel in their effect row (Cancel::check(), Pulse::race, Pulse::timeout, or chan.recv_cancel()), where res(Cancel) = affine.
Static Rejection of Orphaned Obligations: If a developer invokes a Cancel-bearing operation or enters Pulse::race while a WriteTxn or FrameWriter is live and unconsumed, [T-Let] evaluates linear ⊑ affine → FALSE. Compilation aborts with a diagnostic pinpointing the exact linear value that would be corrupted upon cancellation.
12.3 Completion-Based io_uring DMA Borrowing and Intra-Fiber Pipelining
Vesper Pulse resolves the longstanding impedance mismatch between Rust borrows and completion-based kernel rings (io_uring on Linux, IOCP on Windows):
Zero-Copy in / inout Stack and Field-Slice DMA:When a fiber performs Net::read_some(inout stream, inout scratch_buf), [T-Handle] binds the captured fiber continuation k inside the reactor at mode linear. Because the reactor is statically forbidden from dropping k before the kernel posts the corresponding CQE, k's suspended stack frame and caller inout projections remain live and unaliased throughout DMA—requiring zero heap buffer ownership transfers ((Result, Vec)) and zero reference counting.
Explicit Intra-Fiber SQE Pipelining (linear struct InFlightRead[B]):When a single fiber pipelines multiple concurrent SQEs onto an io_uring submission ring before awaiting completions, Pulse reifies each kernel submission as a linear token:
linear struct InFlightRead[B] {
    ring_id: u32,
    sqe_user_data: u64,
    pinned_buf: B,
}
impl[B] InFlightRead[B] {
    // Normal completion: awaits CQE arrival and returns buffer ownership alongside byte count.
    fn await_cqe(sink self) -> (Result[u32, IoError], B) eff [Storage] { ... }
    // Safe cancellation: submits IORING_OP_ASYNC_CANCEL and suspends linearly until the kernel
    // confirms via CQE that DMA into `pinned_buf` has ceased before returning `B`.
    fn cancel_and_reap(sink self) -> B eff [Storage] { ... }
}
12.4 Structured Concurrency, Disjoint Place Projections, and Linear Channels
Pulse replaces detached 'static task spawning with structured nurseries (Pulse::scope), linear join handles, and linear channels:
Zero-Arc Fan-Out Over Disjoint Field Trees:JoinHandle[T] is a linear struct whose obligation is discharged only by .join(). Because every child fiber spawned in Pulse::scope is guaranteed to complete before the scope exits, [T-CallProj] permits sibling fibers to mutably borrow disjoint projections of the parent stack (inout broker.repl_stream in one fiber, sink tx in a second fiber, and shared in config across both) with zero 'a lifetime annotations and zero Arc or Mutex overhead.
Infallible LinearOneshot[T] Channels: Unlike tokio::sync::oneshot::Receiver, which forces callers to handle RecvError in case the sender dropped without sending, Pulse::linear_oneshot[T]() returns (LinearTx[T], LinearRx[T]) where LinearTx[T] has mode linear. Because the producer must call tx.send(v) (or tx.abort_with(err)), rx.recv() returns T directly with zero abandoned-channel failure modes.
Work-Stealing vs. Thread-Per-Core Shards:s.spawn(...) schedules portable fibers across a multi-core work-stealing deque (equipped with Tokio-style cache-hot LIFO slots), while s.spawn_local(...) pins !portable fibers to the current core's dedicated io_uring ring and registered buffer table.
// Concurrent WAL commit and quorum replication with zero lifetimes, zero Arc, and static cancel safety
fn replicate_and_commit(
    inout broker.{index, metrics, repl_stream}: Broker,
    in config: BrokerConfig,
    sink batch: RecordBatch,
) -> CommitReceipt eff [Scope, Storage, Net, Clock, Cancel, Fail[BrokerError]] {
    // Cancellation permitted BEFORE acquiring linear obligations (res(Cancel) = affine):
    Cancel::check()?;
    let wire_slice = batch.as_wire_slice();
    let tx: WriteTxn = Storage::begin_txn(config.max_batch_bytes);
    let (wal_res, repl_res) = Pulse::scope(|inout s| eff [Scope, Storage, Net, Clock] {
        // Child Fiber 1 consumes linear `tx` on the Storage io_uring handler:
        let wal_join: JoinHandle[Result[CommitReceipt, IoError]] = s.spawn(|| eff [Storage] {
            handle Fail[IoError] in {
                Ok(tx.commit())
            } with {
                return r => r,
                raise(e) => Err(e),
            }
        });
        // Child Fiber 2 mutates ONLY `broker.repl_stream` while sharing `in wire_slice`:
        let net_join: JoinHandle[Result[Unit, IoError]] = s.spawn(|| eff [Net, Clock] {
            Net::write_all(inout broker.repl_stream, in wire_slice)
        });
        // Both linear JoinHandles MUST be joined before scope exit:
        (wal_join.join(), net_join.join())
    });
    let receipt = wal_res.map_err(BrokerError::Wal)?;
    let _unit   = repl_res.map_err(BrokerError::Replication)?;
    broker.index.insert_in_place(sink batch);
    broker.metrics.batches_written += 1;
    receipt
}
12.5 Cranelift Stack-Switching Fiber ABI and Evidence-Passing Lowering
vesper-opt-egglog (Stage 4) and vesper-codegen-clif (Stage 5) compile Pulse capabilities without state-machine bloat using a three-tier lowering strategy:
Pinned Lexical Evidence Vector (r15 on x86_64 / x28 on AArch64):Every effectful Vesper function receives a hidden Cranelift vmctx register pointing to the active EvidenceVector—a cache-line-aligned array of (handler_fn_ptr, state_ptr, parent_sp) slots indexed by static lexical capability depth in Σ. Looking up Storage, Net, or Fail is a single O(1) base+offset load with zero dynamic hash-map search.
0 ns Tail-Resumptive Fast Path (egglog Fusion):When an effect operation completes synchronously (for example, Clock::now_ns(), Cancel::check() when uncancelled, or Net::read_some satisfied from an in-memory read buffer), egglog evidence-passing rewrite rules fuse the handler inline into a direct register check and branch—allocating zero continuations and performing zero stack switches.
One-Shot Pooled Fiber Stack Switching (vesper_fiber_yield / vesper_fiber_resume):When an io_uring SQE or contended channel must suspend:
Each spawned fiber runs on a pooled virtual-memory stack slab (64 KiB default virtual reservation with 4 KiB lazy physical page faults and a hardware guard page, pooled per worker core).
At suspension, vesper-codegen-clif emits a lightweight call to the naked assembly trampoline vesper_fiber_yield(ev_slot, op_tag, arg_ptr), which spills only callee-saved registers (rbx, rbp, r12–r15, rsp, rip on x86_64, or x19–x29, lr, sp, d8–d15 on AArch64—80 to 144 bytes total) into an intrusive FiberHeader at the top of the fiber stack, writes the FiberHeader pointer into sqe.user_data (as the linear continuation k), and swaps rsp/sp back to the worker thread's scheduler loop in ~4–6 ns.
By Section 6's Zero-Pin Stack Relocation Corollary, suspended stacks hold zero first-class self-referential &'a borrows across yield points, allowing pooled stacks to recycle or relocate cleanly.
12.6 SimPulse Deterministic Simulator and vesper top Live Telemetry
Because Async, Scope, Net, Storage, Clock, and Cancel are ordinary lexical effects rather than hardcoded OS syscalls, Pulse swaps runtime semantics purely by changing the top-level handle block:
SimPulse Deterministic Simulation Testing (vesper test --sim):Inspired by FoundationDB and Tokio's turmoil, SimPulse replaces the io_uring reactor with a single-threaded, PRNG-seeded deterministic interpreter over the exact same unmodified production code:
Virtual Monotonic Clock:Clock::sleep_ns advances simulated time instantaneously whenever all runnable fibers park, executing 24-hour consensus lease and timeout suites in milliseconds.
Adversarial Schedule Exploration (DPOR): Intercepts every Scope::spawn_scoped and Async::yield_now to explore fiber interleavings deterministically and reproduce failures from a single 64-bit --seed.
Network and Crash-Consistency Fault Injection: Simulates asymmetric network partitions, packet reordering (Net), and sudden power loss / unfsynced WAL sector drops (Storage), verifying that WriteTxn recovery invariants hold under arbitrary crash points.
Zero-Instrumentation vesper top (tokio-console Counterpart):Attaching vesper top (or setting VESPER_PULSE_TRACE=1) interposes a lock-free TelemetryPulse handler wrapper around the active EvidenceVector—requiring zero #[instrument] attributes and zero --cfg recompilation:
Streams per-fiber run-time vs. idle-time histograms, wake-to-poll scheduling latency, and active lexical effect call stacks over a shared-memory ring buffer.
Live Linear Obligation Inspector: Displays every live Mode::Linear resource (WriteTxn, InFlightRead, JoinHandle, LinearTx) currently parked on each suspended fiber stack alongside wait-for graph cycle detection—immediately pinpointing stalled protocol obligations in production.
End-to-End Integration With Loom, SemVer, and Lean 4:
Vesper Loom Confinement (Section 8): Packages with effects = [] in Loom.toml are statically incapable of spawning background fibers, sleeping, or opening sockets.
SemVer Substitutability (Section 9): Adding Cancel or Net to an async API violates [SV-EffRow] and is automatically flagged by vesper semver as Major-breaking.
Lean 4 Concurrency Proofs (Section 11):vesper-verify-lean lowers Pulse functions directly into EffM [ScopeEff, StorageEff, NetEff, CancelEff], allowing engineers to prove protocol safety and deadlock freedom against a pure Lean 4 nondeterministic scheduler model.
12.7 Rust Runtime Core (crates/vesper-pulse)
// crates/vesper-pulse/src/lib.rs
use std::collections::VecDeque;
use std::ptr::NonNull;
/// O(1) lexical capability evidence slot passed via Cranelift `vmctx` (`r15` / `x28`).
#[repr(C, align(64))]
pub struct EvidenceSlot {
    pub handler_fn: unsafe extern "C" fn(*mut u8, u32, *mut u8) -> u64,
    pub state_ptr: *mut u8,
    pub scheduler_sp: usize,
    pub scheduler_ip: usize,
}
/// Intrusive one-shot continuation header (`k :_q^own`) stored at the top of a pooled fiber stack.
#[repr(C, align(64))]
pub struct FiberHeader {
    pub saved_sp: usize,
    pub saved_ip: usize,
    pub fiber_id: u64,
    /// Bitmask/count of live `Mode::Linear` obligations for `vesper top` inspection & debug assertions.
    pub live_linear_obligations: u32,
    pub is_portable: bool,
    pub stack_base: NonNull<u8>,
    pub stack_len: usize,
}
/// Hybrid per-core shard scheduler combining a cache-hot LIFO slot with an io_uring CQE reactor.
pub struct PulseWorkerShard {
    pub core_id: u16,
    pub lifo_slot: Option<NonNull<FiberHeader>>,
    pub run_queue: VecDeque<NonNull<FiberHeader>>,
    pub simulated_clock_ns: Option<u64>, // `Some` under `SimPulse` (`vesper test --sim`), `None` in prod
}
impl PulseWorkerShard {
    /// Schedules a resumed linear continuation `k` harvested from an `io_uring` CQE or `LinearTx`.
    #[inline]
    pub fn wake_linear_continuation(&mut self, k_fiber: NonNull<FiberHeader>) {
        if let Some(prev) = self.lifo_slot.replace(k_fiber) {
            self.run_queue.push_back(prev);
        }
    }
    /// Deterministic `SimPulse` step: pops the next runnable fiber according to a seeded PRNG choice.
    pub fn step_deterministic(&mut self, prng_choice: usize) -> Option<NonNull<FiberHeader>> {
        if let Some(hot) = self.lifo_slot.take() {
            self.run_queue.push_front(hot);
        }
        if self.run_queue.is_empty() {
            return None;
        }
        let idx = prng_choice % self.run_queue.len();
        self.run_queue.remove(idx)
    }
}

13. Vesper Bridge: Zero-Copy FFI, Omniglot Isolation, and Declarative Sys-Specs
Systems languages succeed or fail by how safely and cheaply they cross foreign boundaries—linking kernel rings (liburing), C codecs (liblz4, libzstd), network stacks (rdma-core), and zero-copy wire formats (Apache Arrow IPC, Kafka RecordBatch segments). In Rust, unsafe extern "C" disables static safety checks wholesale: a foreign C call can return invalid bit patterns (such as 2u8 for bool), retain borrowed pointers past return, alias mutable references, or corrupt host stack frames.
Grounded in Building Bridges: Safe Interactions with Foreign Languages through Omniglot (Schuermann, Toubes, Potyondy, Pannuto, Milano, and Levy, OSDI 2025 Best Paper), SafeFFI (Braunsdorf et al., USENIX Security 2026), Rage Against the State Machine / Abacus (Potyondy et al., ASPLOS 2026), and formal zero-copy layout typing (zerocopy, 2024–2026), Vesper Bridge (@vesper-std/ffi, implemented in crates/vesper-ffi) achieves zero-serialization cross-language interoperability while preserving Vesper's modal linearity, disjoint borrowing, and Loom supply-chain confinement.
13.1 Architectural Improvements Over Rust FFI
Wire and DMA Struct Casting: Rust requires external zerocopy or bytemuck procedural macros or unsafe transmute, where implicit struct padding causes immediate undefined behavior. Vesper provides a built-in wire struct declaration enforcing zero implicit padding, explicit endian scalars (u32be, u64le), and 100% safe O(1) slice projection.
Foreign Return Validity: Rust asserts valid host types on faith across extern "C", so an invalid C bool, enum discriminant, or misaligned pointer triggers immediate undefined behavior. Vesper Bridge adopts Omniglot's ForeignVal[T] (?T) wrapper: foreign returns carry zero validity assumptions until promoted via constant-time validate().
Cross-Language Borrowing: Rust raw pointers (*const T, *mut T) lose borrow-checker tracking at the call boundary. Vesper's declarative .vsys specifications map nocapture C pointers directly onto [T-CallProj] in and inout slice projections.
Foreign Handle Lifecycle: Rust manual Drop wrappers ignore close/flush errors and permit mem::forget leaks. Vesper's linear extern type enforces exact-once C finalizer execution or protocol state transitions across 100% of control-flow paths.
Supply-Chain and Hardware Memory Isolation: Whereas any Rust crate can link C code via build.rs and corrupt process memory, Vesper gates foreign libraries via eff [Ffi[Lib]] in Loom.toml and supports 1.5 ns Hardware MPK/PKRU and Arm POE zero-copy sandboxing.
13.2 Zero-Copy wire struct Layouts and Endian-Safe Projections
To eliminate defensive memcpy calls and unsafe pointer casts when parsing network frames or kernel rings, Vesper introduces wire struct—a nominal layout-verified declaration kind (mod copy):
Zero Implicit Padding Invariant: The compiler computes exact byte offsets for every field. If natural alignment would introduce an uninitialized padding hole, compilation fails with a suggested explicit ZeroPad[N] field (deterministically zero-initialized on construction and ignored on comparison).
Total Bit-Validity (FromBytes and AsBytes): Every field of a wire struct must inhabit all bit patterns of its width: fixed-width integers, explicit-endian wire integers (u16be, u32be, u64be, i64be, u32le, u64le), fixed byte arrays (ByteArray[N]), or nested wire struct types. bool and sparse enum tags are rejected inside wire struct in favor of total WireFlag8 or WireTag16 wrappers.
Safe Zero-Copy Views (view_wire and split_wire_mut): Any ByteSlice or io_uring DMA buffer projects into in W or inout W via a single bounds and alignment check—compiling to a direct pointer offset in Cranelift CLIF with zero heap allocation:
// Kafka-compatible record batch header: big-endian wire fields, 0 implicit padding, 61 bytes exact.
wire struct KafkaBatchWireHeader {
    base_offset: i64be,
    batch_length: i32be,
    partition_leader_epoch: i32be,
    magic: u8,
    crc: u32be,
    attributes: u16be,
    last_offset_delta: i32be,
    base_timestamp: i64be,
    max_timestamp: i64be,
    producer_id: i64be,
    producer_epoch: i16be,
    base_sequence: i32be,
    records_count: i32be,
}
// 100% safe zero-copy header inspection and in-place CRC stamp—zero unsafe, zero memcpy!
fn stamp_batch_header(
    inout raw_frame: ByteSlice,
    computed_crc: u32,
) -> Result[i64, WireLayoutError] {
    let (inout hdr, in _payload) = raw_frame.split_wire_mut[KafkaBatchWireHeader]()?;
    hdr.crc = computed_crc.to_be();
    Ok(hdr.base_offset.to_native())
}
13.3 Declarative .vsys Bindings, ForeignVal[T], and Linear Foreign Handles
Rather than executing imperative build.rs scripts or bindgen macros, C and Rust ABI bindings live in declarative .vsys manifests lowered directly into Vesper Loom's Package Calculus (Section 8):
// sys/lz4.vsys — Declarative C ABI contract verified by Vesper Loom and Vesper Bridge
sys library LibLz4 (pkg_config = "liblz4 ^1.9.4", header = "lz4frame.h") {
    // Linear opaque foreign handle: cannot go out of scope without LZ4F_freeDecompressionContext!
    linear extern type Lz4DecompressCtx;
    fn LZ4F_createDecompressionContext(version: u32)
        -> ForeignVal[Option[Lz4DecompressCtx]];
    // `in src` and `inout dst` enforce [T-CallProj] Alias-XOR-Mutability at the FFI call site.
    // `nocapture` asserts C retains neither pointer after LZ4F_decompress returns.
    fn LZ4F_decompress(
        inout ctx: Lz4DecompressCtx,
        nocapture inout dst: CSlice[u8],
        nocapture in src: CSlice[u8],
    ) -> ForeignVal[usize];
    // Terminal `sink` consumer discharging the `linear` obligation on Lz4DecompressCtx.
    fn LZ4F_freeDecompressionContext(sink ctx: Lz4DecompressCtx) -> ForeignVal[usize];
}
Omniglot ForeignVal[T] Boundary Validation: Every value returned across a foreign ABI lands in ForeignVal[T], which assumes zero bit-validity invariants until explicitly checked via .validate_non_error() or .validate_enum().
Loom Supply-Chain Confinement: Importing sys/lz4.vsys synthesizes the lexical capability Ffi[LibLz4]. Any package calling LibLz4 must declare "Ffi[LibLz4]" in its Loom.toml capability_ceiling; pure packages (effects = []) are statically barred from linking or invoking foreign symbols.
13.4 Hardware-Isolated Zero-Copy Sandboxing (Intel MPK/PKRU and Arm POE)
To achieve Omniglot-grade spatial memory safety without copying multi-megabyte storage or network buffers across IPC boundaries, Vesper Bridge provides two handler tiers for handle Ffi[Lib]:
Direct Native Handler (unsafe handle Ffi[Lib] direct): Emits a raw Cranelift System V or AAPCS64 call instruction directly on the current fiber's 64 KiB virtual stack slab (~0 ns overhead). Reserved for kernel VDSO/liburing syscalls and formally verified libraries.
Hardware Domain-Isolated Zero-Copy Handler (handle Ffi[Lib] isolated(mpk)):
Uses Intel Memory Protection Keys (WRPKRU) on x86_64 and Arm Permission Overlay Extension (POR_EL0) on AArch64 to partition virtual memory into three hardware page-table domains within a single address space:
Domain 0 (Vesper Host): Fiber stacks (FiberHeader), evidence vectors, and private Vesper structs.
Domain 1 (Shared Zero-Copy I/O Arena): Registered ByteSlice, Arrow, and io_uring buffers passed as in (mapped read-only in C's key mask) or inout (mapped read-write in C's key mask).
Domain 2 (Foreign Sandbox Heap): Private allocator heap, stack, and globals belonging to Lib.
At the FFI trampoline, Cranelift emits a 1.5 ns unprivileged WRPKRU / MSR POR_EL0 instruction revoking C's read/write access to Domain 0, pivots rsp/sp to Domain 2's sandbox stack, invokes C directly on the zero-copy Domain 1 pointers, restores Domain 0 permissions on return, and validates ForeignVal[T]. Stray C writes into Vesper memory trap immediately in hardware with zero memcpy overhead.
14. Vesper Vector: Capability-Typed Portable and Scalable SIMD
Modern CPUs derive massive throughput gains in parsing, checksums, compression, and columnar analytics from wide and scalable vector units (SSE4.2, AVX2, AVX-512 / AVX10 on x86_64; NEON and SVE2 on AArch64; ratified RVV v1.0 on RISC-V). Yet SIMD in Rust remains ergonomics-hostile: architecture intrinsics require unsafe solely due to missing static CPU-feature tracking, #[target_feature] functions fail to inline cleanly across generic closures, portable Simd assumes fixed compile-time lane counts incompatible with length-agnostic SVE2 and RVV registers, and hand-rolled bit-shuffles lack formal equivalence proofs.
Drawing on VeriISLE (ASPLOS 2024), Guided Equality Saturation (Koehler et al., POPL 2024), Verified Equality Saturation to SIMD in Lean 4 (2026), and ratified RISC-V RVV 1.0 / Arm SVE2 predication semantics, Vesper Vector (@vesper-std/simd, implemented in crates/vesper-simd) makes portable fixed-width and length-agnostic scalable SIMD 100% safe without unsafe, effect-polymorphic, equality-saturated via egglog, and bit-blast verifiable in Lean 4.
14.1 Architectural Improvements Over Rust SIMD
100% Safe CPU Feature Tracking: Instead of marking pure register math unsafe because missing target features trigger SIGILL, Vesper models CPU ISA extensions (Baseline128, Avx2, Avx512, Sve2, Rvv1) as zero-cost copy lexical capabilities eff [Isa[P]] proven once at Isa::dispatch.
Effect-Polymorphic Higher-Order Inlining: Generic combinators carrying eff [Isa[P] | r] inline and monomorphize target-specialized closures cleanly without dynamic function-pointer barriers.
Unified Fixed and Scalable Vector Registers: Supports both fixed-width Vec[T, N] (128/256/512-bit) and length-agnostic predicated VVec[P, T] with hardware active-lane masks (whilelo on SVE2, vsetvli on RVV 1.0, k1 opmasks on AVX-512)—eliminating scalar remainder loops.
Zero Aliasing Bailouts: [T-CallProj] statically guarantees inout dst and in src slices never overlap, freeing Cranelift and egglog from emitting runtime pointer-overlap fallback branches.
SAT Bit-Blasted Verification: vesper-verify-lean lowers SIMD lanes into BitVec and proves exact bit-for-bit equivalence against scalar reference functions via Lean 4's bv_decide tactic.
14.2 ISA Profiles as Zero-Cost Lexical Capabilities (eff [Isa[P]])
In Vesper, hardware instruction-set availability is a stateless copy lexical capability Isa[P] tracked in Σ:
// Built-in ISA capability hierarchy:
//   x86_64:  Baseline128 -> Avx2 -> Avx512
//   AArch64: Baseline128 (Neon) -> Sve2
//   RISC-V:  Scalar -> Rvv1
copy effect Isa[P] {}
// Runtime multiversion dispatcher: probes CPUID / HWCAP once at startup or boundary and
// dispatches to target-specialized machine code compiled for each supported profile P.
fn dispatch_simd[R](
    kernel: [P: IsaProfile] fn(in cap: IsaCap[P]) -> R eff [Isa[P]]
) -> R;
Because Isa[P] has mode copy and carries zero runtime state, vesper-opt-egglog erases the capability token completely during monomorphization while stamping Cranelift target flags (enable_simd, has_avx512f, use_sve2, has_v) on the specialized codegen unit.
14.3 Unified Fixed-Width (Vec[T, N]) and Scalable Strip-Mined Predication (VVec)
Vesper Vector exposes two interoperable layers lowered through Cranelift SSA IR:
Fixed-Width Portable Vectors (copy struct Vec[T: SimdElem, N]):
Supports lane scalars u8, i8, u16, i16, u32, i32, u64, i64, f32, f64 at 128, 256, and 512 bits.
Provides total lane-wise arithmetic (wrapping_add, saturating_sub, mul_add), typed masks Mask[T, N], compile-time checked swizzle, horizontal reductions (reduce_add, reduce_xor, bitmask), and bounds-checked loads/stores on ByteSlice and AlignedSlice[T, 64].
Length-Agnostic Predicated Strip-Mining (VVec[P, T] and VMask[P, T]):
P::active_mask[T](offset, total_len) computes a hardware predicate mask covering min(VL_lanes, total_len - offset) active lanes.
VVec::load_masked(in slice, offset, mask, passthru) and VVec::store_masked(inout slice, offset, mask, vec) lower directly to fault-suppressing predicated instructions (whilelo + ld1b on Arm SVE2; vsetvli + vle8.v on RISC-V RVV 1.0; k1 opmask vmovdqu8 on AVX-512; unrolled vector body + synthesized masked tail on AVX2/NEON):
// Length-agnostic, 100% safe SIMD delimiter scanner and XOR checksum over a zero-copy ByteSlice.
// Runs at full hardware width across NEON, AVX2, AVX-512, SVE2, and RVV without `unsafe`!
fn scan_record_delimiters[P: IsaProfile](
    in data: ByteSlice,
    delim: u8,
) -> (u64, u8) eff [Isa[P]] {
    let needle = VVec::splat[P, u8](delim);
    let mut matches_count: u64 = 0;
    let mut xor_acc = VVec::splat[P, u8](0);
    let mut idx: usize = 0;
    let len = data.len();
    while idx != len {
        let mask: VMask[P, u8] = P::active_mask[u8](idx, len);
        let chunk = VVec::load_masked(in data, idx, mask, 0);
        let eq_mask = chunk.simd_eq(needle).and(mask);
        matches_count = matches_count.wrapping_add(eq_mask.count_ones() as u64);
        xor_acc = xor_acc.bit_xor(chunk); // inactive lanes zeroed by load_masked
        idx = idx.wrapping_add(P::lanes[u8](mask));
    }
    (matches_count, xor_acc.reduce_xor())
}
14.4 egglog Vectorization Saturation and Lean 4 bv_decide Equivalence Proofs
Stage 4 — egglog SIMD Equality Saturation (vesper-opt-egglog):
Rewrites fuse add(mul(a, b), c) -> fma(a, b, c), recognize widen-multiply-accumulate dot products (u8 -> u16 -> u32 lowered to vpdpbusd on x86 and udot on Arm), and collapse adjacent swizzle permutations into a single register shuffle.
Profile-parameterized cost extraction divides elementwise op costs by P::register_bits while penalizing cross-lane permutations, emitting Cranelift vector ops lowered via VeriISLE-verified instruction selection rules.
Stage 5 — Automated Bit-Accurate SIMD Proofs (vesper-verify-lean):
vesper-verify-lean translates Vec[T, N] to BitVec (width(T) * N) (and instantiates VVec parametrically across valid vscale multipliers 1, 2, 4, 8, 16), allowing Lean 4's built-in SAT bit-blaster (bv_decide) to verify branchless SIMD parsers and checksums automatically:
-- proofs/SimdDelimiterProof.lean
-- Proves via Lean 4 SAT bit-blasting (bv_decide) that 16-lane SIMD delimiter counting
-- is bit-for-bit identical to the scalar specification across all 2^136 inputs!
theorem simd_chunk_delim_eq_scalar (chunk : BitVec 128) (delim : UInt8) :
    Simd128.countMatchesU8 chunk delim = ScalarSpec.countMatches16 chunk delim := by
  bv_decide
14.5 Workspace Crates (crates/vesper-ffi and crates/vesper-simd)
Adding "crates/vesper-ffi" and "crates/vesper-simd" to the Section 10.1 Cargo workspace completes the native systems layer:
// crates/vesper-simd/src/lib.rs
use smol_str::SmolStr;
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum IsaProfileKind {
    Baseline128, // x86_64 SSE4.2 / AArch64 NEON
    Avx2,        // x86_64 256-bit AVX2 + BMI2 + FMA
    Avx512,      // x86_64 512-bit AVX-512F/BW/VL/VBMI + k-mask predication
    Sve2,        // AArch64 scalable 128..2048-bit SVE2 + whilelo predication
    Rvv1,        // RISC-V Vector v1.0 scalable vlenb + vsetvli strip-mining
}
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum VectorWidth {
    FixedBits(u16),
    Scalable { min_bits: u16, max_bits: u16 },
}
impl IsaProfileKind {
    pub fn cranelift_isa_flags(self) -> &'static [(&'static str, &'static str)] {
        match self {
            Self::Baseline128 => &[("enable_simd", "true")],
            Self::Avx2 => &[("enable_simd", "true"), ("has_avx2", "true"), ("has_fma", "true")],
            Self::Avx512 => &[
                ("enable_simd", "true"),
                ("has_avx512f", "true"),
                ("has_avx512bw", "true"),
                ("has_avx512vl", "true"),
            ],
            Self::Sve2 => &[("enable_simd", "true"), ("use_sve2", "true")],
            Self::Rvv1 => &[("enable_simd", "true"), ("has_v", "true")],
        }
    }
}
/// Verified wire struct layout metadata computed by crates/vesper-ffi.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct WireStructLayout {
    pub name: SmolStr,
    pub exact_byte_size: usize,
    pub alignment: usize,
    pub has_zero_implicit_padding: bool,
    pub all_fields_from_bytes: bool,
}
