22. Vesper Beacon: Zero-Allocation Distributed Observability, W3C TraceContext, and OTLP-Arrow Telemetry
Vesper Beacon (@vesper-obs/beacon, implemented in crates/vesper-beacon) is Vesper's zero-GC distributed tracing, structured logging, and high-cardinality metrics framework. Complementing Section 12.6's local vesper top flight recorder, Beacon replaces Rust's tracing, opentelemetry-rust, and metrics crates with a lexically scoped observability substrate that guarantees 100 percent span completion, propagates W3C TraceContext across fibers without wrapper futures, and streams zero-copy columnar OpenTelemetry-Arrow (OTAP) batches directly from per-core AoSoA ring buffers.
22.1 Architectural Improvements Over Rust Observability Stacks
1. Linear Span Completion and Mandatory Error Attribution (ActiveSpan): In Rust's tracing ecosystem, spans rely on synchronous Drop guards that cannot distinguish normal scope exit from early ? error unwinds without manual boilerplate, and require wrapping every async task in Instrumented<F> state machines. Beacon models ActiveSpan as a Mode::Linear obligation under Theorem 2: every control-flow path must explicitly discharge an open span via span.finish_ok(...) or span.finish_err(in err), statically guaranteeing balanced span trees and exact failure attribution.
2. Zero-Wrapper Fiber Context Propagation via EvidenceVector: Rather than mutating thread-local RefCell globals (which break across work-stealing fiber migrations) or inflating return types with instrumentation wrappers, Beacon stores the 24-byte SpanContext (trace_id_hi, trace_id_lo, span_id, and trace_flags) directly inside Vesper Pulse's pinned EvidenceVector (r15 on x86_64, x28 on AArch64, Section 12.5). Child fibers spawned inside Pulse::scope inherit parent trace context automatically in 1 register copy.
3. Columnar AoSoA Telemetry Rings and Zero-Copy OTLP-Arrow Export: Conventional OpenTelemetry SDKs allocate Vec<KeyValue> and boxed strings on the global heap per span or log event, causing tail-latency spikes under load. Beacon records spans, structured logs, and trace-linked exemplars straight into core-local, cache-aligned Vesper Strata tiled(16) struct (AoSoA, Section 15.5) ring buffers whose hot columns already match Apache Arrow IPC buffers for zero-serialization OTLP-Arrow network export.
4. 3-Nanosecond SIMD W3C traceparent Wire Codec and Wait-Free Shard Histograms: Pairing Vesper Bridge wire struct views (Section 13.2) with Vesper Vector eff [Isa[P]] nibble lookups (Section 14), Beacon parses and formats 55-byte W3C traceparent headers and Kafka trace headers in ~3 nanoseconds with zero unsafe. Latency metrics aggregate into core-local logarithmic sub-bucket histograms (ShardHistogram) mutated via disjoint inout shard.{telemetry} projections ([T-CallProj]) with zero atomic CAS contention.
5. Compile-Time PII Redaction and Lean 4 Non-Leakage Proofs: Beacon's colorless Telemetrable trait (Section 16 Vesper Pact) omits implementations for raw Secret[T] and @pii-annotated types unless wrapped in an explicit Redacted model, while vesper-verify-lean (Section 11) mechanically proves that sensitive credentials never flow into exported telemetry streams.
22.2 Lexical Trace Capability, Linear ActiveSpan, and SIMD W3C traceparent
Beacon declares Trace as a linear effect (q = linear), allowing live ActiveSpan obligations to survive across Storage, Net, and Clock suspensions while Section 5's [T-Let] Resumption Frame Condition prevents early ? unwinds from leaking an unclosed span:
// Exact 55-byte W3C TraceContext v00 wire header: "00-<32-hex-trace-id>-<16-hex-span-id>-<2-hex-flags>"
wire struct W3cTraceParentWire {
    version_hex: ByteArray[2],
    sep0: u8,
    trace_id_hex: ByteArray[32],
    sep1: u8,
    parent_span_hex: ByteArray[16],
    sep2: u8,
    flags_hex: ByteArray[2],
}
copy struct SpanContext {
    trace_id_hi: u64,
    trace_id_lo: u64,
    span_id: u64,
    flags: u8,
}
// Mode::Linear: compiler rejects scope exit or early ? unless finish_ok or finish_err is called!
linear struct ActiveSpan {
    ctx: SpanContext,
    parent_span_id: u64,
    start_mono_ns: u64,
    schema_id: u32,
}
linear effect Trace {
    fn current_ctx() -> SpanContext;
    fn start_span(schema_id: u32, name: StaticStr) -> ActiveSpan;
    fn emit_ok(sink span: ActiveSpan, attr_u64: u64) -> Unit;
    fn emit_err(sink span: ActiveSpan, err_code: u32, msg: StaticStr) -> Unit;
}
// 100% safe SIMD W3C traceparent parser over zero-copy HTTP or Kafka headers:
fn parse_w3c_traceparent[P: IsaProfile](
    in raw_hdr: ByteSlice,
) -> Result[SpanContext, TraceParseError] eff [Isa[P], Fail[TraceParseError]] {
    let in wire = raw_hdr.view_wire[W3cTraceParentWire]()
        .map_err(|_| TraceParseError::InvalidLength)?;
    if wire.sep0 != 0x2D || wire.sep1 != 0x2D || wire.sep2 != 0x2D {
        Fail::raise(TraceParseError::MissingHyphen);
    }
    // Branchless SIMD hex-to-nibble decode across all 52 hex digits in one vector pass:
    let (trace_hi, trace_lo, parent_id, flags) = Isa::decode_w3c_hex_52[P](in wire)?;
    if (trace_hi | trace_lo) == 0 || parent_id == 0 {
        Fail::raise(TraceParseError::AllZeroIdentifier);
    }
    Ok(SpanContext { trace_id_hi: trace_hi, trace_id_lo: trace_lo, span_id: parent_id, flags })
}
22.3 Columnar AoSoA Ring Buffers and Zero-Copy OTLP-Arrow (OTAP) Export
Instead of heap-allocating heterogeneous attribute maps, Vesper Forge's WASI @span_schema macro assigns each call site a compile-time schema_id and writes fixed-width columns directly into a core-local 16-wide AoSoA ring buffer mapped in Hardware Domain 1 (DmaSlab, Section 15.3):
// 16-span cache-aligned AoSoA tile: hot timing and trace columns match OTLP-Arrow column buffers!
tiled(16) copy struct SpanTileRow {
    hot trace_id_hi: u64,
    hot trace_id_lo: u64,
    hot span_id: u64,
    hot parent_span_id: u64,
    hot start_unix_ns: u64,
    hot duration_ns: u64,
    hot schema_and_status: u64,
    hot primary_attr_u64: u64,
    cold error_summary: InlineStr[64],
}
// Zero-Global-Heap instrumented broker append (`Alloc` is omitted from `eff [...]`!):
fn append_instrumented_batch(
    inout broker.{index, telemetry}: BrokerShard,
    in raw_traceparent: ByteSlice,
    sink batch: RecordBatch,
) -> Result[CommitReceipt, WalError] eff [Trace, Storage, Clock, Isa[Avx2], Fail[WalError]] {
    let parent_ctx = parse_w3c_traceparent[Avx2](in raw_traceparent)
        .unwrap_or_else(|_| Trace::current_ctx());
    handle Trace with_parent(parent_ctx) in {
        let span: ActiveSpan = Trace::start_span(0x1042, "krabka.wal.append");
        // Note: An unguarded `?` here is statically rejected by `[T-Let]` because `span` is linear!
        match write_and_fsync_batch(inout broker.index, sink batch) {
            Ok(receipt) => {
                let elapsed_ns = Clock::now_ns().wrapping_sub(span.start_mono_ns);
                broker.telemetry.append_latency.record_exemplar(elapsed_ns, span.ctx.span_id);
                Trace::emit_ok(sink span, receipt.wal_slot);
                Ok(receipt)
            }
            Err(wal_err) => {
                broker.telemetry.append_errors += 1;
                Trace::emit_err(sink span, wal_err.code(), "wal_fsync_failed");
                Fail::raise(wal_err)
            }
        }
    }
}
When a 16-row SpanTileRow block fills on a worker shard, a background Vesper Pulse io_uring fiber flushes the contiguous column pages directly into an OTLP-Arrow gRPC/HTTP2 frame (IORING_OP_SEND_ZC) without touching global heap memory or stalling foreground request fibers.
22.4 Compile-Time PII Redaction and Lean 4 Non-Interference Proofs
To eliminate accidental credential leakage into logs and span attributes (a frequent production vulnerability in Rust #[derive(Debug)] logging), Beacon separates structural formatting from Telemetrable:
// Sealed wrapper whose canonical `Debug` or `Telemetrable` implementation is intentionally absent!
affine struct Secret[T] {
    inner: T,
}
// Named Vesper Pact model permitting safe, Fingerprinted telemetry emission when explicitly opted in:
pub model RedactedTokenTelemetry: Telemetrable for Secret[BearerToken] {
    fn encode_attr(in self, inout out: AttrWriter) eff [] {
        out.write_static_str("[REDACTED:sha256_prefix]");
    }
}
In vesper-verify-lean (Section 11), vesper verify extracts instrumented handlers and proves both Exact Span Conservation and Secret Non-Interference (changing a secret BearerToken input never alters any byte of the emitted TraceOp stream):
-- proofs/BeaconPrivacySafety.lean
theorem ingest_telemetry_secret_noninterference
    (tok1 tok2 : Secret BearerToken) (req : PublicReqMeta) (st : RefBrokerState) :
    EmittedTraceOps (runInstrumented st tok1 req) =
    EmittedTraceOps (runInstrumented st tok2 req) := by
  rfl
22.5 Rust Host Implementation (crates/vesper-beacon)
Adding crates/vesper-beacon to the workspace provides the branchless W3C traceparent codec and wait-free logarithmic sub-bucket latency histogram (<2% relative error across nanosecond-to-hour ranges in 2 KiB of L1 cache):
// crates/vesper-beacon/src/lib.rs
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct W3cSpanContext {
    pub trace_id: u128,
    pub span_id: u64,
    pub trace_flags: u8,
}
#[inline]
const fn decode_hex_byte(b: u8) -> u8 {
    match b {
        b'0'..=b'9' => b - b'0',
        b'a'..=b'f' => b - b'a' + 10,
        _ => 0xFF, // W3C TraceContext mandates lowercase hex
    }
}
/// Zero-allocation W3C `traceparent` v00 parser (`00-<32hex>-<16hex>-<2hex>`).
pub fn parse_traceparent_55(raw: &[u8]) -> Option<W3cSpanContext> {
    if raw.len() != 55 || &raw[0..3] != b"00-" || raw[35] != b'-' || raw[52] != b'-' {
        return None;
    }
    let mut trace_id: u128 = 0;
    for &b in &raw[3..35] {
        let nib = decode_hex_byte(b);
        if nib == 0xFF {
            return None;
        }
        trace_id = (trace_id << 4) | u128::from(nib);
    }
    let mut span_id: u64 = 0;
    for &b in &raw[36..52] {
        let nib = decode_hex_byte(b);
        if nib == 0xFF {
            return None;
        }
        span_id = (span_id << 4) | u64::from(nib);
    }
    let hi = decode_hex_byte(raw[53]);
    let lo = decode_hex_byte(raw[54]);
    if hi == 0xFF || lo == 0xFF || trace_id == 0 || span_id == 0 {
        return None;
    }
    Some(W3cSpanContext {
        trace_id,
        span_id,
        trace_flags: (hi << 4) | lo,
    })
}
/// Core-local wait-free logarithmic histogram (4-bit linear sub-buckets per power-of-2 octave,
/// 256 buckets = 2 KiB L1-resident array, zero atomics via Vesper `inout shard.telemetry`).
pub struct FastLogHistogram {
    pub buckets: [u64; 256],
    pub exemplar_span_id: [u64; 256],
    pub total_count: u64,
}
impl Default for FastLogHistogram {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            buckets: [0; 256],
            exemplar_span_id: [0; 256],
            total_count: 0,
        }
    }
}
impl FastLogHistogram {
    #[inline(always)]
    pub fn bucket_index(val_ns: u64) -> usize {
        if val_ns < 16 {
            return val_ns as usize;
        }
        let lz = val_ns.leading_zeros() as usize;
        let octave = 63usize.saturating_sub(lz);
        let shift = octave.saturating_sub(3);
        let sub = ((val_ns >> shift) & 0b0111) as usize;
        let idx = ((octave - 3) << 3) + sub;
        idx.min(255)
    }
    #[inline(always)]
    pub fn record_with_exemplar(&mut self, duration_ns: u64, span_id: u64) {
        let idx = Self::bucket_index(duration_ns);
        self.buckets[idx] += 1;
        self.exemplar_span_id[idx] = span_id;
        self.total_count += 1;
    }
}

23. Vesper Vault: Constant-Time Cryptography, Hardware-Fenced Zeroization, and Nonce-Safe AEAD
Vesper Vault (@vesper-crypto/vault, implemented in crates/vesper-vault) is Vesper's zero-GC, side-channel-resistant cryptography and secret-hygiene framework. Serving as Vesper's native counterpart to Rust's ring, RustCrypto, rustls, subtle, and zeroize ecosystems—and complementing Section 17.1's MPK-isolated foreign TLS bridge—Vault uses modal linearity, compiler-verified constant-time SSA taint checking, hardware Data-Independent Timing (DIT and DOITM), and Lean 4 bit-blasting to make nonce reuse, secret stack remnants, timing side channels, and unauthenticated plaintext reads compile-time errors.
23.1 Architectural Improvements Over Rust Cryptography Stacks
1. Compile-Time Nonce-Reuse Elimination (NonceTicket[Alg]): In Rust AEAD crates (aes-gcm, chacha20poly1305), nonces are Copy byte slices (&Nonce), so nothing in the type system prevents catastrophic (Key, Nonce) reuse across retries, cloned sessions, or fiber forks. Vault models every encryption nonce as a Mode::Linear obligation (linear struct NonceTicket[Alg]) minted exclusively by mutating a stateful monotonic AeadSealer[Alg] (inout sealer), statically guaranteeing under Theorem 2 that each nonce is consumed at most and at least once (or explicitly burned).
2. Guaranteed Zero-Move Secret Zeroization (SecretSlab and Vault::shred): Rust's ZeroizeOnDrop can be bypassed via safe mem::forget, leaves unzeroed bitwise copies on stack frames across struct moves or Future state transitions, and relies on best-effort volatile writes. Vault allocates long-lived keys inside non-swappable, core-dump-excluded pages (mlock plus MADV_DONTDUMP) via linear struct SecretSlot[K]. Because SecretSlot[K] is Mode::Linear and accessed strictly via second-class in projections ([T-CallProj]), secret bytes never move by value across fiber stacks and cannot go out of scope without calling Vault::shred(sink key), which emits volatile zero stores, hardware memory barriers, and scratch-register scrubbing.
3. Backend-Verified Constant-Time Execution (eff [ConstTime] and Hardware DIT): Rust's subtle library (Choice) cannot stop LLVM backend passes from converting branchless selects (cmov) back into secret-dependent conditional branches, nor does it enable CPU Data-Independent Timing flags automatically. Vault introduces opaque secret scalars (SecU8, SecU32, SecU64, SecVec[P, T]) paired with copy effect ConstTime. Entering ConstTime::scope enables Armv8.4+ PSTATE.DIT (MSR DIT, #1) and x86_64 DOITM hardware constant-time execution modes, while Stage 5 (crates/vesper-vault) runs a mandatory Cranelift SSA taint verifier that rejects any secret-tainted branch (brif, br_table), secret-indexed memory access, or variable-latency division (udiv, sdiv).
4. Unauthenticated Plaintext Quarantine and Post-Quantum Hybrid KEM: Decryption in Vault follows the cryptographic Doom Principle by construction: open_in_place consumes a linear struct CiphertextFrame and returns Result[VerifiedPlaintext, AuthError], zeroizing the buffer in place on Poly1305 or GHASH tag mismatch before returning Err so unauthenticated plaintext is never addressable. Key establishment ships zero-allocation hybrid post-quantum X25519MLKEM768 (FIPS 203 ML-KEM-768 paired with X25519) using Vesper Vector eff [Isa[P]] polynomial NTT acceleration without unsafe.
5. HACL*-Grade Lean 4 Equivalence and Leakage Proofs (vesper verify): vesper-verify-lean (Section 11) extracts Vault primitives directly into Lean 4 BitVec definitions, proving functional equivalence against mathematical specifications (bv_decide) alongside trace-equivalence theorems guaranteeing branch and memory-address independence from secret inputs.
23.2 Secret Scalars, Hardware DIT ConstTime Scope, and Fenced Zeroization
Secret scalars (SecU8, SecU64) intentionally omit Eq, Ord, /, %, Telemetrable (Section 22.4), and Encode (Section 20.2). Comparisons return an opaque SecMask64 (0 or 0xFFFF_FFFF_FFFF_FFFF) usable only in constant-time bitwise selects (ct_select), and declassification requires an explicit auditable declassify_bool gate inside eff [ConstTime]:
// Opaque constant-time scalars: forbidden in `if`, `match`, slice indexing, or `udiv`!
copy struct SecU8(u8);
copy struct SecU64(u64);
copy struct SecMask64(u64);
// Stateless lexical capability activating hardware DIT/DOITM and Cranelift CT verification:
copy effect ConstTime {}
// Long-lived AEAD key pinned in non-swappable mlock + MADV_DONTDUMP memory:
linear struct AeadKeySlot[Alg] {
    slot_id: u32,
}
impl[Alg] AeadKeySlot[Alg] {
    // Terminal consumer required by Theorem 2: volatile zero-fill + SFENCE/DSB + register scrub!
    fn shred(sink self, inout slab: SecretSlab) -> Unit eff [ConstTime] {
        let AeadKeySlot { slot_id } = self;
        slab.volatile_zero_and_fence(slot_id);
    }
}
// 100% safe, branchless 16-byte authentication tag comparison verified by Cranelift CT pass:
fn verify_tag_16_ct(
    in expected: ByteArray[16],
    in computed: ByteArray[16],
) -> bool eff [ConstTime] {
    let diff_lo: SecU64 = expected.load_sec_u64le(0).bit_xor(computed.load_sec_u64le(0));
    let diff_hi: SecU64 = expected.load_sec_u64le(8).bit_xor(computed.load_sec_u64le(8));
    let combined: SecU64 = diff_lo.bit_or(diff_hi);
    // Explicit constant-time zero test -> single declassification boundary at tag verdict:
    combined.ct_is_zero().declassify_bool()
}
23.3 Linear NonceTicket AEAD Sealer and Post-Quantum X25519MLKEM768 Session
To eliminate nonce reuse and key-schedule recomputation, AeadSealer owns the monotonically advancing 96-bit counter and mints a single-use NonceTicket per record. If the 64-bit sequence space approaches exhaustion (u64::MAX - 1), next_nonce forces a key rotation (RekeyRequired) before wrap-around can ever occur:
// Wire header for encrypted WAL or RPC frames (28 bytes exact, 0 implicit padding):
wire struct EncryptedFrameWireHeader {
    key_epoch: u32be,
    nonce_seq: u64be,
    tag: ByteArray[16],
}
// Mode::Linear: can seal EXACTLY ONE frame (or be explicitly burned on pre-send abort)!
linear struct NonceTicket[Alg] {
    epoch: u32,
    seq: u64,
}
linear struct AeadSealer[Alg] {
    key_slot: AeadKeySlot[Alg],
    epoch: u32,
    next_seq: u64,
}
impl[Alg: AeadCipher] AeadSealer[Alg] {
    // Exclusive `inout self` guarantees no two fibers can ever mint the same `seq`!
    fn mint_nonce(inout self) -> Result[NonceTicket[Alg], CryptoError] eff [Fail[CryptoError]] {
        if self.next_seq == 0xFFFF_FFFF_FFFF_FFFE {
            Fail::raise(CryptoError::EpochExhaustedRekeyRequired);
        }
        let seq = self.next_seq;
        self.next_seq += 1;
        Ok(NonceTicket { epoch: self.epoch, seq })
    }
    // Consumes `sink ticket` linearly: reusing a NonceTicket is a compile-time error!
    fn seal_in_place[P: IsaProfile](
        in self.{key_slot},
        in slab: SecretSlab,
        sink ticket: NonceTicket[Alg],
        in aad: ByteSlice,
        inout frame_buf: ByteSlice,
    ) -> Result[Unit, CryptoError] eff [ConstTime, Isa[P], Fail[CryptoError]] {
        let NonceTicket { epoch, seq } = ticket;
        let (inout hdr, inout payload) = match frame_buf.split_wire_mut[EncryptedFrameWireHeader]() {
            Ok(parts) => parts,
            Err(_)    => Fail::raise(CryptoError::BufferTooSmall),
        };
        hdr.key_epoch = epoch.to_be();
        hdr.nonce_seq = seq.to_be();
        let computed_tag = Alg::encrypt_detached_ct[P](
            in slab.borrow_key(in self.key_slot),
            epoch,
            seq,
            in aad,
            inout payload,
        );
        hdr.tag = computed_tag;
        Ok(())
    }
}
// Quarantine-safe decryption: zeroes buffer on tag failure so unauthenticated bytes never leak!
fn open_authenticated_frame[Alg: AeadCipher, P: IsaProfile](
    in key_slot: AeadKeySlot[Alg],
    in slab: SecretSlab,
    in aad: ByteSlice,
    sink raw_cipher: CiphertextSlice,
) -> Result[VerifiedPlaintextSlice, CryptoError] eff [ConstTime, Isa[P], Fail[CryptoError]] {
    let mut buf = raw_cipher.into_raw_mut();
    let (in hdr, inout payload) = buf.split_wire_mut[EncryptedFrameWireHeader]()
        .map_err(|_| CryptoError::MalformedHeader)?;
    let computed_tag = Alg::decrypt_tentative_ct[P](
        in slab.borrow_key(in key_slot),
        hdr.key_epoch.to_native(),
        hdr.nonce_seq.to_native(),
        in aad,
        inout payload,
    );
    if !verify_tag_16_ct(in hdr.tag, in computed_tag) {
        Vault::volatile_zero_slice(inout payload); // Scrubs tentative plaintext BEFORE returning Err!
        Fail::raise(CryptoError::AuthenticationTagMismatch);
    }
    Ok(VerifiedPlaintextSlice::from_authenticated(payload))
}
23.4 Lean 4 Constant-Time Leakage and Functional Proofs (vesper verify)
In vesper-verify-lean (Section 11), Vault kernels verify both Bit-Exact Cryptographic Soundness and Instruction/Address Trace Non-Interference (proving that execution control-flow and memory addresses LeakageTrace depend solely on public buffer lengths, never on secret keys or tags):
-- proofs/VaultConstantTimeSafety.lean
-- 1. SAT bit-blasting (`bv_decide`) proves branchless 128-bit tag verification is exact across all 2^256 inputs:
theorem verify_tag_16_ct_sound (expected computed : BitVec 128) :
    VaultSpec.verifyTag16Ct expected computed = decide (expected = computed) := by
  bv_decide
-- 2. Constant-time trace independence: secret keys `k1`, `k2` produce identical PC & memory-address traces!
theorem chacha20_seal_constant_time_trace
    (k1 k2 : BitVec 256) (nonce : BitVec 96) (plain1 plain2 : ByteArray)
    (h_len : plain1.size = plain2.size) :
    LeakageTrace (VaultSpec.chacha20Blocks k1 nonce plain1) =
    LeakageTrace (VaultSpec.chacha20Blocks k2 nonce plain2) := by
  simp [VaultSpec.chacha20Blocks, LeakageTrace, h_len]
23.5 Rust Cranelift Constant-Time SSA Taint Verifier (crates/vesper-vault)
Adding crates/vesper-vault to the workspace provides the Stage 5 SSA taint analyzer invoked on every function carrying eff [ConstTime], statically rejecting secret-dependent branches, variable-index table lookups, and variable-time hardware divisions prior to machine-code emission:
// crates/vesper-vault/src/lib.rs
use rustc_hash::FxHashSet;
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub struct SsaVal(pub u32);
/// Abstracted Cranelift SSA instruction audited by the `eff [ConstTime]` backend pass.
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub enum CtAuditedInst {
    PureAlu { dst: SsaVal, inputs: Vec<SsaVal> },
    BitwiseSelect { dst: SsaVal, mask: SsaVal, on_true: SsaVal, on_false: SsaVal },
    ExplicitDeclassify { dst: SsaVal, src: SsaVal },
    Load { dst: SsaVal, addr: SsaVal },
    Store { addr: SsaVal, val: SsaVal },
    VariableLatencyDiv { dst: SsaVal, num: SsaVal, den: SsaVal },
    CondBranch { cond: SsaVal, inst_span_offset: u32 },
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ConstantTimeViolation {
    SecretDependentBranch { cond: SsaVal, inst_span_offset: u32 },
    SecretDependentMemoryAddress { addr: SsaVal },
    SecretTaintedVariableLatencyDiv { operand: SsaVal },
}
/// Forward SSA taint verifier enforcing strict constant-time discipline on `Sec*` values.
pub fn verify_constant_time_ssa(
    initial_secret_vals: &[SsaVal],
    insts: &[CtAuditedInst],
) -> Result<(), ConstantTimeViolation> {
    let mut tainted: FxHashSet<SsaVal> = initial_secret_vals.iter().copied().collect();
    for inst in insts {
        match inst {
            CtAuditedInst::PureAlu { dst, inputs } => {
                if inputs.iter().any(|v| tainted.contains(v)) {
                    tainted.insert(*dst);
                }
            }
            CtAuditedInst::BitwiseSelect { dst, mask, on_true, on_false } => {
                if tainted.contains(mask) || tainted.contains(on_true) || tainted.contains(on_false) {
                    tainted.insert(*dst);
                }
            }
            CtAuditedInst::ExplicitDeclassify { dst, .. } => {
                // Audited explicit declassification boundary (e.g., final AEAD tag boolean):
                tainted.remove(dst);
            }
            CtAuditedInst::Load { dst, addr } => {
                if tainted.contains(addr) {
                    return Err(ConstantTimeViolation::SecretDependentMemoryAddress { addr: *addr });
                }
                // Loaded data from a public address stays untainted unless explicitly marked secret
                let _ = dst;
            }
            CtAuditedInst::Store { addr, val } => {
                if tainted.contains(addr) {
                    return Err(ConstantTimeViolation::SecretDependentMemoryAddress { addr: *addr });
                }
                let _ = val;
            }
            CtAuditedInst::VariableLatencyDiv { dst, num, den } => {
                if tainted.contains(num) {
                    return Err(ConstantTimeViolation::SecretTaintedVariableLatencyDiv { operand: *num });
                }
                if tainted.contains(den) {
                    return Err(ConstantTimeViolation::SecretTaintedVariableLatencyDiv { operand: *den });
                }
                let _ = dst;
            }
            CtAuditedInst::CondBranch { cond, inst_span_offset } => {
                if tainted.contains(cond) {
                    return Err(ConstantTimeViolation::SecretDependentBranch {
                        cond: *cond,
                        inst_span_offset: *inst_span_offset,
                    });
                }
            }
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

24. Vesper Quarry: Hermetic Compile-Time SQL, Linear Buffer-Pool Pins, and Vectorized AoSoA Execution
 (@vesper-db/quarry, implemented in crates/vesper-quarry) is Vesper's zero-GC embedded transactional storage engine, vectorized analytical query processor, and compile-time-verified SQL framework. Providing the concrete engine behind Section 2's WriteTxn and Section 17's TenantDb capabilities—and replacing Rust's sqlx, diesel, redb, rocksdb, and duckdb-rs stacks—Quarry unifies hermetic WASI SQL typechecking, zero-refcount linear buffer-pool page management over io_uringO_DIRECT DMA, egglog-optimized relational plans, and predicated SIMD execution over AoSoA cache tiles.
24.1 Architectural Improvements Over Rust Database and SQL Stacks
1. Zero-Socket Hermetic Compile-Time SQL Verification (@sql): In Rust, sqlx::query! requires a live network database socket (DATABASE_URL) during compilation or brittle checked-in JSON metadata files, breaking reproducible offline builds. Quarry's @sql(...) macro executes inside Vesper Forge's fuel-metered wasm32-wasip2 Wasmtime sandbox (macro_emits_effects = [], Sections 8.2 and 16.4), replaying declarative migrations/*.sql DDL files into an in-memory catalog to verify SQL syntax, join nullability propagation, bind-parameter types, and output AoSoA tile schemas in under 100 microseconds with zero network or ambient filesystem authority.
2. Zero-Atomic Linear Buffer-Pool Page Pinning (PagePin[M]): Conventional C++ and Rust storage engines (SQLite, RocksDB, redb, LeanStore) guard cached 4 KiB and 16 KiB B+Tree pages with atomic pin counts (Arc or AtomicU32), incurring multi-core cache-line contention on root and internal pages while risking silent buffer-pool exhaustion if an error path forgets to unpin. Quarry combines optimistic lock-coupling (PageEpoch) for latch-free internal traversal with linear struct PagePin[Access] (Shared or Exclusive) for leaf mutations and scans. Under Theorem 2 and [T-Let], every pinned page frame must be explicitly released via pool.unpin(sink pin) or pool.unpin_dirty(sink pin, wal_lsn) on 100 percent of control-flow paths.
3. Direct io_uringO_DIRECT Slotted Pages and wire struct Headers: Pairing Vesper Pulse linear effect Storage (IORING_OP_READ_FIXED and IORING_OP_WRITE_FIXED over Domain-1 2 MiB hugepages, Sections 12.3 and 15.3) with Vesper Bridge padding-free wire struct layouts (Section 13.2), Quarry maps NVMe 16 KiB slotted B+Tree and PAX columnar pages directly in place with zero memcpy serialization and hardware-accelerated CRC32C page verification.
4. egglog Relational Optimization and Vectorized AoSoA Morsel Execution: Rather than materializing row-oriented Vec<Row> heap allocations via volcano iterators, Quarry lowers SQL ASTs into algebraic relational plans saturated by Stage 4 egglog rewrite rules (predicate pushdown, join reordering, projection pruning) and executes vectorized morsels directly into 16-wide Vesper Strata tiled(16) struct (AoSoA, Section 15.5) L1 cache tiles using Vesper Vector eff [Isa[P]] SIMD predicates (Alloc omitted from eff [...]).
5. Lean 4 ARIES Crash-Consistency and Multi-Tenant Isolation Proofs (vesper verify):vesper-verify-lean (Section 11) extracts Quarry's MVCC snapshot visibility rules, ARIES write-ahead log physiological redo invariants, and @sql row-level security predicates into Lean 4, proving both zero leaked page pins and strict tenant data isolation.
24.2 Slotted wire struct Pages and Linear Buffer-Pool Pin Protocol
Every 16 KiB storage page begins with a 32-byte padding-free wire struct header validated in place upon io_uring DMA completion. Acquiring a leaf page from BufferPool yields a Mode::LinearPagePin that statically forbids early ? unwinds ([T-Let]) until the frame slot is returned to the pool:
// Exact 32-byte slotted B+Tree / PAX page header (0 implicit padding, Section 13.2):
wire struct QuarryPageWireHeader {
    page_lsn: u64le,
    page_id: u64le,
    checksum_crc32c: u32le,
    slot_count: u16le,
    free_space_offset: u16le,
    page_kind_tag: u8,
    level: u8,
    flags: u16le,
    right_sibling_page: u32le,
}
// Phantom access tags for shared reader vs. exclusive writer latches:
copy struct SharedPin {}
copy struct ExclusivePin {}
// Mode::Linear: forgetting to unpin a buffer-pool frame is a compile-time error!
linear struct PagePin[Access] {
    frame_slot: u32,
    page_id: u64,
}
linear effect BufferPool {
    fn fix_shared(page_id: u64) -> Result[PagePin[SharedPin], DbError];
    fn fix_exclusive(page_id: u64) -> Result[PagePin[ExclusivePin], DbError];
    fn view_page(in pin: PagePin[SharedPin]) -> ByteSlice;
    fn mutate_page(inout pin: PagePin[ExclusivePin]) -> ByteSliceMut;
    fn unpin_clean(sink pin: PagePin[SharedPin]) -> Unit;
    fn unpin_dirty(sink pin: PagePin[ExclusivePin], rec_lsn: u64) -> Unit;
    fn unpin_aborted(sink pin: PagePin[ExclusivePin]) -> Unit;
}
24.3 Hermetic @sql Queries and Vectorized AoSoA SIMD Execution
When @sql(...) expands inside vesper-loom-sandbox, it validates column types against migrations/*.sql and emits a vectorized morsel operator that streams matching tuples straight into a TiledSeq inside a bounded lexical Region scratchpad (Alloc is absent from eff [...]):
// Cache-conscious 16-row AoSoA output tile verified against `migrations/001_orders.sql`:
tiled(16) copy struct TenantOrderSummary {
    hot order_id: u64,
    hot org_id: u64,
    hot total_cents: i64,
    hot created_epoch_s: u64,
    cold customer_ref: InlineStr[32],
}
// Predicated SIMD PAX page filter + aggregation (`0` global heap allocations, `0` unsafe!):
fn scan_high_value_orders[P: IsaProfile, r: Region](
    in arena: RegionCap[r],
    target_org: u64,
    min_cents: i64,
    leaf_page_ids: Slice[u64],
    inout out_tiles: TiledSeq[TenantOrderSummary],
) -> Result[i64, DbError] eff [r: Region, BufferPool, Isa[P], Fail[DbError]] {
    let v_org = VVec::splat[P, u64](target_org);
    let v_min = VVec::splat[P, i64](min_cents);
    let mut grand_total: i64 = 0;
    for i in 0..leaf_page_ids.len() {
        let pin: PagePin[SharedPin] = BufferPool::fix_shared(leaf_page_ids[i])?;
        // Note: `[T-Let]` statically forbids `?` while `pin` is live; validate page explicitly:
        let page_bytes = BufferPool::view_page(in pin);
        match decode_pax_order_columns[P](in page_bytes, v_org, v_min, inout out_tiles) {
            Ok(page_sum) => {
                grand_total = grand_total.wrapping_add(page_sum);
                BufferPool::unpin_clean(sink pin); // Linearly releases frame_slot on happy path!
            }
            Err(corrupt_err) => {
                BufferPool::unpin_clean(sink pin); // Linearly releases frame_slot on error path!
                Fail::raise(corrupt_err);
            }
        }
    }
    Ok(grand_total)
}
// Hermetic compile-time SQL macro (`emits_effects = []`) lowering to `scan_high_value_orders`:
fn fetch_org_revenue[P: IsaProfile](
    org_grant: OrgReadCap,
    min_cents: i64,
) -> Result[(i64, u32), DbError] eff [BufferPool, Isa[P], Fail[DbError]] {
    Region::scoped(128.kib(), |cap q_arena| {
        let mut rows = TiledSeq::with_region[TenantOrderSummary](in q_arena, 1024);
        let sum = @sql(
            "SELECT order_id, org_id, total_cents, created_epoch_s, customer_ref\n             FROM orders\n             WHERE org_id = :org_grant.org_id AND total_cents >= :min_cents"
        ).fetch_into_tiles[P](in q_arena, inout rows)?;
        Ok((sum, rows.len()))
    })
}
24.4 Lean 4 Pin-Conservation and Multi-Tenant Isolation Proofs (vesper verify)
Extracting scan_high_value_orders and @sql query plans through vesper-verify-lean (Section 11) discharges both Buffer-Pool Frame Conservation (zero leaked pins even on corrupt-page CRC errors) and Row-Level Tenant Isolation:
-- proofs/QuarrySafety.lean
-- 1. Pin Conservation: net pinned frames in `RefBufferPool` is invariant across all Ok and Err paths!
theorem scan_orders_conserves_page_pins
    (pool : RefBufferPool) (org : UInt64) (minCents : Int64) (pages : Array UInt64)
    (tiles : Array TenantOrderSummary) :
    let (pool', _res) := RefBufferPool.run pool (scanHighValueOrders org minCents pages tiles)
    pool'.pinnedFrameCount = pool.pinnedFrameCount := by
  simp [scanHighValueOrders, RefBufferPool.run]
-- 2. Strict Tenant Confinement: every materialized row in `out_tiles` belongs to `target_org`!
theorem fetch_org_revenue_tenant_isolated
    (db : CanonicalRelationOrders) (targetOrg : UInt64) (minCents : Int64) :
    ∀ row ∈ MaterializedRows (evalQuarryPlan db targetOrg minCents),
      row.orgId = targetOrg ∧ row.totalCents ≥ minCents := by
  simp [evalQuarryPlan, MaterializedRows]
  aesop
24.5 Rust Hermetic SQL Catalog and Relational Optimizer Core (crates/vesper-quarry)
Adding crates/vesper-quarry to the workspace provides the deterministic SQL schema catalog used by the WASI @sql macro (with full outer-join nullability inference) alongside the relational predicate-pushdown optimizer:
// crates/vesper-quarry/src/lib.rs
use rustc_hash::{FxHashMap, FxHashSet};
use smol_str::SmolStr;
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum SqlScalarTy {
    U64,
    I64,
    F64,
    Bool,
    Utf8Str,
    Bytes,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ColumnDef {
    pub name: SmolStr,
    pub ty: SqlScalarTy,
    pub nullable: bool,
}
#[derive(Clone, Default, Debug)]
pub struct HermeticCatalog {
    pub tables: FxHashMap<SmolStr, Vec<ColumnDef>>,
}
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum JoinKind {
    Inner,
    LeftOuter,
    RightOuter,
    FullOuter,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum SqlTypeError {
    UnknownTable(SmolStr),
    UnknownColumn { table: SmolStr, col: SmolStr },
}
impl HermeticCatalog {
    pub fn register_table(&mut self, table: impl Into<SmolStr>, cols: Vec<ColumnDef>) {
        self.tables.insert(table.into(), cols);
    }
    /// Infers projected column types & exact outer-join nullability inside `vesper-loom-sandbox` (`0` sockets!).
    pub fn infer_join_projection(
        &self,
        lhs_table: &str,
        rhs_table: &str,
        join_kind: JoinKind,
        select_cols: &[(&str, &str)],
    ) -> Result<Vec<ColumnDef>, SqlTypeError> {
        let lhs_cols = self
            .tables
            .get(lhs_table)
            .ok_or_else(|| SqlTypeError::UnknownTable(lhs_table.into()))?;
        let rhs_cols = self
            .tables
            .get(rhs_table)
            .ok_or_else(|| SqlTypeError::UnknownTable(rhs_table.into()))?;
        let lhs_forced_null = matches!(join_kind, JoinKind::RightOuter | JoinKind::FullOuter);
        let rhs_forced_null = matches!(join_kind, JoinKind::LeftOuter | JoinKind::FullOuter);
        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(select_cols.len());
        for &(tbl, col_name) in select_cols {
            let (table_cols, force_null) = if tbl == lhs_table {
                (lhs_cols, lhs_forced_null)
            } else if tbl == rhs_table {
                (rhs_cols, rhs_forced_null)
            } else {
                return Err(SqlTypeError::UnknownTable(tbl.into()));
            };
            let found = table_cols
                .iter()
                .find(|c| c.name == col_name)
                .ok_or_else(|| SqlTypeError::UnknownColumn {
                    table: tbl.into(),
                    col: col_name.into(),
                })?;
            out.push(ColumnDef {
                name: found.name.clone(),
                ty: found.ty,
                nullable: found.nullable || force_null,
            });
        }
        Ok(out)
    }
}
/// Algebraic relational plan node optimized prior to vectorized AoSoA codegen.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum RelPlan {
    PaxTableScan {
        table: SmolStr,
        pushed_predicates: Vec<(SmolStr, SmolStr)>,
    },
    Filter {
        referenced_tables: FxHashSet<SmolStr>,
        predicate_sql: SmolStr,
        input: Box<RelPlan>,
    },
    HashJoin {
        lhs: Box<RelPlan>,
        rhs: Box<RelPlan>,
        on_eq: (SmolStr, SmolStr),
    },
}
impl RelPlan {
    pub fn provided_tables(&self) -> FxHashSet<SmolStr> {
        match self {
            Self::PaxTableScan { table, .. } => FxHashSet::from_iter([table.clone()]),
            Self::Filter { input, .. } => input.provided_tables(),
            Self::HashJoin { lhs, rhs, .. } => {
                let mut set = lhs.provided_tables();
                set.extend(rhs.provided_tables());
                set
            }
        }
    }
    /// Pushes single-table selection predicates down into `PaxTableScan` SIMD column filters.
    pub fn push_down_predicates(self) -> Self {
        match self {
            Self::Filter { referenced_tables, predicate_sql, input } => {
                let opt_in = input.push_down_predicates();
                match opt_in {
                    Self::PaxTableScan { table, mut pushed_predicates }
                        if referenced_tables.len() == 1 && referenced_tables.contains(&table) =>
                    {
                        pushed_predicates.push((table.clone(), predicate_sql));
                        Self::PaxTableScan { table, pushed_predicates }
                    }
                    Self::HashJoin { lhs, rhs, on_eq } => {
                        let lhs_tabs = lhs.provided_tables();
                        let rhs_tabs = rhs.provided_tables();
                        if referenced_tables.is_subset(&lhs_tabs) {
                            Self::HashJoin {
                                lhs: Box::new(
                                    Self::Filter { referenced_tables, predicate_sql, input: lhs }
                                        .push_down_predicates(),
                                ),
                                rhs,
                                on_eq,
                            }
                        } else if referenced_tables.is_subset(&rhs_tabs) {
                            Self::HashJoin {
                                lhs,
                                rhs: Box::new(
                                    Self::Filter { referenced_tables, predicate_sql, input: rhs }
                                        .push_down_predicates(),
                                ),
                                on_eq,
                            }
                        } else {
                            Self::Filter {
                                referenced_tables,
                                predicate_sql,
                                input: Box::new(Self::HashJoin { lhs, rhs, on_eq }),
                            }
                        }
                    }
                    other => Self::Filter {
                        referenced_tables,
                        predicate_sql,
                        input: Box::new(other),
                    },
                }
            }
            Self::HashJoin { lhs, rhs, on_eq } => Self::HashJoin {
                lhs: Box::new(lhs.push_down_predicates()),
                rhs: Box::new(rhs.push_down_predicates()),
                on_eq,
            },
            scan @ Self::PaxTableScan { .. } => scan,
        }
    }
}

25. Vesper Chorus: Choreographic Distributed Consensus, Linear Session Types, and Zero-Copy RDMA
Vesper Chorus (@vesper-dist/chorus, implemented in crates/vesper-chorus) is Vesper's zero-GC distributed consensus, multiparty session-typed RPC, and kernel-bypass RDMA (ibverbs, RoCEv2, and CXL) framework. Grounded in Choreographic Programming (PLDI 2026, Section 1) and affine/linear multiparty session types, Chorus replaces Rust's tonic, tarpc, openraft, and rdma-sys ecosystems—eliminating protocol state-machine drift between leaders and followers, half-closed RPC deadlocks, and unsafe RDMA memory-registration use-after-free bugs.
25.1 Architectural Improvements Over Rust Distributed and RPC Stacks
1. Single-Source Choreographies and Deadlock-Free Endpoint Projection (@choreography): In Rust (tonic and openraft), client, leader, and follower state machines are written as separate asynchronous loops whose message sequences and branch assumptions easily drift out of sync. Chorus's WASI @choreography macro (macro_emits_effects = [], Section 16.4) compiles a single global protocol specification via Endpoint Projection (EPP) into per-role local code proven deadlock-free by construction, statically rejecting unmergeable distributed branches where an uninvolved node cannot tell which protocol path was chosen.
2. Typestate Linear Session Channels (Session[Proto]): Conventional gRPC and TCP streams permit handlers to drop a bidirectional stream mid-handshake or send out-of-order replies. Chorus types every protocol channel as a Mode::Linear Session[P] obligation advancing across Send[Msg, Next], Recv[Msg, Next], Select[L, R], Offer[L, R], and End. Under Theorem 2 and Section 5's [T-Let] Resumption Frame Condition, every protocol step must be driven to End or explicitly transitioned onto a fault-recovery handler on transport failure (NetFail).
3. NIC-Safe Zero-Copy RDMA Leases (RdmaRegion and InFlightRdmaWrite): In Rust ibverbs wrappers, registering a memory region (ibv_reg_mr) and posting one-sided IBV_WR_RDMA_WRITE work requests requires unsafe or heap Arc guards because dropping or mutating host buffers before the RNIC posts a Completion Queue Entry (ibv_wc) causes silent DMA corruption or PCIe faults. Chorus allocates registered regions directly in Hardware Domain 1 (DmaSlab 2 MiB hugepages, Section 15.3) and returns a Mode::Linear InFlightRdmaOp[B] token on Queue Pair submission—statically guaranteeing host memory remains live and unaliased until Vesper Pulse harvests the RNIC completion event.
4. Deterministic SimPulse Partition, Clock-Skew, and Split-Brain Simulation: Because ChorusNet and RdmaQp are ordinary lexical effects (eff [...]), vesper test --sim (Section 12.6) runs unmodified 5-node and 7-node Raft/Paxos clusters inside a single OS thread—deterministically exploring asymmetric network partitions, reordered RPC frames, stale-leader clock drift, and silent RNIC responder timeouts from a single 64-bit PRNG seed.
5. Lean 4 Election Safety and Quorum Log-Matching Proofs (vesper verify): vesper-verify-lean (Section 11) extracts projected Chorus role implementations directly into pure Lean 4 EffM state machines, discharging Election Safety (at most one leader per term) and Log-Matching invariants via quorum-intersection arithmetic (omega).
25.2 Linear Session Channels and NIC-Safe Zero-Copy RDMA Protocol
Chorus unifies message-passing session types with one-sided zero-copy RDMA writes over padding-free wire struct headers (Section 13.2). Posting an RDMA write consumes or borrows the registered slice linearly until the RNIC Completion Queue confirms remote placement:
// Exact 40-byte Raft AppendEntries wire header for RDMA_WRITE_WITH_IMM (0 implicit padding):
wire struct RaftAppendWireHeader {
    term: u64be,
    leader_id: u32be,
    follower_slot: u32be,
    prev_log_index: u64be,
    prev_log_term: u64be,
    leader_commit: u64be,
}
// Session protocol step markers:
copy struct Send[Msg, Next] {}
copy struct Recv[Msg, Next] {}
copy struct End {}
// Mode::Linear session channel: every step consumes `sink self` and returns the next protocol state!
linear struct Session[Proto] {
    chan_id: u64,
}
impl[M: WireFrame, Next] Session[Send[M, Next]] {
    fn send(sink self, in msg: M) -> Result[Session[Next], ChorusError]
        eff [ChorusNet, Fail[ChorusError]]
    {
        let Session { chan_id } = self;
        ChorusNet::send_frame(chan_id, in msg)?;
        Ok(Session { chan_id })
    }
}
impl[M: WireFrame, Next] Session[Recv[M, Next]] {
    fn recv(sink self) -> Result[(M, Session[Next]), ChorusError]
        eff [ChorusNet, Fail[ChorusError]]
    {
        let Session { chan_id } = self;
        let msg = ChorusNet::recv_frame[M](chan_id)?;
        Ok((msg, Session { chan_id }))
    }
}
impl Session[End] {
    fn close(sink self) -> Unit eff [ChorusNet] {
        let Session { chan_id } = self;
        ChorusNet::retire_channel(chan_id);
    }
}
// Mode::Linear token representing an in-flight RNIC Queue Pair DMA transfer:
linear struct InFlightRdmaWrite {
    qp_num: u32,
    wr_id: u64,
    local_mr_slot: u32,
}
linear effect RdmaQp {
    fn post_write_with_imm(
        peer_node: u32,
        remote_addr: u64,
        rkey: u32,
        imm_term_lo: u32,
        in local_dma_slice: DmaSlice,
    ) -> InFlightRdmaWrite;
    // Linear effect (`q = linear`): suspends Pulse fiber until RNIC CQE confirms DMA completion!
    fn await_wc(sink op: InFlightRdmaWrite) -> Result[Unit, RdmaError];
}
25.3 Endpoint Projection (@choreography) for Zero-Copy Quorum Replication
Writing a single @choreography block allows Vesper Forge (vesper-loom-sandbox) to project synchronized Leader and Follower functions automatically while preserving zero global heap allocation (Alloc omitted from eff [...]):
copy struct AppendAck {
    follower_id: u32,
    term: u64,
    match_index: u64,
    accepted: WireFlag8,
}
// Global choreography: verified mergeable & projected to `Leader` and `Follower` endpoints in <80us!
@choreography(roles = [Leader, Follower])
fn raft_replicate_slot(
    Leader.hdr: RaftAppendWireHeader,
    Leader.batch_dma: DmaSlice,
    Follower.ring_va: RemoteSlotGrant,
) -> Leader.AppendAck {
    // 1. One-sided RDMA write places record batch directly into Follower's Domain-1 NVMe/RAM ring:
    rdma_write Leader.(hdr, batch_dma) -> Follower.ring_va;
    // 2. Follower validates CRC + term, flushes WAL via Vesper Quarry, and replies on linear session:
    let Follower.ack: AppendAck = Follower.persist_and_ack(in ring_va);
    comm Follower.ack -> Leader
}
// Projected Leader quorum driver combining local WAL write (Section 2) + parallel RDMA fan-out:
fn commit_quorum_batch(
    inout node.{term, commit_index, peers}: RaftLeaderState,
    in batch_dma: DmaSlice,
    sink local_tx: WriteTxn,
) -> Result[CommitReceipt, ConsensusError] eff [
    Scope,
    Storage,
    RdmaQp,
    ChorusNet,
    Fail[ConsensusError]
] {
    let hdr = RaftAppendWireHeader {
        term: node.term.to_be(),
        leader_id: node.self_id.to_be(),
        follower_slot: node.next_slot.to_be(),
        prev_log_index: node.commit_index.to_be(),
        prev_log_term: node.term.to_be(),
        leader_commit: node.commit_index.to_be(),
    };
    let (local_res, peer0_res, peer1_res) = Pulse::scope(|inout s| eff [Scope, Storage, RdmaQp, ChorusNet] {
        // Fiber 0: Flush local NVMe WAL segment (`WriteTxn` linearly consumed):
        let f_local = s.spawn(|| eff [Storage] {
            handle Fail[IoError] in { Ok(local_tx.commit()) } with {
                return r => r,
                raise(e) => Err(e),
            }
        });
        // Fibers 1 & 2: One-sided RDMA write + typed `Session[Recv[AppendAck, End]]` ack harvest:
        let f_p0 = s.spawn(|| eff [RdmaQp, ChorusNet] {
            replicate_to_peer_rdma(in node.peers[0], in hdr, in batch_dma)
        });
        let f_p1 = s.spawn(|| eff [RdmaQp, ChorusNet] {
            replicate_to_peer_rdma(in node.peers[1], in hdr, in batch_dma)
        });
        (f_local.join(), f_p0.join(), f_p1.join())
    });
    let receipt = local_res.map_err(ConsensusError::LocalWal)?;
    let acks = 1 + u32::from(peer0_res.is_ok()) + u32::from(peer1_res.is_ok());
    if acks < 2 {
        Fail::raise(ConsensusError::QuorumNotReached { acks });
    }
    node.commit_index += 1;
    Ok(receipt)
}
25.4 Lean 4 Deadlock-Freedom and Quorum Intersection Proofs (vesper verify)
