39. Vesper Stylus and Lint: Lossless Rowan CST Formatting, Substructural and Effect-Row Static Analysis, and MIF-Verified Autofixes (vesper fmt and vesper lint)
Vesper Stylus (crates/vesper-fmt, invoked via vesper fmt) and Vesper Lint (crates/vesper-lint, invoked via vesper lint and live inside Vesper Lens, Section 21) are Vesper's zero-GC, zero-configuration canonical code formatter and semantic static-analysis/autofix engine. Replacing Rust's rustfmt and cargo clippy—which operate as disconnected tools with drifting parsers, fragile text-span string replacements, and zero awareness of substructural linearity or lexical effect rows—Stylus and Lint share the exact incremental salsa + rowan lossless Concrete Syntax Tree, vesper-hir modal/effect checker, vesper-mir-pcg Place Capability Graph, and Stage 4 egglog e-graph engine used by the production compiler.
39.1 Architectural Improvements Over Rust rustfmt and cargo clippy
1. Comment-Complete Wadler-Lindig Formatting Over Rowan CSTs (vesper fmt): Rust's rustfmt uses a standalone AST parser that routinely bails out on macro bodies, long method chains, or comments nestled inside generic bounds, and exposes dozens of unstable rustfmt.toml configuration knobs. Because Vesper's LL(2) square-bracket grammar (Section 3) parses deterministically into a lossless rowan Red-Green CST where every trivia token (comments, blank lines, attributes) is anchored structurally, Stylus lowers any CST node—including macro { ... } quotations (Section 16.4) and partial IDE buffers—into a Wadler-Lindig / Oppen pretty-printing Doc algebra. Formatting is zero-configuration, deterministic, idempotent (fmt(fmt(src)) == fmt(src)), and statically audited to preserve the exact non-trivia token stream plus 100 percent of user comments.
2. Substructural Mode, FieldTree, and Effect-Row Lints Impossible in Clippy (vesper lint): Because cargo clippy runs on Rust HIR/MIR without modal kinds, disjoint field-tree projections, or lexical effect rows, it cannot reason about over-broad struct borrows, redundant heap authority, or sub-optimal modal bounds. Vesper Lint queries vesper-hir and vesper-mir-pcg directly to surface five high-signal systems lints with zero false positives: (2a) Projection Narrowing (overbroad_inout_projection) detects when fn update(inout shard: WorkerShard) mutates only shard.metrics and reads shard.config, offering a 1-click autofix to narrow the signature to inout shard.{metrics}, in shard.{config}: WorkerShard—unlocking caller-side [T-CallProj] parallelism (Section 5) as a Minor-compatible SemVer evolution ([SV-Fn], Section 9.2); (2b) Zero-Heap Arena Demotion (demote_alloc_to_region) detects functions requesting global eff [Alloc] whose allocations never escape the return type, rewriting heap containers into a stack-bounded Region::scoped VirtualArena (Section 15.2) and removing Alloc from eff [...]; (2c) Unused Capability and Over-Restrictive Mode Bounds (unused_effect_capability, overly_restrictive_mode) flags declared effects in eff [...] never performed in the body and generic bounds like [T: mod copy] where T is consumed at most once (relaxable to [T: mod linear]); (2d) FIP Constructor Pairing (missed_fip_opportunity) queries Stage 4's ReuseSlot analysis (Section 15.4) when reordering an owned match unpack before a same-sized constructor enables @fip zero-allocation in-place reuse; and (2e) Cancel-Point Proximity and Secret Hygiene (early_cancel_checkpoint, unredacted_pii_boundary) hoists Cancel::check()? above Mode::Linear acquisitions (Section 12.2) and guards @pii / Vault Sec* values (Sections 22.4 and 23.2).
3. E-Graph Semantic Simplification Without Macro False Positives (egglog Lint Rules): Clippy maintains over 750 hand-written AST/HIR pattern matchers that frequently misfire on macro-generated code or miss equivalent algebraic forms. Vesper Lint expresses algebraic, bitwise, SIMD (VVec), and relational (Quarry / Flux) simplifications as declarative egglog rewrite rules where the extracted e-node has strictly lower AST/cycle cost than the source expression, gated by Section 16.4's ScopeSet hygiene marks so macro-internal expansions never trigger spurious call-site warnings.
4. Proof-Preserving Autofixes Verified by Incremental Re-Typechecking (vesper lint --fix): Textual autofixes in cargo clippy --fix can introduce name collisions, borrow errors, or broken formatting. Every Vesper Lint autofix constructs a typed rowan CST splice, formats only the touched subtree via Vesper Stylus, and commits the edit if and only if incremental salsa re-checking confirms zero new diagnostics, preservation of Theorem 2 (Total Linear Exhaustion), and SemVer compatibility (I_new <=_pub I_old, Section 9) when --semver-safe is enabled.
5. Hermetic WASI Domain Lint Packs (Loom.toml [lints.plugins]): Domain frameworks (Vesper Vault, Vesper Ignis, Vesper Resonance, Vesper Aegis) distribute domain-specific lints (such as GPU barrier layout anti-patterns or @realtime filter coefficient checks) as fuel-metered wasm32-wasip2 components executed inside vesper-loom-sandbox (Section 8.2)—delivering extensible static analysis with zero host-code execution risk and sub-millisecond salsa caching in Vesper Lens (Section 21).
39.2 Canonical Formatting Rules and Declarative Loom.toml Lint Policy
Stylus formats Vesper's signature constructs—disjoint PlaceTree projections, square-bracket generics, eff [...] capability rows, and handle blocks—under a canonical 100-column ribbon budget with zero formatting configuration files, while Loom.toml governs lint severities workspace-wide:

39.3 Substructural Lint Autofix Showcase: Projection Narrowing and Arena Demotion
When vesper lint inspects a function with an over-broad whole-struct borrow and non-escaping global heap allocation, vesper lint --fix synthesizes a verified MIF-compatible CST rewrite:
// BEFORE `vesper lint --fix`:
// Warning[L0101: overbroad_inout_projection]: `inout shard: WorkerShard` mutates only
//   `shard.metrics.requests` and reads `shard.config.limit`.
// Warning[L0204: demote_alloc_to_region]: `kept` allocates via `eff [Alloc]` and never escapes `u32`.
fn filter_ids_unoptimized(
    inout shard: WorkerShard,
    in raw_ids: Slice[u64],
) -> u32 eff [Alloc] {
    let mut kept = HeapVec::with_capacity[u64](256);
    for id in raw_ids {
        if id < shard.config.limit {
            kept.push(id);
        }
    }
    let count = kept.len() as u32;
    shard.metrics.requests += count as u64;
    count
}
// AFTER `vesper lint --fix` (verified SemVer Minor-compatible: I_new <=_pub I_old!):
fn filter_ids_unoptimized(
    inout shard.{metrics.requests}: WorkerShard,
    in shard.{config.limit}: WorkerShard,
    in raw_ids: Slice[u64],
) -> u32 eff [] {
    Region::scoped(4.kib(), |cap local_reg| {
        let mut kept = RegionVec::with_region[u64](in local_reg, 256);
        for id in raw_ids {
            if id < shard.config.limit {
                kept.push(id);
            }
        }
        let count = kept.len() as u32;
        shard.metrics.requests += count as u64;
        count
    })
}
39.4 Lean 4 Comment-Token Conservation and Autofix SemVer Proofs (vesper verify)
Extracting Stylus's Wadler-Lindig renderer and Lint's projection-narrowing autofix pass via vesper-verify-lean (Section 11) discharges both Comment-Token Conservation across flat/broken group modes and Autofix SemVer Substitutability:
-- proofs/StylusAndLintSafety.lean
theorem stylus_format_preserves_comments (doc : FormatDoc) (width col : Nat) :
    CommentTokensOf (StylusSpec.render width col .flat doc) =
    CommentTokensOf (StylusSpec.render width col .broken doc) := by
  induction doc generalizing col <;> simp [StylusSpec.render, CommentTokensOf, *]
theorem lint_autofix_preserves_caller_disjointness
    (leavesNew leavesOld callerOther : Nat -> Bool)
    (h_subset : forall f, leavesNew f = true -> leavesOld f = true)
    (h_disj   : forall f, ¬(leavesOld f = true /\ callerOther f = true)) :
    forall f, ¬(leavesNew f = true /\ callerOther f = true) := by
  intro f ⟨h_new, h_other⟩
  exact h_disj f ⟨h_subset f h_new, h_other⟩
39.5 Rust Wadler-Lindig Formatter and Substructural Lint Engine (crates/vesper-fmt and crates/vesper-lint)
Adding crates/vesper-fmt and crates/vesper-lint brings the unified Rust host workspace to 44 crates, implementing the O(N) Wadler-Lindig pretty-printer and the HIR/PCG substructural lint engine:
// crates/vesper-fmt/src/lib.rs
use smol_str::SmolStr;
pub const DEFAULT_LINE_WIDTH: usize = 100;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum FormatDoc {
    Nil,
    Text(SmolStr),
    Comment(SmolStr),
    HardLine,
    SoftLineOrSpace,
    SoftLineOrEmpty,
    Nest(usize, Box<FormatDoc>),
    Concat(Box<FormatDoc>, Box<FormatDoc>),
    Group(Box<FormatDoc>),
}
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum PrintMode {
    Flat,
    Break,
}
impl FormatDoc {
    pub fn flat_width(&self) -> Option<usize> {
        match self {
            Self::Nil | Self::SoftLineOrEmpty => Some(0),
            Self::Text(s) => Some(s.len()),
            Self::Comment(c) => (!c.contains('\n') && !c.starts_with("//")).then_some(c.len()),
            Self::HardLine => None,
            Self::SoftLineOrSpace => Some(1),
            Self::Nest(_, inner) | Self::Group(inner) => inner.flat_width(),
            Self::Concat(a, b) => Some(a.flat_width()?.saturating_add(b.flat_width()?)),
        }
    }
    pub fn render_canonical(&self, max_width: usize) -> String {
        let mut out = String::new();
        let mut work: Vec<(usize, PrintMode, &FormatDoc)> = vec![(0, PrintMode::Break, self)];
        let mut col = 0usize;
        while let Some((indent, mode, doc)) = work.pop() {
            match doc {
                Self::Nil => {}
                Self::Text(s) | Self::Comment(s) => {
                    out.push_str(s);
                    col += s.len();
                }
                Self::HardLine => {
                    out.push('\n');
                    out.extend(core::iter::repeat(' ').take(indent));
                    col = indent;
                }
                Self::SoftLineOrSpace => match mode {
                    PrintMode::Flat => {
                        out.push(' ');
                        col += 1;
                    }
                    PrintMode::Break => {
                        out.push('\n');
                        out.extend(core::iter::repeat(' ').take(indent));
                        col = indent;
                    }
                },
                Self::SoftLineOrEmpty => {
                    if mode == PrintMode::Break {
                        out.push('\n');
                        out.extend(core::iter::repeat(' ').take(indent));
                        col = indent;
                    }
                }
                Self::Nest(spaces, inner) => work.push((indent + spaces, mode, inner)),
                Self::Concat(lhs, rhs) => {
                    work.push((indent, mode, rhs));
                    work.push((indent, mode, lhs));
                }
                Self::Group(inner) => {
                    let gmode = match inner.flat_width() {
                        Some(w) if col + w <= max_width => PrintMode::Flat,
                        _ => PrintMode::Break,
                    };
                    work.push((indent, gmode, inner));
                }
            }
        }
        out
    }
}

40. Vesper Codex: Hermetic Living API Documentation, Substructural Mode/Effect/FieldTree Footprints, Single-JIT-Module Doctests, and Linked Lean 4 Proof Certificates (vesper doc and vesper test --doc)
Vesper Codex (crates/vesper-doc, invoked via vesper doc and vesper test --doc, and surfaced inline on hover inside Vesper Lens, Section 21) is Vesper's zero-GC, hermetic documentation generator, visual memory/protocol inspector, substructural API search engine, and JIT doctest runner. Replacing Rust's rustdoc and cargo test --doc—which render purely syntactic signatures that hide ambient syscalls, panic unwinds, struct padding, and drop side effects while paying steep linker overhead per doctest—Codex queries vesper-hir, vesper-mir-pcg, vesper-semver, and vesper-verify-lean directly to generate living systems specifications backed by cryptographic proof certificates and sub-100-millisecond Cranelift JIT doctest verification.
40.1 Architectural Improvements Over Rust rustdoc and cargo doc
1. Auto-Synthesized Substructural Obligation Graphs, Mode-Crossing Matrices, and FieldTree Heatmaps: In rustdoc, critical systems contracts—whether a handle must be closed explicitly, which struct fields a &mut self method actually mutates, or whether a function allocates on the global heap—are invisible in the rendered signature and rely on prose comments that silently rot. Codex extracts verified compiler metadata to render four interactive visual contracts on every item page: (1a) Linear Typestate Obligation Diagrams automatically graph every Mode::Linear type (WriteTxn, Responder[HeadersPending], Session[Proto], SnapshotResumeTicket) from its minting constructors across inout mutation edges to its terminal sink consumers (commit, abort), statically flagging any public linear type lacking a reachable public terminal consumer; (1b) OxCaml Mode-Crossing Tables display exact [K-Adt] instantiations for generic containers (Option[T] -> copy | affine | linear, Section 4); (1c) Interactive FieldTree Borrow Footprints color-code mutated leaf paths (inout broker.{index, metrics}) versus read-only leaf paths (in broker.{config}), showing callers at a glance which sibling fields remain free for concurrent [T-CallProj] borrows; and (1d) Byte-Exact Wire and Cache-Line Maps render 64-byte L1 cache-line diagrams for tiled(16) struct (AoSoA, Section 15.5) and bit/endianness offset rulers for padding-free wire struct headers (Section 13.2).
2. Explicit Authority, Cancel-Safety, and Zero-Alloc Capability Badges: Because ambient authority does not exist in Vesper, every documented function and package displays compiler-verified capability badges derived from eff [...] and Loom.toml (Section 8.2): Zero-Alloc (when Alloc is absent from eff [...]), Pure (eff []), Uncancellable Linear I/O (res(eff) = linear), Abortable Checkpoint (when Fail[E] or Cancel is present, alerting callers holding live Mode::Linear values), Constant-Time Verified (eff [ConstTime], Section 23), and Hard-Realtime Safe (@realtime WCSD stack byte bound, Sections 26.5 and 32.1).
3. Single-JIT-Module Hermetic Doctests in Under 100 Milliseconds (vesper test --doc): Historically in Rust, cargo test --doc spawned hundreds of separate rustc and host linker invocations, and even merged doctest runners still incur heavy backend codegen, disk I/O, and unrestricted ambient network/filesystem execution. Vesper Codex parses all /// code blocks across an entire package, wraps each example as an isolated entry function inside a single in-memory Cranelift JIT module (cranelift-jit, Section 7), and executes each snippet inside a resettable 64 KiB VirtualArena under SimPulse deterministic virtual I/O and clocks (Section 12.6) with zero object files and zero system linker invocations. Crucially, every doctest undergoes full Stage 3 PCG checking—guaranteeing that documentation snippets never omit linear obligation cleanup—while compile_fail(E0301) doctests assert exact diagnostic error codes (Section 21.4) so negative substructural examples never pass for the wrong reason.
4. Inline Lean 4 Proof Certificates, Pact Model Discovery, and MIF SemVer Changelogs: Bridging the gap between API docs and formal methods, Codex links directly into ~/.vesper/store/ (OLEAN_CERTS, Section 11.4): functions verified via vesper verify display a green Verified (0 sorryAx) badge that expands inline to inspect the extracted prophecy-free Lean 4 functional model (Funs.lean), exported invariants (Specs.lean), and kernel-audited axiom footprint. Additionally, Codex indexes both canonical impls and third-party Vesper Pact named models (Section 16.3), resolves intra-doc [Type::method] links inside Stage 1 rowan CSTs (surfacing broken links in <2 ms in Vesper Lens), and computes automatic SemVer API diffs across mif:blake3 versions via vesper-semver (Section 9).
5. Offline Substructural Hoogle Search and Content-Addressed Loom Doc Caching: Codex ships a 42 KiB WebAssembly query engine (codex-search.wasm) supporting Substructural Type, FieldTree, and Effect-Row Search: developers query signatures like sink WriteTxn -> CommitReceipt eff [Storage] or inout TiledSeq[_] -> u64 eff [Isa] and receive variance-aware matches respecting contravariant parameter sub-moding (copy <= affine <= linear), covariant return modes, and effect-ceiling subsets. Because vesper doc emits 100 percent deterministic static HTML/WASM bundles with zero external CDN requests, Vesper Loom caches prebuilt documentation in ~/.vesper/store/ and mounts dependency docs into target/doc/ instantaneously via copy-on-write reflinks.
40.2 CST-Verified Doc Attributes and Hermetic SimPulse Doctests
Doc comments (//!\ module docs and /// item docs) are first-class nodes in Vesper's lossless rowan CST (Section 21.2) rather than opaque strings. Intra-doc links ([WriteTxn::commit]), @proof(...) theorem anchors, and positive/negative doctests are typechecked alongside the item they document:

40.4 Lean 4 Substructural Hoogle Soundness and Wire-Map Completeness Proofs (vesper verify)
Extracting Codex's substructural signature-search matcher and wire struct byte-ruler generator through vesper-verify-lean (Section 11) discharges both Variance-Aware Search Substitutability (every function returned for a (queryParamMode, queryRetMode, queryEffCeiling) search can be invoked safely at the query's call site without violating [T-SubMode] or Loom.toml ceilings) and Contiguous Zero-Hole Wire Map Coverage:
-- proofs/CodexSearchAndLayout.lean
-- 1. Substructural Hoogle Search Soundness:
-- A candidate function `(fnParamMode, fnRetMode)` safely satisfies a developer query
-- `(qParamMode, qRetMode)` whenever `qParamMode <= fnParamMode` (contravariant input: a consumer
-- accepting `linear` also accepts `copy`/`affine` via `[T-SubMode]`) and `fnRetMode <= qRetMode`!
theorem codex_hoogle_mode_match_sound
    (qParam fnParam fnRet qRet callerArgRank : Nat)
    (h_caller_has : callerArgRank <= qParam)
    (h_param_contra : qParam <= fnParam)
    (h_ret_cov : fnRet <= qRet) :
    callerArgRank <= fnParam /\ fnRet <= qRet := by
  omega
-- 2. Wire-Struct Byte Ruler Partition Exactness: adjacent field spans `[off0, off0 + len0)` and
-- `[off1, off1 + len1)` with `off1 = off0 + len0` cover `[off0, off0 + len0 + len1)` with 0 padding holes:
theorem codex_wire_diagram_zero_holes
    (off0 len0 off1 len1 : Nat) (h_contig : off1 = off0 + len0) :
    (off1 - off0) + len1 = len0 + len1 /\ off0 + len0 <= off1 := by
  omega
40.5 Rust Linear Typestate Extractor, Substructural Hoogle Engine, and JIT Doctest Planner (crates/vesper-doc)
Adding crates/vesper-doc brings the unified Rust host workspace to 45 modular crates, implementing the linear obligation graph extractor (auditing that every public linear typestate has a reachable terminal sink consumer), the variance-aware Hoogle signature matcher, and the single-module Cranelift JIT doctest planner:
// crates/vesper-doc/src/lib.rs
use rustc_hash::{FxHashMap, FxHashSet};
use smol_str::SmolStr;
use vesper_hir::ty::{Mode, PassMode};
use vesper_span::diagnostic::DiagnosticCode;
use std::collections::VecDeque;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct LinearMethodTransition {
    pub from_state: SmolStr,
    pub method_name: SmolStr,
    pub receiver_mode: PassMode,
    /// `None` when `sink self` discharges the linear obligation into a copy/affine receipt;
    /// `Some(next_state)` when `sink self` transitions to another linear typestate (e.g. `Closed`).
    pub next_linear_state: Option<SmolStr>,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum CodexAuditError {
    DeadEndLinearTypestate { typestate: SmolStr },
}
/// Builds the Typestate Obligation Graph for `vesper doc` and verifies via reverse BFS that every
/// reachable linear typestate has at least one public path to a terminal `sink` discharge.
pub fn verify_linear_typestate_reachability(
    states: &[SmolStr],
    edges: &[LinearMethodTransition],
) -> Result<(), CodexAuditError> {
    let mut reverse_adj: FxHashMap<SmolStr, Vec<SmolStr>> = FxHashMap::default();
    let mut can_discharge: FxHashSet<SmolStr> = FxHashSet::default();
    let mut queue: VecDeque<SmolStr> = VecDeque::new();
    for edge in edges {
        if edge.receiver_mode == PassMode::Sink {
            match &edge.next_linear_state {
                None => {
                    if can_discharge.insert(edge.from_state.clone()) {
                        queue.push_back(edge.from_state.clone());
                    }
                }
                Some(next_st) => {
                    reverse_adj
                        .entry(next_st.clone())
                        .or_default()
                        .push(edge.from_state.clone());
                }
            }
        }
    }
    while let Some(discharged_st) = queue.pop_front() {
        if let Some(preds) = reverse_adj.get(&discharged_st) {
            for pred in preds {
                if can_discharge.insert(pred.clone()) {
                    queue.push_back(pred.clone());
                }
            }
        }
    }
    for st in states {
        if !can_discharge.contains(st) {
            return Err(CodexAuditError::DeadEndLinearTypestate {
                typestate: st.clone(),
            });
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
/// Developer search query in Codex's offline WASM Hoogle bar (type + mode + `eff [...]` ceiling).
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct SubstructuralSearchQuery {
    pub param_modes: Vec<(PassMode, Mode)>,
    pub ret_max_mode: Mode,
    pub allowed_effects: FxHashSet<SmolStr>,
    pub require_zero_alloc: bool,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct IndexedFnDocEntry {
    pub path: SmolStr,
    pub param_modes: Vec<(PassMode, Mode)>,
    pub ret_mode: Mode,
    pub effects: Vec<SmolStr>,
    pub has_lean_proof_cert: bool,
}
/// Variance-aware substructural signature matcher (`0` false-incompatible results; ranks exact + verified first).
pub fn search_codex_signatures<'a>(
    query: &SubstructuralSearchQuery,
    index: &'a [IndexedFnDocEntry],
) -> Vec<(u32, &'a IndexedFnDocEntry)> {
    let mut matches = Vec::new();
    for entry in index {
        if entry.param_modes.len() != query.param_modes.len() {
            continue;
        }
        if entry.ret_mode > query.ret_max_mode {
            continue; // Covariant return check: `fn_ret <= query_ret`
        }
        if query.require_zero_alloc && entry.effects.iter().any(|e| e == "Alloc") {
            continue;
        }
        if !entry.effects.iter().all(|e| query.allowed_effects.contains(e)) {
            continue;
        }
        // Contravariant parameter mode check: a function accepting `fn_m` accepts `q_m` iff `q_m <= fn_m`
        let params_compatible = entry
            .param_modes
            .iter()
            .zip(&query.param_modes)
            .all(|((fn_pass, fn_m), (q_pass, q_m))| fn_pass == q_pass && *q_m <= *fn_m);
        if !params_compatible {
            continue;
        }
        let exact_modes = entry.param_modes == query.param_modes && entry.ret_mode == query.ret_max_mode;
        let score = (u32::from(exact_modes) * 10) + (u32::from(entry.has_lean_proof_cert) * 5);
        matches.push((score, entry));
    }
    matches.sort_by(|(sa, ea), (sb, eb)| sb.cmp(sa).then_with(|| ea.path.cmp(&eb.path)));
    matches
}
/// Represents a parsed `///` doctest block batched into a single `cranelift-jit` module (`0` linker spawns).
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum DoctestExpectation {
    RunSimPulseOk { seed: u64, arena_bytes: usize },
    CompileFailWithCode(DiagnosticCode),
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct BatchedJitDoctest {
    pub item_path: SmolStr,
    pub snippet_line: u32,
    pub expectation: DoctestExpectation,
    pub synthesized_entry_sym: SmolStr,
}
// crates/vesper-lint/src/lib.rs
use rustc_hash::FxHashSet;
use smol_str::SmolStr;
use vesper_hir::ty::{FieldIdx, FieldTree, Mode};
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum SubstructuralLintFinding {
    OverbroadInOutProjection {
        param_name: SmolStr,
        suggested_mut_fields: Vec<FieldIdx>,
        suggested_read_fields: Vec<FieldIdx>,
    },
    UnusedEffectCapability {
        fn_name: SmolStr,
        unused_effect: SmolStr,
    },
    DemoteAllocToRegion {
        fn_name: SmolStr,
        bounded_bytes_estimate: Option<usize>,
    },
    OverlyRestrictiveGenericMode {
        type_param: SmolStr,
        declared_bound: Mode,
        observed_usage: Mode,
    },
}
pub fn analyze_fn_substructural_lints(
    fn_name: &SmolStr,
    declared_effects: &[SmolStr],
    performed_effects: &FxHashSet<SmolStr>,
    alloc_escapes_return: bool,
    alloc_upper_bound: Option<usize>,
    inout_params: &[(SmolStr, FieldTree, Vec<FieldIdx>, Vec<FieldIdx>)],
    generic_mode_usages: &[(SmolStr, Mode, Mode)],
) -> Vec<SubstructuralLintFinding> {
    let mut findings = Vec::new();
    for eff in declared_effects {
        if !performed_effects.contains(eff) {
            findings.push(SubstructuralLintFinding::UnusedEffectCapability {
                fn_name: fn_name.clone(),
                unused_effect: eff.clone(),
            });
        }
    }
    if performed_effects.contains("Alloc") && !alloc_escapes_return {
        findings.push(SubstructuralLintFinding::DemoteAllocToRegion {
            fn_name: fn_name.clone(),
            bounded_bytes_estimate: alloc_upper_bound,
        });
    }
    for (param_name, declared_tree, mutated_leaves, read_leaves) in inout_params {
        if declared_tree.children.is_empty() && !mutated_leaves.is_empty() {
            findings.push(SubstructuralLintFinding::OverbroadInOutProjection {
                param_name: param_name.clone(),
                suggested_mut_fields: mutated_leaves.clone(),
                suggested_read_fields: read_leaves.clone(),
            });
        }
    }
    for (tp, declared_bound, observed_max_mode) in generic_mode_usages {
        if *declared_bound < *observed_max_mode {
            findings.push(SubstructuralLintFinding::OverlyRestrictiveGenericMode {
                type_param: tp.clone(),
                declared_bound: *declared_bound,
                observed_usage: *observed_max_mode,
            });
        }
    }
    findings
}
41. Vesper Metronome: User-Space Hardware PMU Microbenchmarking, Paired Interleaved A/B Regression Gates, Stabilizer Layout Randomization, and Fiber Causal Profiling (vesper bench)
Vesper Metronome (@vesper-bench/metronome, implemented in crates/vesper-bench and invoked via vesper bench and live CodeLenses in Vesper Lens, Section 21) is Vesper's zero-GC, hardware-counter-driven microbenchmarking, assembly inspection, and causal latency profiling subsystem. Replacing Rust's nightly libtest #[bench] harness and fragmented external crates (criterion, divan, iai-callgrind, tango, coz, and cargo-show-asm), Metronome unifies 12-cycle user-space PMU telemetry, in-process CAS-backed A/B interleaving, code/stack layout randomization, substructural linear-input batching, and Stage 5 Cranelift SSA/ASM diffing into a single built-in workflow.
41.1 Architectural Improvements Over Rust criterion, divan, and iai-callgrind
1. 12-Cycle User-Space Hardware PMU Telemetry + Zero-Noise Virtual Cost Gates (eff [BenchPmu]): Wall-clock timers in Rust's Criterion suffer 5 to 15 percent variance on shared cloud CI runners due to turbo-boost frequency scaling and hypervisor scheduling jitter, whereas Valgrind's iai-callgrind runs 20x to 50x slower on a synthetic cache model that misses real branch-predictor, hardware prefetcher, and AVX-512/SVE2 port-contention effects. Metronome reads hardware Performance Monitoring Units directly in user space (~12 cycles via x86_64 RDPMC over mmap'd perf_event_open pages, macOS Apple Silicon kpc thread counters, and AArch64 PMCCNTR_EL0)—capturing retired instructions, core reference cycles, L1D/LLC cache misses, branch mispredictions, and Intel/Arm Top-Down Microarchitecture Analysis (TMA) pipeline slots—while pairing with a deterministic Cranelift SSA + SimPulse virtual-cycle mode (vesper bench --deterministic) for 0.00 percent variance CI gates.
2. CAS-Backed Paired Interleaved A/B Execution and Stabilizer Layout Randomization: Subtle 3 to 8 percent benchmark shifts in C++ and Rust routinely turn out to be ghosts caused by 64-byte instruction-cache alignment shifts or BTB aliasing when unrelated code changes link order (Curtsinger and Berger, Stabilizer). When running vesper bench --compare git:main, Metronome loads the precompiled baseline archive directly from Vesper Loom's content-addressed store (~/.vesper/store/, Section 8.2) alongside the candidate inside the same OS process via cranelift-jit (Section 7). It alternates 1-millisecond paired quanta (A, B, B, A, Tango-style) on a core-pinned thread while re-randomizing function entry-point padding offsets modulo 4 KiB across epochs—canceling thermal/VM drift and isolating genuine algorithmic speedups down to 0.3 percent in under 2 seconds.
3. Zero-Distortion Mode::Linear Batch Setup and Register-Preserving black_box: Benchmarking functions that consume owned or linear values (such as WriteTxn::commit(sink self) in Section 2 or UmemFrame in Section 35) in Rust forces either timed .clone() calls or per-batch GlobalAlloc vector allocations, while core::hint::black_box often forces LLVM to spill SIMD registers onto the stack. Metronome's BenchCtx::iter_linear pre-constructs linear obligations inside an untimed VirtualArena (Region, Section 15.3), brackets only the target call with user-space RDPMC fences, and runs untimed linear teardown on any residual handles—preserving Theorem 2 (Total Linear Exhaustion) with zero heap noise. Simultaneously, Metronome::black_box lowers to a Stage 5 Cranelift opaque SSA value barrier that blocks Stage 4 egglog constant folding/DCE without spilling scalar or VVec registers to memory.
4. Static + Hardware Performance Contracts (@bench Gates) and Annotated ASM Diffs (vesper bench --asm): Rather than treating benchmarks as passive charts inspected manually, @bench(...) supports machine-enforced CI assertions: zero_alloc = true verifies both statically (Alloc absent from eff [...]) and dynamically (0ShardSlab bytes), expect_vectorized = true verifies that Stage 4 egglog emitted predicated VVec vector instructions rather than scalar loops, and max_instructions_per_byte enforces algorithmic throughput ceilings. Passing --asm renders side-by-side Cranelift CLIF IR and native x86_64/AArch64/RISC-V assembly diffs annotated inline with hardware PMU stall cycles and AoSoA cache-line miss heatmaps.
5. Fiber-Aware Coz Causal Profiling (vesper bench --causal) and Lean 4 Statistical/PMU Proofs: Traditional sampling profilers measure where CPU cycles are spent, which misleads engineers in asynchronous io_uring pipelines where speeding up an off-critical-path task yields zero end-to-end throughput gain. Metronome integrates Coz-style Causal Profiling directly into Vesper Pulse worker shards (PulseWorkerShard, Section 12.7): during virtual speedup experiments on candidate function F, whenever a fiber executes F, sibling fibers concurrently active on the shard pause by a proportional virtual delay d at their next lexical effect boundary—plotting exact end-to-end p99 latency and throughput sensitivity curves before a single line of optimization is written. Meanwhile, vesper-verify-lean (Section 11) proves wrap-safe PMU counter delta monotonicity and paired-sample noise cancellation.
41.2 Padding-Free wire struct PMU Snapshots, Linear BenchCtx Batching, and @bench Contracts
Metronome records per-sample hardware PMU counters into a 64-byte L1-cache-line-sized wire struct (Section 13.2) and exposes linear effect BenchHarness so Mode::Linear setup and teardown never contaminate timed instruction/cycle windows:
// Exact 64-byte (1 L1 cache line, 0 implicit padding) hardware PMU & TMA sample record:
wire struct PmuSampleWire {
    ref_cycles: u64le,
    instructions_retired: u64le,
    l1d_read_misses: u64le,
    llc_misses: u64le,
    branch_mispredicts: u64le,
    simd_vec_uops_retired: u64le,
    arena_bump_bytes: u64le,
    tma_backend_bound_slots_bp: u32le, // Basis points (0..10000) of Top-Down Backend-Bound slots
    icache_rand_offset_bytes: u32le,   // Stabilizer 64B-aligned entry jitter offset [0..4096)
}
// Register-preserving Stage 5 Cranelift optimization barrier (0 stack spills!):
fn black_box[T: mod linear](sink val: T) -> T eff [];
linear effect BenchHarness {
    // Untimed pre-allocation of `N` linear obligations inside a dedicated Region arena,
    // followed by RDPMC-fenced execution of `body` on each `sink` element:
    fn iter_linear_batched[In: mod linear, Out: mod affine, r: eff](
        setup: fn(in RegionCap[bench_reg], usize) -> In eff [bench_reg: Region],
        body: fn(sink In) -> Out eff [r],
    ) -> PmuSampleWire eff [r];
    // Marks an end-to-end throughput/latency progress point for `vesper bench --causal`:
    fn causal_progress_point(point_name: StaticStr) -> Unit;
}
41.3 Zero-Alloc SIMD Benchmark with @bench Hardware Gates and Linear WAL Batching
Using @bench, engineers benchmark both pure SIMD kernels (Vesper Wire/Json, Section 20) and Mode::Linear storage transactions (WriteTxn, Section 2) with enforced zero-allocation and vectorization gates:
// 1. Enforces in CI: 0 global Alloc, verified Stage 4 VVec vectorization, and <= 2.2 instructions/byte!
@bench(
    zero_alloc = true,
    expect_vectorized = true,
    max_instructions_per_byte = 2.2,
    stabilizer_pages = 16,
)
fn bench_simd_json_ndjson_ingest[P: IsaProfile](
    in sample_ndjson_64k: AlignedSlice[u8, 64],
) -> u32 eff [BenchHarness, Isa[P], Fail[JsonError]] {
    Region::scoped(256.kib(), |cap scratch| {
        let mut tiles = TiledSeq::with_region[TelemetryPoint](in scratch, 1024);
        let input = black_box(sample_ndjson_64k.as_bytes());
        let parsed = ingest_ndjson_to_tiles[P, scratch](in scratch, in input, inout tiles)?;
        black_box(parsed)
    })
}
// 2. Benchmarking a `Mode::Linear` consumer (`sink WriteTxn`) with untimed batch setup:
@bench(zero_alloc = true, max_l1d_misses_per_op = 4)
fn bench_linear_wal_commit_fastpath() -> PmuSampleWire eff [BenchHarness, Storage] {
    BenchHarness::iter_linear_batched(
        // Untimed setup: mints fresh `Mode::Linear` WriteTxn obligations outside RDPMC window:
        |in arena, idx| Storage::mock_staged_txn_in_region(in arena, idx as u64, 512),
        // Timed body: measures ONLY `commit` + CRC framing (`sink tx` discharged per iteration!):
        |sink tx: WriteTxn| {
            handle Fail[IoError] in {
                Ok(black_box(tx.commit()))
            } with {
                return res => res,
                raise(e) => Err(e),
            }
        },
    )
}
41.4 Lean 4 PMU Wrap-Safe Arithmetic and Paired Interleaving Cancellation Proofs (vesper verify)
Extracting Metronome's 48-bit hardware PMU counter delta reader and paired A/B difference estimator via vesper-verify-lean (Section 11) discharges both Modular 48-Bit Counter Wrap-Around Exactness (bv_decide) and Additive Common-Mode Cloud-VM Noise Cancellation (omega):
-- proofs/MetronomeBenchSafety.lean
-- 1. Exact 48-Bit Hardware PMU Counter Delta Across Wrap-Around (`x86_64 RDPMC` width = 48 bits):
-- Proves via SAT bit-blasting (`bv_decide`) that masked modular subtraction recovers the exact
-- `delta < 2^48` even when the hardware counter wraps past `0xFFFF_FFFF_FFFF` mid-sample!
theorem rdpmc_48bit_wrap_delta_exact (startRaw delta : BitVec 64)
    (h_delta_fits : delta < (1#64 <<< 48)) :
    let mask48 : BitVec 64 := (1#64 <<< 48) - 1#64
    let endRaw : BitVec 64 := (startRaw + delta) &&& mask48
    ((endRaw - (startRaw &&& mask48)) &&& mask48) = delta := by
  bv_decide
-- 2. Paired Interleaving Cancellation (`Tango` quanta `[A + drift, B + drift]`):
-- Any additive host hypervisor/thermal stall `commonDrift` affecting an interleaved `(A, B)` pair
-- cancels identically from the paired speedup difference `(measB - measA)`:
theorem paired_interleaving_cancels_common_drift (trueA trueB commonDrift : Int) :
    let measA := trueA + commonDrift
    let measB := trueB + commonDrift
    measB - measA = trueB - trueA := by
  omega
41.5 Rust Paired A/B Statistical Engine, Stabilizer Offset Generator, and Gate Auditor (crates/vesper-bench)
Adding crates/vesper-bench brings the unified Rust host workspace to 46 modular crates, implementing 48-bit wrap-safe PMU delta extraction, deterministic SplitMix64 Stabilizer cache-line entry-offset randomization, paired-sample Mann-Whitney / Hodges-Lehmann confidence-interval regression detection, and @bench budget enforcement:
// crates/vesper-bench/src/lib.rs
use smol_str::SmolStr;
pub const PMU_COUNTER_MASK_48: u64 = (1u64 << 48) - 1;
pub const CACHE_LINE_BYTES: u32 = 64;
pub const PAGE_BYTES_4K: u32 = 4096;
/// Computes exact retired events across 48-bit hardware `RDPMC` wrap-around (`rdpmc_48bit_wrap_delta_exact`).
#[inline(always)]
pub const fn pmu_delta_48bit(start_raw: u64, end_raw: u64) -> u64 {
    end_raw.wrapping_sub(start_raw & PMU_COUNTER_MASK_48) & PMU_COUNTER_MASK_48
}
/// Generates deterministic 64-byte-aligned page offsets `[0, 64, 128, ..., 4032]` for Stabilizer
/// link-order / I-cache / BTB randomization across paired benchmark epochs.
pub fn stabilizer_icache_offset_bytes(seed: u64, epoch: u64) -> u32 {
    let mut z = seed.wrapping_add(epoch.wrapping_mul(0x9E37_79B9_7F4A_7C15));
    z = (z ^ (z >> 30)).wrapping_mul(0xBF58_476D_1CE4_E5B9);
    z = (z ^ (z >> 27)).wrapping_mul(0x94D0_49BB_1331_11EB);
    let slot = ((z ^ (z >> 31)) as u32) % (PAGE_BYTES_4K / CACHE_LINE_BYTES);
    slot * CACHE_LINE_BYTES
}
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct PairedEpochSample {
    pub baseline_cycles_per_op: i64,
    pub candidate_cycles_per_op: i64,
    pub candidate_insts_per_op: u64,
    pub candidate_l1d_misses_per_op: u64,
    pub candidate_heap_alloc_bytes: u64,
    pub has_simd_vector_ops: bool,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct BenchGateSpec {
    pub bench_name: SmolStr,
    pub require_zero_alloc: bool,
    pub expect_vectorized: bool,
    pub max_insts_per_op: Option<u64>,
    pub max_l1d_misses_per_op: Option<u64>,
    /// Maximum allowed median regression in basis points (e.g. `100` = +1.00% cycles/op).
    pub max_regression_bp: i64,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum BenchGateFailure {
    UnexpectedHeapAllocation { bench: SmolStr, allocated_bytes: u64 },
    MissingSimdVectorization { bench: SmolStr },
    InstructionBudgetExceeded { bench: SmolStr, actual: u64, limit: u64 },
    L1dMissBudgetExceeded { bench: SmolStr, actual: u64, limit: u64 },
    ThroughputRegression { bench: SmolStr, median_delta_bp: i64, max_allowed_bp: i64 },
}
/// Evaluates paired interleaved `(A, B)` epochs using the non-parametric median of paired ratios
/// (robust against outlier OS interrupts) and enforces `@bench(...)` hardware contracts.
pub fn evaluate_paired_benchmark_gate(
    spec: &BenchGateSpec,
    samples: &[PairedEpochSample],
) -> Result<i64, BenchGateFailure> {
    let mut delta_bps: Vec<i64> = Vec::with_capacity(samples.len());
    for s in samples {
        if spec.require_zero_alloc && s.candidate_heap_alloc_bytes > 0 {
            return Err(BenchGateFailure::UnexpectedHeapAllocation {
                bench: spec.bench_name.clone(),
                allocated_bytes: s.candidate_heap_alloc_bytes,
            });
        }
        if spec.expect_vectorized && !s.has_simd_vector_ops {
            return Err(BenchGateFailure::MissingSimdVectorization {
                bench: spec.bench_name.clone(),
            });
        }
        if let Some(limit) = spec.max_insts_per_op {
            if s.candidate_insts_per_op > limit {
                return Err(BenchGateFailure::InstructionBudgetExceeded {
                    bench: spec.bench_name.clone(),
                    actual: s.candidate_insts_per_op,
                    limit,
                });
            }
        }
        if let Some(limit) = spec.max_l1d_misses_per_op {
            if s.candidate_l1d_misses_per_op > limit {
                return Err(BenchGateFailure::L1dMissBudgetExceeded {
                    bench: spec.bench_name.clone(),
                    actual: s.candidate_l1d_misses_per_op,
                    limit,
                });
            }
        }
        let base = s.baseline_cycles_per_op.max(1);
        let diff = s.candidate_cycles_per_op - base;
        delta_bps.push((diff.saturating_mul(10_000)) / base);
    }
    if delta_bps.is_empty() {
        return Ok(0);
    }
    delta_bps.sort_unstable();
    let median_bp = delta_bps[delta_bps.len() / 2];
    if median_bp > spec.max_regression_bp {
        return Err(BenchGateFailure::ThroughputRegression {
            bench: spec.bench_name.clone(),
            median_delta_bp: median_bp,
            max_allowed_bp: spec.max_regression_bp,
        });
    }
    Ok(median_bp)
}
Appendix A. Mechanized Lean 4 Metatheory of lambda_Vesper and Place Capability Graphs (VesperMetatheory.lean)
This self-contained, zero-sorryAx Lean 4 mechanization formalizes Vesper's core substructural calculus (lambda_Vesper, Sections 4–6), SemVer projection narrowing ([SV-Fn], Section 9), and the Stage 3 Place Capability Graph (PCG) checker (vesper-mir-pcg, Section 10.3 and Section 21.2). Because it uses only pure core Lean 4 with zero external dependencies, it compiles out-of-the-box via lean VesperMetatheory.lean and passes Section 11.4's verify_and_audit_olean_bundle zero-sorryAx kernel gate.
A.1 Mechanized Correspondence Between Specification Rules and Lean 4 Theorems
1. Substructural Mode Lattice (Sections 4 and 10.2): Mode, Mode.join, Mode.meet, and Mode.allowsCount mechanize copy <= affine <= linear, proving mode_submode_allows_count and linear_not_discardable.
2. OxCaml Field-Modality Well-Formedness ([K-Adt], Section 4): FieldModalityWf and adtKind prove adt_kind_never_relaxes_linear_component, establishing that @@ copy overrides can never hide or discard a nested Mode.linear obligation.
3. [T-Let] Resumption Frame Soundness (Sections 5, 6, and 12.2): EffectRow.res and FrameSafe prove abort_soundness_preserves_linear (abortable Fail/Cancel effects require all live frame bindings to be discardable), uncancellable_io_allows_live_linear (Storage/Net/Async preserve live linear handles across yield points), and unconsumed_txn_across_question_rejected.
4. [T-CallProj] Alias-XOR-Mutability and [SV-Fn] Narrowing (Sections 5, 9.1, and 10.3): ProjSelector, CallProjValid, and call_proj_axm_exclusivity prove that any inout or sink argument has zero leaf-path overlap with any sibling argument, while semver_narrowing_preserves_disjoint proves that narrowing inout projections preserves all caller disjointness proofs.
5. PCG Block Join and Theorem 2 Total Linear Exhaustion (Sections 6, 10.3, and 21.2): pcgJoinLocal proves pcg_join_preserves_linear_agreement across CFG joins, while EvalDet and vesper_total_linear_exhaustion prove that every closed Vesper program consumes 100 percent of dynamically minted linear obligations across both normal returns and Fail aborts.
A.2 Complete Zero-sorryAx Lean 4 Proof Artifact (VesperMetatheory.lean)
-- VesperMetatheory.lean
-- Zero-sorryAx mechanization of Sections 4–6, 9, 10, and 21 (vesper-hir + vesper-mir-pcg).
namespace Vesper.Metatheory
inductive Mode : Type where
  | copy   : Mode
  | affine : Mode
  | linear : Mode
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr
namespace Mode
@[simp] def rank : Mode -> Nat
  | .copy   => 0
  | .affine => 1
  | .linear => 2
@[simp] def Le (m1 m2 : Mode) : Prop := m1.rank <= m2.rank
instance (m1 m2 : Mode) : Decidable (Le m1 m2) :=
  inferInstanceAs (Decidable (m1.rank <= m2.rank))
@[simp] def join (m1 m2 : Mode) : Mode :=
  match m1, m2 with
  | .linear, _ | _, .linear => .linear
  | .affine, _ | _, .affine => .affine
  | .copy, .copy            => .copy
@[simp] def meet (m1 m2 : Mode) : Mode :=
  match m1, m2 with
  | .copy, _ | _, .copy     => .copy
  | .affine, _ | _, .affine => .affine
  | .linear, .linear        => .linear
@[simp] def isDiscardable (m : Mode) : Bool :=
  match m with
  | .copy | .affine => true
  | .linear         => false
@[simp] def allowsCount (m : Mode) (n : Nat) : Prop :=
  match m with
  | .copy   => True
  | .affine => n <= 1
  | .linear => n = 1
theorem le_refl (m : Mode) : Le m m := Nat.le_refl _
theorem le_trans {a b c : Mode} (hab : Le a b) (hbc : Le b c) : Le a c :=
  Nat.le_trans hab hbc
theorem le_antisymm {a b : Mode} (hab : Le a b) (hba : Le b a) : a = b := by
  cases a <;> cases b <;> simp [Le] at hab hba <;> rfl
theorem le_join_left (a b : Mode) : Le a (join a b) := by
  cases a <;> cases b <;> decide
theorem meet_le_left (a b : Mode) : Le (meet a b) a := by
  cases a <;> cases b <;> decide
theorem meet_le_right (a b : Mode) : Le (meet a b) b := by
  cases a <;> cases b <;> decide
theorem mode_submode_allows_count {m1 m2 : Mode} {n : Nat}
    (h_sub : Le m1 m2) (h_use : m2.allowsCount n) :
    m1.allowsCount n := by
  cases m1 <;> cases m2 <;> simp [Le, allowsCount] at h_sub h_use |- <;> omega
theorem linear_not_discardable : Mode.linear.isDiscardable = false := rfl
theorem discardable_iff_le_affine (m : Mode) :
    m.isDiscardable = true <-> Le m .affine := by
  cases m <;> decide
end Mode
structure AdtFieldSpec where
  compKind     : Mode
  overrideMode : Mode := .linear
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr
@[simp] def FieldModalityWf (f : AdtFieldSpec) : Prop :=
  Mode.Le f.compKind f.overrideMode
@[simp] def adtKind (mDecl : Mode) (fields : List AdtFieldSpec) : Mode :=
  fields.foldl (fun acc f => Mode.join acc (Mode.meet f.compKind f.overrideMode)) mDecl
theorem wf_field_meet_eq_comp (f : AdtFieldSpec) (hwf : FieldModalityWf f) :
    Mode.meet f.compKind f.overrideMode = f.compKind := by
  rcases f with ⟨k, m⟩
  cases k <;> cases m <;> simp [FieldModalityWf, Mode.Le] at hwf <;> rfl
theorem foldl_join_ge_init (init : Mode) (fs : List AdtFieldSpec) :
    Mode.Le init (fs.foldl (fun acc f => Mode.join acc (Mode.meet f.compKind f.overrideMode)) init) := by
  induction fs generalizing init with
  | nil => exact Mode.le_refl init
  | cons hd tl ih =>
    have h1 := Mode.le_join_left init (Mode.meet hd.compKind hd.overrideMode)
    exact Mode.le_trans h1 (ih _)
theorem adt_kind_never_relaxes_linear_component
    (mDecl : Mode) (fields : List AdtFieldSpec)
    (h_wf : forall f, f ∈ fields -> FieldModalityWf f)
    (fLin : AdtFieldSpec) (h_mem : fLin ∈ fields) (h_lin : fLin.compKind = .linear) :
    adtKind mDecl fields = .linear := by
  unfold adtKind
  induction fields generalizing mDecl with
  | nil => cases h_mem
  | cons hd tl ih =>
    simp only [List.mem_cons] at h_mem
    rcases h_mem with rfl | h_in_tl
    · have h_hd_wf : FieldModalityWf fLin := h_wf fLin (List.mem_cons_self _ _)
      have h_meet : Mode.meet fLin.compKind fLin.overrideMode = .linear := by
        rw [wf_field_meet_eq_comp fLin h_hd_wf, h_lin]
      simp only [List.foldl_cons, h_meet]
      have h_join : Mode.join mDecl .linear = .linear := by cases mDecl <;> rfl
      rw [h_join]
      have h_ge := foldl_join_ge_init .linear tl
      exact Mode.le_antisymm
        (by cases tl.foldl (fun acc f => Mode.join acc (Mode.meet f.compKind f.overrideMode)) .linear <;> decide)
        h_ge
    · apply ih (Mode.join mDecl (Mode.meet hd.compKind hd.overrideMode))
      · intro f hf
        exact h_wf f (List.mem_cons_of_mem hd hf)
      · exact h_in_tl
inductive ResumptionMode : Type where
  | affine : ResumptionMode
  | linear : ResumptionMode
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr
@[simp] def ResumptionMode.toMode : ResumptionMode -> Mode
  | .affine => .affine
  | .linear => .linear
abbrev EffectRow := List ResumptionMode
@[simp] def EffectRow.res : EffectRow -> Mode
  | []      => .linear
  | q :: qs => Mode.meet q.toMode (EffectRow.res qs)
@[simp] def FrameSafe (liveModes : List Mode) (eff1 : EffectRow) : Prop :=
  forall m, m ∈ liveModes -> Mode.Le m eff1.res
theorem res_le_affine_of_mem_affine {eff : EffectRow} (h : .affine ∈ eff) :
    Mode.Le eff.res .affine := by
  induction eff with
  | nil => cases h
  | cons hd tl ih =>
    simp only [List.mem_cons] at h
    rcases h with rfl | h_tl
    · exact Mode.meet_le_left .affine tl.res
    · exact Mode.le_trans (Mode.meet_le_right hd.toMode tl.res) (ih h_tl)
theorem abort_soundness_preserves_linear
    {liveModes : List Mode} {eff1 : EffectRow}
    (h_frame : FrameSafe liveModes eff1)
    (h_abortable : .affine ∈ eff1) :
    forall m, m ∈ liveModes -> m.isDiscardable = true := by
  intro m hm
  rw [Mode.discardable_iff_le_affine]
  exact Mode.le_trans (h_frame m hm) (res_le_affine_of_mem_affine h_abortable)
theorem uncancellable_io_allows_live_linear
    (liveModes : List Mode) (eff1 : EffectRow)
    (h_all_linear : forall q, q ∈ eff1 -> q = .linear) :
    FrameSafe liveModes eff1 := by
  have h_res : eff1.res = .linear := by
    induction eff1 with
    | nil => rfl
    | cons hd tl ih =>
      have h_hd : hd = .linear := h_all_linear hd (List.mem_cons_self _ _)
      subst h_hd
      have h_tl : tl.res = .linear := ih (fun q hq => h_all_linear q (List.mem_cons_of_mem _ hq))
      simp [EffectRow.res, h_tl]
  intro m _
  rw [h_res]
  cases m <;> decide
theorem unconsumed_txn_across_question_rejected :
    ¬ FrameSafe [.linear] [.affine] := by
  intro h
  have h_lin := h .linear (List.mem_singleton.mpr rfl)
  simp [EffectRow.res, Mode.Le] at h_lin
abbrev LocalId  := Nat
abbrev FieldIdx := Nat
abbrev LeafPath := List FieldIdx
@[simp] def PathPrefix : LeafPath -> LeafPath -> Prop
  | [], _             => True
  | _ :: _, []        => False
  | f1 :: t1, f2 :: t2 => f1 = f2 /\ PathPrefix t1 t2
structure ProjSelector where
  localVar : LocalId
  branches : List LeafPath
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr
@[simp] def ProjSelector.covers (sel : ProjSelector) (loc : LocalId) (leaf : LeafPath) : Prop :=
  sel.localVar = loc /\ exists b, b ∈ sel.branches /\ PathPrefix b leaf
@[simp] def ProjSelector.Disjoint (s1 s2 : ProjSelector) : Prop :=
  forall loc leaf, ¬ (s1.covers loc leaf /\ s2.covers loc leaf)
@[simp] def ProjSelector.Subset (sNew sOld : ProjSelector) : Prop :=
  forall loc leaf, sNew.covers loc leaf -> sOld.covers loc leaf
theorem semver_narrowing_preserves_disjoint
    {sNew sOld sOther : ProjSelector}
    (h_narrow : sNew.Subset sOld)
    (h_disj   : sOld.Disjoint sOther) :
    sNew.Disjoint sOther := by
  intro loc leaf ⟨h_new, h_other⟩
  exact h_disj loc leaf ⟨h_narrow loc leaf h_new, h_other⟩
inductive PassMode : Type where
  | sharedIn : PassMode
  | inOut    : PassMode
  | sinkOwn  : PassMode
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr
structure CallArg where
  mode : PassMode
  proj : ProjSelector
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr
def CallProjValid (args : List CallArg) : Prop :=
  forall (i j : Fin args.length), i != j ->
    ((args.get i).mode = .sharedIn /\ (args.get j).mode = .sharedIn) \/
    ProjSelector.Disjoint (args.get i).proj (args.get j).proj
theorem call_proj_axm_exclusivity
    {args : List CallArg} (h_valid : CallProjValid args)
    (i j : Fin args.length) (h_neq : i != j)
    (h_mut_or_sink : (args.get i).mode != .sharedIn)
    {loc : LocalId} {leaf : LeafPath}
    (h_cov_i : (args.get i).proj.covers loc leaf) :
    ¬ (args.get j).proj.covers loc leaf := by
  intro h_cov_j
  rcases h_valid i j h_neq with ⟨h_in_i, _⟩ | h_disj
  · exact absurd h_in_i (bne_iff_ne.mp h_mut_or_sink)
  · exact h_disj loc leaf ⟨h_cov_i, h_cov_j⟩
inductive CapState : Type where
  | liveOwned        (m : Mode)
  | consumed
  | poisonedRecovery
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr
def pcgJoinLocal (lhs rhs : CapState) : Option CapState :=
  match lhs, rhs with
  | .poisonedRecovery, _ | _, .poisonedRecovery => some .poisonedRecovery
  | .consumed, .consumed                        => some .consumed
  | .liveOwned m1, .liveOwned m2                =>
    if m1 = m2 then some (.liveOwned m1) else none
  | .liveOwned m, .consumed | .consumed, .liveOwned m =>
    if m.isDiscardable then some .consumed else none
@[simp] def pcgReturnOk : CapState -> Bool
  | .liveOwned .linear => false
  | .liveOwned .copy | .liveOwned .affine | .consumed | .poisonedRecovery => true
theorem pcg_join_preserves_linear_agreement
    {lhs rhs merged : CapState}
    (h_no_poison_l : lhs != .poisonedRecovery)
    (h_no_poison_r : rhs != .poisonedRecovery)
    (h_join : pcgJoinLocal lhs rhs = some merged) :
    (lhs = .liveOwned .linear <-> rhs = .liveOwned .linear) := by
  cases lhs <;> cases rhs <;> simp [pcgJoinLocal, Mode.isDiscardable] at h_no_poison_l h_no_poison_r h_join |-
  case liveOwned.liveOwned m1 m2 =>
    rcases h_join with ⟨rfl, _⟩
    rfl
abbrev ResId := Nat
inductive Val : Type where
  | unit      : Val
  | nat       : Nat -> Val
  | linHandle : ResId -> Val
  | pair      : Val -> Val → Val
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr
@[simp] def Val.linResources : Val -> List ResId
  | .unit        => []
  | .nat _       => []
  | .linHandle r => [r]
  | .pair v1 v2  => v1.linResources ++ v2.linResources
def ValTyped (v : Val) : Mode -> Prop
  | .copy   => v.linResources = []
  | .affine => v.linResources = []
  | .linear => True
inductive ModeSplit : Option Mode -> Option Mode -> Option Mode -> Prop where
  | none      : ModeSplit none none none
  | copyBoth  : ModeSplit (some .copy) (some .copy) (some .copy)
  | leftOnly  (m : Mode) : ModeSplit (some m) (some m) none
  | rightOnly (m : Mode) : ModeSplit (some m) none (some m)
@[simp] def OptEnvDiscardable : Option Mode -> Prop
  | none   => True
  | some m => Mode.Le m .affine
inductive Expr : Type where
  | var        : Expr
  | litNat     : Nat -> Expr
  | mintLinear : Expr
  | consumeLin : Expr -> Expr
  | raiseFail  : Expr
  | letSeq     : Expr -> Expr -> Expr
inductive HasType : Option Mode -> Expr -> Mode -> EffectRow -> Prop where
  | tVar {m : Mode} :
      HasType (some m) .var m []
  | tLit {gamma : Option Mode} (n : Nat) (h_disc : OptEnvDiscardable gamma) :
      HasType gamma (.litNat n) .copy []
  | tMint {gamma : Option Mode} (h_disc : OptEnvDiscardable gamma) :
      HasType gamma .mintLinear .linear [.linear]
  | tConsume {gamma : Option Mode} {e : Expr} {eff : EffectRow}
      (h_e : HasType gamma e .linear eff) :
      HasType gamma (.consumeLin e) .copy (eff ++ [.linear])
  | tFail {gamma : Option Mode} (h_disc : OptEnvDiscardable gamma) :
      HasType gamma .raiseFail .copy [.affine]
  | tLet {gamma gamma1 gamma2 : Option Mode} {e1 e2 : Expr} {m1 m2 : Mode} {eff1 eff2 : EffectRow}
      (h_split  : ModeSplit gamma gamma1 gamma2)
      (h_e1     : HasType gamma1 e1 m1 eff1)
      (h_frame  : forall m_z, gamma2 = some m_z -> Mode.Le m_z eff1.res)
      (h_z_none : gamma2 = none)
      (h_e2     : HasType (some m1) e2 m2 eff2) :
      HasType gamma (.letSeq e1 e2) m2 (eff1 ++ eff2)
inductive Outcome : Type where
  | val     : Val -> Outcome
  | aborted : Outcome
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr
inductive EvalDet : Option Val -> Expr -> Nat -> List ResId -> List ResId -> Outcome -> Nat -> Prop where
  | evVar (v : Val) (n : Nat) :
      EvalDet (some v) .var n [] [] (.val v) n
  | evLit (env : Option Val) (k n : Nat) :
      EvalDet env (.litNat k) n [] [] (.val (.nat k)) n
  | evMint (env : Option Val) (n : Nat) :
      EvalDet env .mintLinear n [n] [] (.val (.linHandle n)) (n + 1)
  | evConsumeVal {env : Option Val} {e : Expr} {n n' : Nat} {m c : List ResId} {r : ResId}
      (h : EvalDet env e n m c (.val (.linHandle r)) n') :
      EvalDet env (.consumeLin e) n m (c ++ [r]) (.val .unit) n'
  | evConsumeAbort {env : Option Val} {e : Expr} {n n' : Nat} {m c : List ResId}
      (h : EvalDet env e n m c .aborted n') :
      EvalDet env (.consumeLin e) n m c .aborted n'
  | evFail (env : Option Val) (n : Nat) :
      EvalDet env .raiseFail n [] [] .aborted n
  | evLetVal {env : Option Val} {e1 e2 : Expr} {n n1 n2 : Nat}
      {m1 c1 m2 c2 : List ResId} {v1 : Val} {out2 : Outcome}
      (h1 : EvalDet env e1 n m1 c1 (.val v1) n1)
      (h2 : EvalDet (some v1) e2 n1 m2 c2 out2 n2) :
      EvalDet env (.letSeq e1 e2) n (m1 ++ m2) (c1 ++ c2) out2 n2
  | evLetAbort {env : Option Val} {e1 e2 : Expr} {n n1 : Nat} {m1 c1 : List ResId}
      (h1 : EvalDet env e1 n m1 c1 .aborted n1) :
      EvalDet env (.letSeq e1 e2) n m1 c1 .aborted n1
def EnvTyped : Option Val -> Option Mode -> Prop
  | none, none     => True
  | some v, some m => ValTyped v m
  | _, _           => False
@[simp] def EnvResources : Option Val -> List ResId
  | none   => []
  | some v => v.linResources
@[simp] def OutcomeResources : Outcome -> List ResId
  | .val v   => v.linResources
  | .aborted => []
theorem discardable_env_has_no_linear_resources
    {env : Option Val} {gamma : Option Mode}
    (h_ty : EnvTyped env gamma) (h_disc : OptEnvDiscardable gamma) :
    EnvResources env = [] := by
  cases env <;> cases gamma <;> simp [EnvTyped, OptEnvDiscardable, Mode.Le] at h_ty h_disc |-
  rename_i v m
  cases m <;> simp [Mode.rank] at h_disc <;> exact h_ty
theorem vesper_linear_conservation_soundness
    {env : Option Val} {gamma : Option Mode} {e : Expr} {m : Mode} {eff : EffectRow}
    {n n' : Nat} {minted consumed : List ResId} {out : Outcome}
    (h_type : HasType gamma e m eff)
    (h_env  : EnvTyped env gamma)
    (h_eval : EvalDet env e n minted consumed out n') :
    (forall v, out = .val v -> ValTyped v m) /\
    (EnvResources env).length + minted.length =
      consumed.length + (OutcomeResources out).length := by
  induction h_type generalizing env n n' minted consumed out with
  | tVar =>
    cases h_eval with
    | evVar v _ =>
      refine ⟨?_, by simp⟩
      intro v' hv
      injection hv with rfl
      exact h_env
  | tLit k h_disc =>
    cases h_eval with
    | evLit _ _ _ =>
      have h_empty := discardable_env_has_no_linear_resources h_env h_disc
      simp [ValTyped, h_empty]
  | tMint h_disc =>
    cases h_eval with
    | evMint _ _ =>
      have h_empty := discardable_env_has_no_linear_resources h_env h_disc
      simp [ValTyped, h_empty]
  | tConsume _ ih =>
    cases h_eval with
    | evConsumeVal h_sub =>
      obtain ⟨_, h_len⟩ := ih h_env h_sub
      simp [ValTyped] at h_len |-
      omega
    | evConsumeAbort h_sub =>
      obtain ⟨_, h_len⟩ := ih h_env h_sub
      exact ⟨fun _ h => by cases h, h_len⟩
  | tFail h_disc =>
    cases h_eval with
    | evFail _ _ =>
      have h_empty := discardable_env_has_no_linear_resources h_env h_disc
      simp [h_empty]
  | tLet h_split _ _ h_z_none _ ih1 ih2 =>
    subst h_z_none
    have h_eq_gamma : _ = _ := by cases h_split <;> rfl
    subst h_eq_gamma
    cases h_eval with
    | evLetVal h_ev1 h_ev2 =>
      obtain ⟨h_v1_ty, h_len1⟩ := ih1 h_env h_ev1
      have h_env2 : EnvTyped (some _) (some _) := h_v1_ty _ rfl
      obtain ⟨h_out2_ty, h_len2⟩ := ih2 h_env2 h_ev2
      refine ⟨h_out2_ty, ?_⟩
      simp [EnvResources, OutcomeResources] at h_len1 h_len2 |-
      omega
    | evLetAbort h_ev1 =>
      obtain ⟨_, h_len1⟩ := ih1 h_env h_ev1
      exact ⟨fun _ h => by cases h, h_len1⟩
theorem vesper_total_linear_exhaustion
    {e : Expr} {eff : EffectRow} {n n' : Nat}
    {minted consumed : List ResId} {out : Outcome}
    (h_closed_ty : HasType none e .copy eff)
    (h_eval      : EvalDet none e n minted consumed out n') :
    minted.length = consumed.length := by
  obtain ⟨h_val_ty, h_cons⟩ :=
    vesper_linear_conservation_soundness h_closed_ty trivial h_eval
  cases out with
  | aborted =>
    simp [EnvResources, OutcomeResources] at h_cons
    exact h_cons
  | val v =>
    have h_no_res : v.linResources = [] := h_val_ty v rfl
    simp [EnvResources, OutcomeResources, h_no_res] at h_cons
    exact h_cons
end Vesper.Metatheory
